# CodeGG Active Planning Registry

This file is the compact control surface for active interim planning. Detailed requirements and completed history remain in source roadmaps, implementation plans, `plans/closure/`, and Git history.

Canonical direction remains in:

- `plans/000-long-term-specification.md`
- `plans/001-terminology-and-domain-model.md`
- `plans/002-long-term-roadmap.md`
- `plans/003-planning-process.md`

## Status vocabulary

- **proposed** — roadmap or plan exists but is not approved for execution.
- **ready** — dependencies and interfaces are satisfied; plan may be handed off.
- **active** — implementation or closure work is in progress.
- **blocked** — a named dependency or evidence requirement prevents progress.
- **closing** — implementation landed and closure evidence is being gathered.
- **closed** — closure record accepted.
- **conditionally closed** — substantial work landed, but a named correctness or operational evidence condition remains.
- **superseded** — replaced by another document.
- **archived** — no longer active and retained for traceability.

| TUI subagent and job visibility | **active; M001–M005 landed but never closed; superseded for corrective work by the convergence addendum** | `plans/subsystems/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-roadmap.md` | M005 landed: the missing `Shift+T` help-overlay entry and a corrected sidebar `Enter` entry; `docs/tui.md` gained a subagents/jobs section covering all four new keys; the `architecture/tui.md` module map and `overview.md` Verified-counts rows were corrected against their listed Sources (`app/state/` was missing four files and three other counts were stale). P1's "verify, don't assume" found the Workspace path was never actually tested end to end — the test named `workspace_command_and_hotkey_open_same_dashboard` called the handler twice directly — so convergence is now driven through the command registry and `App::on_key`. Doing that exposed a real, older bug: `is_normal_only` gates on the *action*, but the Insert branch's text-key guard was gated on `bindings.is_none()`, which never holds because `App::on_key` always passes `Some(&ui_state.bindings)`. Typing a capital `F` opened the project picker and swallowed the character **in the default configuration**; `Shift+W` and vim-only `Shift+G` did the same. Fixed at the key rather than in the list, because `OpenWorkspaceDashboard`/`OpenProjectPicker` also own `Ctrl+O`/`Ctrl+\`, which must stay live in Insert. `builtin_shift_letters_are_composed_text_in_insert_mode` sweeps both keymaps and asserts it is not vacuous. M001 landed: curated pool, active-scoped `SubagentNameRegistry` (RAII lease releases on completion/failure/cancel/panic), `display_name` + real `run_id` carried **in the event**, projection fields `#[serde(default)]`, and the dead `run_id` join deleted (it compared a `u64` decimal against a UUID string, so the field was permanently `None`). M002 landed: unified `Shift+T` overlay (todos, then in-flight subagents, then jobs) with a global row budget, fair-share allocation so no group starves, and a reservation clamped against the pane height left after the transcript's `Min(10)` floor — an overlay that overshoots its carve is skipped entirely and the key reads as dead. M003 landed: subagent tab set in the main buffer keyed by the projection's `task_id` (not a fresh UUID), with `active == None` **as** the main conversation (the close-fallback analogue of `ProjectTabs::close_fallback_tab`), a `MAX_OPEN_SUBAGENT_TABS = 16` capacity that refuses rather than evicts, an explicit attached/detached state for D-b that makes `is_running` return false once the per-turn tree drops the subject, and a one-row tab bar costing exactly one transcript row. `Shift+N`/`Shift+P`/`Shift+X` are bound under both encodings and Normal-only. The overlay gained a `j`/`k`/`↵` keyboard path into a tab, which required adding `task_id` to `TaskRow::Subagent` — a display label is not an identity. M004 landed: two collapsible sessions-panel sections between Todos and MCP Servers, with `SidebarActivation::InspectSubagent(task_id)` mapping to `PanelKey::Intent(TuiMsg::OpenSubagentTab)` on the panel path and `process_msg` on the click path. The panel's subagent rows are derived from the *same* `SubagentRow` vector the main buffer strip and the `Shift+T` overlay read, so all three surfaces show one name per subagent; jobs reuse `state::tasks::is_live_state` so "waiting on" is one rule. Job focus targets key on `job_id`, not list index — jobs are upserted then filtered, so an index-keyed target would move onto a different job when an earlier one finishes. The subagent section lists every tree row with a status glyph rather than only live ones, so a finished subagent does not vanish while its tab may still be open (D3 watch-item resolved in favour of agreement with M003's detached tab state). The existing agent-tree rows keep resolving to `InspectRun`. Two sections were added to `line_targets`/`content_lines`, which nothing previously checked for agreement; a rendered line with no hover target shifts every row below it onto the wrong subject, so `hover_targets_stay_aligned_with_rendered_lines` now asserts the invariant. M005 closed the roadmap: help overlay, `docs/tui.md`, and the `architecture/tui.md` module map all landed, and P1's verification found a pre-existing Insert-mode hijack (`Shift+F`, `Shift+W`, vim `Shift+G`) that is now fixed and swept by test. The roadmap's own completion definition is met. | Names ephemeral and active-scoped (D1) — no storage migration, no `STORAGE_LAYOUT_VERSION` bump; durability revisit requires an ADR. D5 resolved narrowly (carry `run_id` in the event) so no projection-identity ADR was needed. Jobs need no protocol/daemon work (`snapshot.jobs` already populated, zero TUI readers). Requested `Ctrl+J` is undeliverable in legacy xterm encoding (0x0A = LF = Enter) and was consolidated into the single `Shift+T` tasks overlay; `Ctrl+S` remains `StashPrompt`; `Shift+X` is close-tab. Subagent-tab ids are projection-scoped and must never be persisted. The sessions-panel Enter/click path shipped in M004: `Enter` on the existing agent-tree rows still opens the run-detail dialog, and the new `Subagents` section rows open a tab instead — two questions ("what happened to this run" / "show me its transcript") on two sets of rows. A job row activates to nothing: there is no job transcript to open. No send-to-subagent capability may be implied by M001–M005 (quarantined in M900, not approved for planning); M900 stays blocked on run addressing, permission scoping, and what a "reply" to a delegated run means. Nothing is derived from a `Shift+<letter>` key binding without also checking the Insert branch: an action-keyed `is_normal_only` cannot make one spelling Normal-only while a `Ctrl` chord of the same action stays live. |
| TUI subagent and job visibility convergence (corrective) | **active; C001–C007 all closed** | `plans/subsystems/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence-corrective-addendum.md` | Seven milestones. Opened because M001–M005 landed in `c0d6ff2d3` and are verified present in production code, yet a user following `/sessions` then `Ctrl+T` sees none of it. Four measured causes: **(1)** the combined block lives on `Shift+T`, which is `is_normal_only` while `InputMode` defaults to `Insert`, so the first press composes a literal `T`; **(2)** `SidePanelId::Session` (title `SESSIONS`, config key `sessions`) holds the subagent sections, while `/sessions` opens `Dialog::Session` (key `session_switcher`) with **zero** `subagent` matches across `src/tui/commands/sessions.rs`; **(3)** bare `/agents` calls `open_panel_picker(self, "agents")` (`src/tui/app/mod.rs:5572`) — it asks where agents should go instead of showing them; **(4)** the middle of the naming chain (`sync_subagents_from_snapshot`, event→adapter→snapshot→row) has **zero** test coverage, so a name could stop appearing with a green suite. | C001–C007 **all closed** (closures `plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/001-status.md` through `007-status.md`; note the sequence numbering — `003-status.md` closes C005, `006-status.md` closes C003, `007-status.md` closes C006); **C007 is closed** — plan `plans/implementation/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/005-send-to-subagent.md`, closure `plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/005-status.md`. Consolidating to `Ctrl+T` **resolves** cause (1) rather than documenting it: `ToggleTasksList` is `is_normal_only` only because `Shift+T` is a capital letter, so retiring that spelling lets the action leave the gate entirely and work from Insert mode. `Ctrl+S`/`Ctrl+J` are **withdrawn** (D-C1/D-C2) — `Ctrl+J` remains independently undeliverable, byte `0x0A` being reported as `Enter` (parent D4, not to be re-opened); `Shift+W` stays `/workspace`. D-C4 **reversed**: `/sessions` gains real Subagents + Jobs sections rather than a pointer, fed from the same derived `SubagentRow` vector so no fourth projection is created. Also recorded: `names.toml` descriptors are curated and compiled but no producer puts them on the event; the reducer drops `SubagentStarted` with no active turn; and M001–M005 were marked "landed" with `—` in both the implementation-plan and closure-record columns, so no closure gate ever ran. **Progress:** C001 is **closed** (`plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/001-status.md`) — `Ctrl+T` now consolidates todos/subagents/jobs and works from Insert mode, `Shift+T` is retired, D-C7 retired `todo_expanded`, and the I6 guard found **nine** help rows advertising the wrong action (the old guard passed them because it only checked that a key resolved to *something*). A further decision **D-C9** was recorded: opening the overlay from Insert takes Normal mode with it and hands it back on close, because the overlay's `j`/`k`/`↵`/`Esc` are Normal-mode gestures and shipping only the rebind would have opened a dead surface. **C003 is closed** (`plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/006-status.md`) — bare `/agents` opens `Dialog::Agent`, and the panel picker it used to open was **deleted outright** (`open_panel_picker` has zero occurrences in `src/`), which is the correct outcome: the addendum forbade routing around the picker by relabelling it. The stop condition was never hit, because `Dialog::Agent` already existed as `/agent`'s surface. Closing it found two things: the test file's module doc still described the retired picker in detail while the tests below it drove `/panels`, and `agents_and_agent_open_the_same_surface` was **vacuous** — both spellings resolve through the completion popup to the same highlighted row, so the test passed unchanged when bare `/agents` was repointed at the wrong dialog. That test was **deleted rather than rewritten**: a replacement was attempted and withdrawn. The assertion that matters (`/agent <name>` changes the selected agent, reachable only via the leading-token fallback) cannot be driven from an integration test — tracing shows the composer receives `/agent <name>` correctly, but the popup's rows still win at `Enter`, their label resolves to no command, and the resolver falls through to “send it as a prompt”. Whether the popup should match a query carrying its own argument is a **product question for the command-palette routing milestone**, not a missing assertion. **C006 is closed as answered-and-escalated** (`plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/007-status.md`), and the answer is **yes**: `AppEvent::SubagentStarted` is published unconditionally at the top of `run_subagent_task_with_cancel` (`src/agent/worker.rs`), *before* the `tokio::select!` that observes the cancel tokens, so a turn cancelled while its subagent request is still queued — or a `MAX_CHILD_TIMEOUT` expiry in `specialized_runtime.rs` — emits the event against a terminal turn and the reducer drops the node. The reducer test's stated rationale ("not a turn a subagent can start under") was **false** and is corrected; its assertions are unchanged. Retaining such a node needs a home outside `active_turn.agent_tree`, which is the addendum's stop condition, escalated to parent **D5** and an ADR. **That residual is not fixed.** **C004 is closed** (`plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/004-status.md`) — and closing it changed the tree: the chain was covered link by link but **not at two of its eight links**, so closure added the two missing named tests, for the `AppEvent → CoreEvent` mapping (the hop the **default** daemon mode depends on — deleting `display_name` from that one match arm would have removed every name with the suite green) and for the serde wire round-trip (a snapshot genuinely crosses serde to the frontend, and only the *absent* direction was covered). Both were proven non-vacuous by injecting the defect each exists to catch. **No defect was found in the additive-field chain**, so C005's conditional re-review is **discharged**, not pending. C004 has **no implementation plan** — the D-8 governance gap repeating inside the corrective workstream — and none was written retrospectively; its closure was argued against the addendum's boundary directly. **C002 is also closed** (`plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/002-status.md`) — `/sessions` (`Dialog::Session`) now lists the session's live subagents and jobs beneath its session list, navigable with the same `j`/`k` and skipping the section headings, with `Enter` on a subagent opening the same tab the sidebar and overlay open for that `task_id`. The sections are fed by the **existing** `App::task_rows()` rather than a second derivation, so the liveness rule stays in one place and the plan's stop condition was avoided rather than hit. `job_id` was added to `TaskRow::Job` — it had been dropped when `JobLike` was built from the projection, so job rows previously had no identity to key on at all. `selected_session()` and `toggle_selection()` were made type-safe against the new rows: positionally, a subagent row resolved to the session above it, and bulk mode ticked a checkbox against an entity bulk-archive cannot act on. Focus is carried by **identity** across a snapshot refresh, so a subagent that finishes mid-scroll does not slide the cursor onto a different entity. The dual-instance rule is honoured on open *and* on every live refresh, and the regression test reads the painted focus-manager clone rather than the App-owned field. C005 is **closed** (`plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/003-status.md`); see also its row in *Recently closed*. **C007 is closed** (`plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/005-status.md`) — ADR-0041 Option A as planned: `/subagent-send` arms the composer and one addressed reply issues one `TurnSteer` for the **controlling parent turn**, never the subagent's `task_id` (that dead join is pinned by a test). `InputTarget` still has only `Build`/`Chat` — the addressee is composer data, not a focus mode — and **no key** is bound, so it cannot regress the I3/I6 guards. An armed composer **cannot fall through to `TurnSubmit`**: proven by deleting the branch and watching the test fail naming `submit:…`. Three findings the plan did not have: the frontend **must** supply `turn_id` (M006's send-now deliberately sends none), so it is read at **send** time and `turn_id_mismatch` is user-visible; the parent model has **no name pool**, so the payload carries name *and* agent *and* task description or `Sally` is unresolvable; and a steer is **never stored**, so `send_now_outcome_message` could not be reused verbatim — its accepted sentence claims a queue write a bare steer never makes. It now takes a `SteerConsequence` so one vocabulary serves both senders. M006's own sentences changed as a result and its three tests were updated, not weakened. |

Baseline `b77c8156`: `FilesDialog` paints its modal `HINT` footer inside the docked panel column **and** over the host's ` ● Files ` title (one component, two chrome contracts); `HostPanel::composer_hints` advertises a hardcoded `j/k scroll · i act` line whose `i` verb `FilesDialog::handle_key` does not implement and whose `Shift+←` is placement-dependent; `SidePanelHost::composer_hints` returns `None` unless focused, so a populated docked panel advertises nothing, and `PanelHints.focused` is hardcoded `true`; and because `walk_directory` restarts depth at 0 for a subtree walk, every row of an `l`-scoped listing renders flush-left. M001 must not add a key, change the `Enter`/`l` split, touch the walk/protocol/storage, or let the always-on hint row cost composer input height. M002 candidates (typeahead, `..` row, narrow-column guide policy) are deliberately unopened. ****M001 implemented, verified and committed on `main` as `b4f4b916f` (`feat(tui): panel owns its keymap, host owns its chrome`, fast-forwarded onto `origin/main` `5a7381432` first; closure record `plans/closure/file-picker-rework/001-status.md` records it as *conditionally closed*). Every acceptance criterion passes — `verify.sh quick` green on the merged tree, 1866 `tui::` unit tests pass with one pre-existing failure, and 261/261 panel/render integration tests pass including main's own 44 `tui_main_buffer` tests. The only condition was the existence of a commit at which `tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective` M001 can re-inspect `component.rs` and `hosted.rs`; that condition is now met and its registry row records the SHA.** Its `Component::keymap_hint` / `render_in_panel` seams are consumed by `tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective` M001.** The plan's §6.4 `!panel_is_empty()` gate could not be taken literally: the pre-docked session panel is never empty, so it would have advertised keys on every frame and failed the plan's own `no_panel_focused_advertises_no_keys` requirement; each panel now decides, and the session/chat/help/agents panels decline unfocused. `verify.sh full` does **not** pass in this environment — three pre-existing failures, all checked at baseline with the M001 diff stashed (`session_create_through_daemon` rejects `/tmp`; `same_scope_requests_coalesce_to_one_publication` passes in isolation and times out only under 4-way sweep load; `pressing_i_on_the_open_box_enters_focused_input` fails at baseline) — so the sweep stopped at 1110/14167. M002 remains unopened. |
| TUI panel surface convergence corrective | **M001, M002a, M002b and M003 closed** | `plans/subsystems/tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective-addendum.md` | M001 panel manager outcome contract and registry reachability, **closed** at `plans/closure/tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective/001-status.md`; M002a and M002b **closed**; M003 **closed** at `plans/closure/tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective/004-status.md`, planned at `plans/implementation/tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective/004-theme-sourcing-and-shared-chrome-geometry.md` | Presentation-only convergence over the existing panel host; no ADR required, no protocol/storage/identity change. Baseline `b77c8156` shows the three `Component` seams (`docks_as_viewer`, `keymap_hint`, `render_in_panel`) have **one** adoptor each for the latter two: `render_hosted` paints the panel border, title, and `●` focus marker then calls `render_in_panel`, which defaults to `render`, so every hosted widget that paints its own `Block` overwrites all three — ~15 dockable boxes, with `InfoDialog` alone covering 13 `DialogType`s and the `Placeholder` itself being a clobberer. `Placeholder` renders "Run `<cmd>` to open it, then Shift+← to move it here" — instructing the user to leave, to move a panel that is already in place, with a placement-dependent gesture. `Goal`, `Doctor`, and `Plugins` toast "Loading…" while rendering a placeholder asserting nothing was opened; `ShellShow` instructs in two places at once; `Diff` reaches it on the ordinary clean-tree path. `materialize_hosted_panel` encodes this as `matches!(id, Research \| TaskView)` plus two message shapes. `SidePanelId::Workspace` is listed with a hotkey and config key but `WorkspaceDashboardDialog::new()` is `#[cfg(test)]`-only and its mount arm is empty, so it can only ever render a placeholder. `dock_open_box` discards `adopt`'s `false` and silently drops a box for bespoke targets (`Agent`, `Chat`). Coverage: `tests/tui_panel_docking.rs` asserts 7 of ~20 dockable boxes and documents why the rest cannot be mounted; nothing sweeps `SidePanelId::ALL`. M001 owns the typed outcome, placeholder copy, reachability, and adoption surfacing; **M002 owns chrome/keymap adoption, split into M002a (`InfoDialog`, one widget covering fourteen reachable-hosted panels) and M002b (the remaining twelve dockable boxes) because one widget with a wide fan-out and a scroll-clamp coupling is a differently-sized unit from the bulk migration — **M002a is closed**, and **M002b is now closed too**, so seam counts are `render_in_panel` 15 / `keymap_hint` 14 — re-measured by grepping `src/` for the two method names and excluding `component.rs` where the seam is declared, not copied forward from this text; **M003 is now closed:** theme sourcing is done — five dialogs and `DiffViewer` read the live theme, and the selected-row foreground is a derived token. **The `render_in_panel` inset-by-one default flip is withdrawn rather than deferred:** the host passes `frame.area()` (border cells included) to the widget, so an inset only moves a widget's own `Block` one character inward without preventing it — it does not reduce the override count, while `Component::render` remains the modal path where a border is correct. |

Baseline `b77c8156`: the `ShortCycle` tool-name-only variant is already removed and correctly regression-tested, but the ladder is still over-sensitive for a different reason. `ProgressObservation` is built at exactly one production site where `new_evidence` and `child_advanced` are hardcoded `false` with no other writer anywhere in `src/` or `crates/` — so `ProgressSignal::ChildAdvanced` is unreachable and half of `NewEvidence` is dead, while a comment claims child progress is populated "when one is exposed". Surviving progress is narrow: `StateChanged` needs a drained file-change event AND `is_file_modifying_tool` (`write\|edit\|replace\|apply_patch` only) AND success, so a mutating `bash`, a git mutation, and a job submission are not progress. `detect` classifies `EquivalentError` on `canonical_tool` + `error_class` alone — not `action_key()`, not error text — and failing calls can never produce progress, so five consecutive same-tool same-error-class calls reach `Stalled` even with distinct commands and distinct messages. `recoveries` resets only on progress, never on incident-identity change, so unrelated failures accumulate. C001 owns the contract, progress computation, error keying, and accumulation. C002 (diagnostics + dead `recovery_parallel_limit` + guard) is deliberately unopened and polish-ordered after C001. |

Baseline `b77c8156`: the session's own `workspace_root` is correct and follows the launch directory, but when `BashTool` resolves no workdir (argument absent and `workspace_root` `None`, the default-constructor case) six sites in `src/tool/bash/process.rs` fall back to `std::env::current_dir()`, and the daemon inherits its launcher's cwd for its whole lifetime because `crates/codegg-client` spawns it without an explicit cwd. Observed live at review: daemon cwd `/Users/davidbowman/projects/worktree2/codegg` serving a TUI launched from `/Users/davidbowman/projects/i2pr-tc`. `scripts/check_daemon_cwd_usage.py` cannot catch this — it allowlists `ToolRegistryOptions::default()` on the stated premise that the canonical daemon path populates the root, which is the unproven part, and its `PROTECTED_GLOBS` omit `src/main.rs`/`src/exec.rs` by omission though its docstring names both as permitted bootstrap readers. W002 owns `App::set_session`, which adopts a session directory only when `tab.workspace_root.is_none()`, contradicting ADR-0033 §2 ("switching the tab's execution context, tool workdir default, and displayed path"), plus `/sessions` being project-scoped. W003 is net-new capability, not a regression: `/workspace` is the Work-Orders dashboard (rows are "per-project activity summaries used for navigation only"), already carries `pending_permission_count`/`pending_question_count` rendered "counts only; never the underlying permission/question content", and never enumerates sessions; Enter opens/focuses the project and shows the task view. Hooks already exist and are discarded — `PermissionPending`/`QuestionPending` carry `session_id`/`turn_id` and the TUI drops them, `PermissionRespond` is already session-agnostic with daemon-side ownership resolution, and the desktop bridge already renders cross-session pending lists while the TUI subscribes per-session only. W003 also needs a product decision on nesting and switch-first-vs-answer-in-place. Daemon `chdir` stays rejected (ADR-0033 Option B). |
| Interactive terminal liveness post-closure corrective | active (C001 conditionally closed; C002 closed; C003 closed; C004 closed) | `plans/subsystems/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective-addendum.md` |**C004 terminal surface ergonomics — closed 2026-10-17**, plan `plans/implementation/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/004-terminal-surface-ergonomics.md`, closure `plans/closure/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/004-status.md`, class polish, baseline `18c3dca92`. All four owned items delivered, and each falsified on baseline before any production change: the terminal panel no longer advertises its verbs after `Shift+←` handed the keyboard back (the seam's "advertise without the keyboard" contract is now split by kind — a *hosted* panel keeps it, a *bespoke* panel declines, and the terminal is bespoke); the Insert caret blinks on a pure phase derived from the one frame clock and never blinks in NOR, where it is the anchor `x`/`v`/`d` act on; clear now drives the shell's own repaint through the existing typed input path, so what comes back is the reader's own `PS1` rather than an empty panel; and `Esc` places the reader cursor on the last row that has content. 21 new tests plus one integration test, and one C003 test fixture was hardened after it turned out to race the attachment. **This completes the corrective:** C001 conditionally closed with its condition discharged by C003, C002 closed, C003 closed, C004 closed, and nothing left open.  **C003 live-surface cadence arming and honest mode state — closed 2026-10-17**, plan `plans/implementation/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/003-live-surface-cadence-and-honest-mode-state.md`, closure `plans/closure/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/003-status.md`, class capability, baseline `18c3dca92`. The loop's arming decision is now a pure function, `runtime::event_loop::animation_interval`, and a mounted terminal surface is its fourth contributor at 100ms — so the pull's single call site (the timed `select!` arm) is reachable while the frame is idle instead of parking on `pending()`. The badge and the contextual hint line now read one derived answer, `App::composer_input_mode`, over `App::terminal_box_has_keyboard` and `PanelHost::claims_insert_keys`, so the terminal panel's previously unreachable Insert hint branch is live. **C001's conditional status is discharged** (below). **C001 downgraded to conditionally closed 2026-10-17** per rule 6: its strict liveness claim was falsified in the idle state and the condition was owned by C003. C002 closed at `plans/closure/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/002-status.md`; C001 closure `plans/closure/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/001-status.md`, implementation `66f67f229`. C004 terminal surface ergonomics (polish), registered blocked at `plans/implementation/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/004-terminal-surface-ergonomics.md`, **closed 2026-10-17** | Post-closure corrective against `plans/closure/interactive-process-sessions/003-status.md` and `plans/closure/human-execution-corrective/003-status.md`; no ADR required (the closed attach/resume/resync contract is reused; a push transport would need one). **C003 findings at `18c3dca92`:** (1) `service_terminal_output_pull` has one call site, inside the timed `select!` arm at `src/tui/runtime/event_loop.rs:457`, and that arm runs only when `next_wake` is `Some` (`:288-292`) — `render_interval` is `Duration::ZERO` when `!streaming_active` (`:150-154`) so `render_delay_after` returns `None`, and `animation_interval` is `Some` only while streaming/working/toasting (`:262-264`); with `next_wake == None` the arm parks on `pending()` (`:423-428`). A mounted live terminal is therefore read only while a toast is alive, a keystroke arrives, or a resize debounces, so a command's output appears only when the user presses the next key. (2) The canonical badge reads `ui_state.input_mode` (`src/tui/app/render.rs:1932-1943`), `focus_panel` forces `Normal` on panel focus (`src/tui/commands/panels.rs:692-702`), and `focus_terminal_panel`/`blur_terminal_panel` never touch `input_mode` — so the badge is pinned `[NOR]` in both modes and the INS branch at `src/tui/panels/terminal.rs:409` is unreachable. **Why C001's evidence missed it:** the C001 acceptance tests call `service_terminal_output_pull(&mut app)` directly (`src/tui/commands/interactive_terminal.rs:2975`), so they prove the function, not the wiring — the harness supplied the poll loop the production loop withholds. C003 Work package A must drive the real loop and fail on baseline first. Historical C001/C002 baseline research is retained in this row's predecessor text below. C004 additionally owns the unfocused hint row, the Insert blink, the post-clear prompt line, and `Esc` reader-cursor placement; its blink is technically blocked on C003 because a parked loop cannot animate. Baseline `719cfeb5`: the **write** path is complete on both surfaces — the box routes at `src/tui/app/mod.rs:4024` → `handle_terminal_key` → `send_terminal_input`, the panel at `src/tui/panels/terminal.rs:329` → `TuiMsg::TerminalPanelInput`, and the daemon writes to the PTY master (`src/core/daemon.rs:4113` → `src/interactive_process_attach.rs:792`) — but the **read** path does not exist in production. The TUI builds exactly two output-bearing requests, `InteractiveProcessAttach` (`commands/interactive_terminal.rs:409`) and `InteractiveProcessResume` (`:593`), and `start_terminal_resume` has exactly one caller: the `/terminal-resume` slash command (`src/tui/app/mod.rs:8211`). No timer, no render-cadence hook, no post-input read (`flush_terminal_input` drains input and a pending resize only, `:703-704`); the daemon cannot push because its only interactive event, `CoreEvent::InteractiveProcessExited`, is bytes-free by contract (`crates/codegg-protocol/src/core.rs:4124`). A shell's prompt arrives after the attach request returns and nothing ever reads again, so both surfaces render an empty view indefinitely while the daemon holds the bytes in its ring — the reported symptom, with no error and no toast. `tests/interactive_terminal_tui.rs:83` (`poll_resume_text`) supplies a real 25 ms poll loop but calls `protocol.resume` **directly on the protocol, outside `App`**, so the composition that shipped broken was never tested; `tests/tui_panel_docking.rs:1516` asserts only the send seam. Separately, and verified against the untouched baseline rather than inferred: `pressing_i_on_the_open_box_enters_focused_input` **already fails at `719cfeb5`** (assertion `a freshly opened box starts viewing, never focused`), so a freshly opened box violates the controller's own documented `Viewing` start and forwards the first `i` to the shell instead of entering INS mode — a pre-existing defect owned by C001 Work package G, not a C001 regression. Two hazards C001 owns: the docked panel's resize drains only via `flush_terminal_resize`, reachable only from the input flush (`:704`), so a never-typed terminal keeps its create-time 80x24 (`:260`) and would render wrapped once liveness lands; and `dialog_state.terminal_request` is a single-slot `AsyncUiRequestState` shared by all nine terminal operations (`src/tui/app/state/dialog.rs:169`), so a naive poll calling `begin()` would supersede an in-flight input/resize/detach/terminate completion and silently drop it. C001 is bounded to the socket-daemon frontend; C002 owns the WS-attach/server transport, where every terminal command reads `app.core_client` (`:78`–`:818`) and the WS-attach TUI frontend (`src/client/attach.rs`) opens `/tui`, speaks `TuiMessage`, and never constructs a `CoreClient`. **Correction (2026-10-17, C002 research):** the original claim that "`src/server/**` has no `InteractiveProcess` routing at all" is true of `/tui` only. The server exposes three WS endpoints, and `/core` speaks `CoreFrame::Request(RequestEnvelope<CoreRequest>)` (`crates/codegg-protocol/src/frames.rs:181`) with **no variant allowlist** — its only gates are a five-variant `is_secret_bearing()` check that excludes interactive processes and projection-only checks (`src/server/ws.rs:4352`), then `daemon.handle_request_for_client(envelope, client_id)` with the authenticated connection id (`:4429`). So `InteractiveProcess*` is already routable over `/core`. C002 therefore delivers **Option B** — the nine refusal sites all emit "Daemon connection unavailable; …", which is *false* on the WS-attach frontend where the daemon is healthy, so C002 makes the refusal name the transport and the real cause. Option A (routing terminal bytes) is ADR-gated and out of scope. The separate remote-authorization question is registered as `plans/subsystems/remote-interactive-process-authorization-corrective-roadmap.md`; C002's closure must not claim it is resolved. C002 **closed 2026-10-17** on baseline `8b6191140`: one shared classifier (`TerminalRefusal` — `TransportUnsupported` vs `NoDaemonConnection`) now owns the wording and all nine entry points route through it, so the two causes can no longer share one sentence; the connection wording is preserved byte-for-byte where it is true. Implementation additionally found a defect the plan did not anticipate: `flush_terminal_input` classified *after* `take_pending_input` and `begin_input`, so a refused flush dropped the queued keystrokes **and** left `input_in_flight` permanently set — one visible toast, then silence for the life of the attachment. It was unreachable on the attach frontend, which is why it survived. Fixed, with the drained resize restored to its queue on refusal. 169 tests green across the five terminal/panel suites (6 new); `verify.sh quick` passes locally (CI has not run it). |
| User prompt queue and turn contention | **active; M001 closed; M002a closed; M002b closed; M003 closed; M004 closed; M005 closed; M006 closed; M007 closed; C001 closed** | `plans/subsystems/user-prompt-queue-roadmap.md` | M003 delivery into turns is **closed** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/003-status.md`), and with it the workstream's first observable behaviour. **M004 is now `closed`** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/004-status.md`) — the first milestone a person can operate: `/queue` opens a list, `j`/`k`/`r`/`d` are the only keys it advertises, a `delivering` row's refusal says *being delivered* and never *expired*, a delete adopts the projection the delete returned with no second read, and row 0 names the waiting count without adding a row. **M005 is now `closed`** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/005-status.md`) — the queue is authorable and correctable: `a` composes, `e` revises in place, and `[`/`]` reorder, alongside M004's list and delete. Its plan's §3(b) open question is settled by measurement rather than inference — a released `delivering` entry resumes at the **back** of the queue and keeps the displacement ordinal, which is recorded as accepted-and-documented because changing `renumber_dense` reopens closed M002b/M003. **All four ADRs are accepted** as of 2026-10-18. **M006 and M007 are now written** (`plans/implementation/user-prompt-queue/006-send-now-steering-from-the-queue.md`, `.../007-delayed-queue-delivery.md`): **M006 is now `closed`** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/006-status.md`) — `s` in `/queue` hands a queued prompt to the turn that is already running. It needed **no migration**: the acceptance decision it was planned against shipped as `mark_steer_delivered`, a second `pending → delivered` transition guarded on `pending` and bound to the accepting turn using columns v80 already created, so a steered prompt cannot then be claimed again as a fresh turn. The request carries `entry_id` and nothing else — no prompt text, no `turn_id` — and the handover is gated by the turn controller, never by `ControllerScope::QueueMutation`, with queue ownership re-asked inside the settlement transaction. One outcome exceeded the plan's two-valued shape: `AcceptedNotSettled`, for the turn having taken the prompt while the queue could not record it, which as an error would have invited the user to resend something that already ran. **M007 is now `closed`** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/007-status.md`) — a prompt can be scheduled for later, and it is never delivered early. Its decision is recorded in `plans/adrs/ADR-0039-queue-advancement-wakeup-authority.md`, **accepted 2026-10-18**. The decision was **Option A**, a bounded queue-scoped advance tick: the roadmap premise of "an existing periodic observation point" was falsified during planning (the daemon has no periodic session observation loop, and M003's delivery entry had exactly two permitted callers, neither of which fires for an idle session with a scheduled entry), so a new wakeup source was unavoidable. What the implementation guarantees is narrower and honest — the tick computes the earliest due instant, sleeps exactly until it, and **parks with no timer armed** when nothing is scheduled. M007 takes **migration v82**, so `STORAGE_LAYOUT_VERSION` is now **82**; `run_at_ms` is a nullable column with **no `CHECK`** and **no sentinel**, because v80's `status` `CHECK` cannot be altered additively at all. The authoring surface is a **second step** (text, then duration) rather than an inline `| 1h` suffix, because any inline delimiter is a character the user cannot type and prompt text must not be reinterpreted. A due time is a **floor, not a promise**: delivered at the first turn boundary at or after it, and *late* across a restart, never early. Widening the delivery entry to a third caller was a **recorded decision**, with the guard's `--self-test` re-proven by injecting a **fourth** caller and rejecting it. The milestone's own wakeup test then found and fixed a real defect: a due-but-undeliverable entry left the tick re-arming at zero delay and spinning. **C001 is closed** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/C001-status.md`) — the invariant corrective answering the maintainer decision M005 recorded. The displacement was worse than measured, because `claim_next_for_delivery` orders by `ordinal`, so a released entry was *delivered* last, not merely rendered last. It is fixed with **no migration**: `renumber_dense` writes the whole live set, holding each non-pending row at its existing rank, and `release_delivery` / `resolve_stranded_delivery(Retry)` are now order-neutral. The `docs/tui.md` workaround is gone. Two further defects were found and reported rather than fixed per C001 §14 — `retry_failed`'s revive, and a pre-existing collision when the displacement runs a **second** time — and both are pinned by tests. **M008 is now eligible** (M004 and M005 both closed) and remains explicitly deferrable | Baseline `18c3dca9`: a prompt composed during an active turn is **destroyed**, not merely refused — the composer clears before the transport decision and the `turn_already_active` arm restores nothing. The same arm also **corrupts active-turn state**: it reports every submit failure through `fail_unstarted_turn`, which clears `working_since`/`active_turn_id`, sets `Error`, and stops the spinner *including when a turn is still running on the daemon*. So the UI asserts "no active turn" while one exists — fatal for any queue that must consult turn state to choose between queueing and steering. Two backend facts reshape the estimate: **`CoreRequest::TurnSteer` is already implemented, controller-gated, and has no frontend issuer at all** (zero `TurnSteer` matches under `src/tui/`), and it is `try_send` on a bounded channel with `steer_queue_full`/`unavailable`/`not_supported` — best-effort, so `f` = "force" would misdescribe it (ADR-0036 fixes the guarantee as *acceptance* and forbids "force" in copy). A **second, unrelated** mechanism already drains prompts: `AgentLoop::drain_follow_up` consumes at turn boundaries and documents that late arrivals are not processed by the same run — two "queued prompt" notions with different guarantees, so ADR-0036 fixes terminology and requires cross-references. **Durability is the load-bearing decision (ADR-0035):** session attach is non-exclusive (`SessionAttach` dispatches identically to `SessionLoad`; `attach_session` appends to a `Vec`), single-writer is the durable controller lease, admission is receipted by envelope `request_id`, startup marks accepted→`interrupted` and never replays, the WS transport "makes no staging or acceptance claim from queue delivery", and composer state is memory-only — so a frontend-local queue dies on reconnect and a "replay on reconnect" would bypass at-most-once. ADR-0035 selects a daemon-owned durable table (migration **v80**, `STORAGE_LAYOUT_VERSION` currently **79**, additive precedent v65) modelled on `human_shell_promotion` (24h TTL, status, `consumed_turn_id`), and explicitly rejects modelling prompts as `JobTemplate` because schedules materialize *jobs*. Sequence is backend-first by design: M001 (bug, ready) → M002 storage (infrastructure, **not** capability) → M003 delivery → M004 box+row-0 suffix → M005 add/edit/reorder → M006 steer → M007 timers → M008 desktop parity. M002–M008 are deliberately unplanned: each waits on a closure or an ADR acceptance. Two inherited box rules recorded in the roadmap: no box advertises `Esc`/`Enter` as a close verb, and a caller-supplied footer is not replayed into the keymap hint; `Shift+J/K` are panel directions the docked host intercepts *before* the widget, which is why an existing panel's advertised reorder keys were dead — a modal box avoids it, and M004 must prove non-vacuity rather than assume it. **M001 is now closed:** contention takes `App::fail_contended_turn`, which leaves `active_turn_id`, `working_since`, session status, and the spinner untouched and reports a warning toast instead of latching a turn error (row 0 gives `last_error` absolute precedence, so latching would hide the live turn's status line — the same corruption in a different costume). Every recoverable refusal restores the composed text through the single `PromptState::restore_prompt_with_stash` shape, which three call sites previously reimplemented with a hardcoded `100`. The submit path now uses `spawn_tui_task` plus an `AsyncUiRequestState` generation guard, kept separate from the daemon's `request_id` idempotency key. Two further live defects were found and fixed: `send_prompt` was overwriting the failure release with `Working` (so the no-core-client and no-session paths had never actually fixed anything), and a stale completion could still null a live turn because only the pending-record clear was guarded. M002's hard dependency is discharged. **ADR-0035 was accepted 2026-10-18**, and the acceptance audit found that its authority clause — "the same controller predicate that governs turn admission" — has **no answer while a session is idle**: `session_turn_controller` is keyed `(session_id, turn_id)`, `acquire` rejects an empty `turn_id`, and `release` `DELETE`s the row, so `controller_lease_for` returns `None` between turns and `check_turn_controller` refuses *every* principal, including the owner. The only idle-passing path is `is_controller_bypass` → `is_local_owner_broad`, an identity comparison against `LOCAL_OWNER_PRINCIPAL_ID` that is **not session-bound** — it cannot express "a session you do not control". Mutating a queue *between turns* is the primary use, so this is a canonical ownership-boundary decision, not an implementation detail. **M002 is therefore split:** M002a (storage + read projection, `ready`, dependency-clean under every ADR outcome) and M002b (mutation operations + mutation authority, `blocked`). ADR-0037 is raised as **proposed** and confers no authorization (ADR-0030/ADR-0034 precedent), so M002b does not start. Two ADR-referenced seams were corrected against source: the queue read uses `Capability::SessionRead` rather than the broader `AgentInvoke` that `human_shell_promotions_get` uses, and `is_controller_bypass` gains no queue authority. **M002a is now closed** (closure record `plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/002a-status.md`): **v80** landed with the durable, bounded, expiring `user_prompt_queue_entry` table, a schema-enforced `UNIQUE (session_id, ordinal)`, typed refusals for every bound, the lazy transactional expiry sweep, and `UserPromptQueueList` under `Capability::SessionRead`. It **delivered no user-visible capability** — no mutation exists, so the read has no producer and the queue always reads empty. **ADR-0037 was accepted the same day**, selecting **Option B**: a durable, session-scoped queue-owner binding, independent of turn state, surviving restart, changed only by explicit audited transfer. `is_controller_bypass` is explicitly not extended. Mid-turn the owner and the controller must both match the caller, and all four mismatch combinations are typed refusals rather than a precedence rule — the sharp edge the ADR exists to resolve. One correction was recorded inside ADR-0037 itself: its draft folded the owner binding into **v80**, but M002a had already shipped v80 with the queue table alone, so the claim was corrected to **v81** while the ADR was still `proposed`, with the difference between draft and accepted text stated in the accepted record's header. Consequently `run_at_ms` moves to **v82**. **M002b is now closed** (closure record `plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/002b-status.md`): **v81** landed carrying only `user_prompt_queue_owner`, the four mutations reached production producers, and the queue is no longer permanently empty — but it **delivered no user-visible capability** either, because no command, dialog, or key reaches any of it. The adoption policy ADR-0037 deferred to this milestone is settled and audited: **the calling principal becomes the owner** on first add, not the session creator and not the local interactive owner; first-add adopts only on an `Absent` queue (creation), never on an `Unowned` one (repair), so an invalidated queue cannot silently become owned by whoever touches it next. `is_controller_bypass` gained no queue authority — its early return is guarded by `matches!(scope, ControllerScope::Turn { .. })`, and a test proves it by execution rather than by inspection. Two real bugs the new tests caught and fixed are recorded in the closure record rather than shipped: `delete` read the surviving projection without renumbering, leaving a permanent ordinal gap, and `is_permutation` had an inverted `HashSet` duplicate check (`!insert` accepts a duplicate rather than rejecting it). M002b is closed and M003 is next. |

| Remote interactive process authorization corrective | proposed | `plans/subsystems/remote-interactive-process-authorization-corrective-roadmap.md` | R001 not started; a new ADR is required before any implementation milestone opens | Registered 2026-10-17 from C002 transport research, deliberately **not** folded into the terminal-liveness C002 milestone. Owns one question: which principals may create, drive, and terminate a PTY over a remote transport. Evidence at baseline `8b6191140`: all ten `CoreRequest::InteractiveProcess*` variants are classified `ScopeKind::Global` with `capability: None` (`crates/codegg-core/src/authorization/policy.rs:911-940`), and `Global` is documented as "any active principal may proceed" (`:11-13`); the policy comment states the gate "admits any authenticated caller; the attachment seam fails closed per process" (`:909-910`). `/core` is `CoreAdapter`, not LocalOwner-only (`src/server/authz.rs:358-364`) and routes arbitrary requests to `daemon.handle_request_for_client`. `InteractiveTransport` is consulted in exactly one place — `can_terminate()`, denying a remote principal by default (`src/interactive_process_attach.rs:340-345`) — so a remote principal can **create a PTY and run arbitrary commands but not terminate it**. This is reachable today by any client speaking `CoreFrame` on `/core` without the TUI; it is unexercised only because the interactive-terminal frontend exists solely on the socket-daemon transport where every principal is the local owner. R001 decides the contract and requires a new ADR; the roadmap also inherits `human-execution-corrective` C001's "unsupported remote targets fail explicitly" precedent. No implementation may open before that ADR is accepted. |

| TUI composer Helix keybinding parity | **active** (ADR-0042 **accepted** 2026-10-19 with the `space`-chord amendment; M001 closed; M002 closed; M003 closed; M004 closed; M005 closed; M006 closed; M007 closed) | `plans/subsystems/tui-composer-helix-parity-roadmap.md` | M001 — `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/001-composer-keyboard-ownership-and-cursor-presence.md`, closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/001-status.md`; M002 — `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/002-normal-mode-key-ownership-contract.md`, closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/002-status.md`; M003 — `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/003-composer-text-model.md`, closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/003-status.md`; M004 — `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/004-composer-motions.md`, closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/004-status.md`; M005 — `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/005-composer-selection-and-clipboard.md`, closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/005-status.md`; M006 — `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/006-composer-undo-redo-and-insert-transitions.md`, closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/006-status.md`; M007 — `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/007-keymap-consistency-and-guard-coverage.md`, closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/007-status.md` | New workstream for the **main composer** (the "LLM buffer" the user types into), a peer of the editor and the terminal panel rather than a subordinate of the editor. Baseline `61cbe9222`. The composer is a **frontend-local string** — not a document, not daemon-authorized, delivered only on submit — so it is a new surface, not an editor milestone, and it reuses `src/tui/helix_selection.rs` rather than the editor's document/controller machinery. Survey at baseline: `PromptWidget` has **no selection, no undo, no yank**; its `focused` flag is **dead** (`PromptWidget::blur()`'s only caller is a test), so "no cursor when another surface holds the keyboard" is currently unimplementable; `InputMode` is a single global field that means *"bare letters are claimable app actions"*, not a navigation mode; and **`InputTarget` describes the composer's delivery destination (`Build`/`Chat`), not focus** — "the LLM buffer is selected" has no representation today, so M001 derives a keyboard-ownership fact alongside the existing `composer_input_mode()`. At the baseline the app **positioned no terminal cursor anywhere** — ratatui 0.30.2 hides it whenever the frame did not position it, so M001 gets a *blinking* Insert cursor from the terminal's own cursor with **no new cadence** (the single-100ms-cadence rule in `AGENTS.md` forbids an app-driven blink) and a painted solid block for Normal, with the fake block and the real cursor mutually exclusive; M001 is now **closed**. **Key-alphabet collisions are the central risk and are enumerated in ADR-0042:** `x` is turn stop/resume, `d` is open-diff (vim map), `j`/`k` are transcript scroll, `g` is go-to-top, `i` is focus-prompt; `h`/`l` are **not** conflicts because the existing `Left`/`Right` actions already move the composer caret. `e`, `b`, `v`, `o`, `u`, `U`, `p`, `w` are free. `space` is **not** a conflict either, and that was the ADR amendment: help keeps `space` in both maps, and the Helix copy gesture is preserved as the **second step** of that chord — `space` opens the help menu, and `y` in the menu `space` opened copies the composer's selection, while the `/help` command's box does not carry the verb; the composer therefore claims neither `space` nor `y`. The rule adopted is the terminal panel's: a claimed key is consumed even when the verb is a no-op, so it can never leak to the app action — the editor's `check_tui_editor_key_authority.py` targets only the editor's command module, so M002 adds a **composer-scoped** guard with a `--self-test` (the three editor TUI guards predate that convention; `check_tui_subagent_name_pool_isolation.py` is the precedent). The re-spellings (stop/resume, open-diff, focus-prompt) are a deliberate user-visible break, recorded in the ADR rather than discovered; help is not re-spelled. **M002 is now `closed`** (`plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/002-status.md`) — a proposed ADR confers no implementation authorization (the ADR-0030/0034 precedent), and both of M002's dependencies were discharged before the work opened. What landed: `src/tui/composer_claims.rs` is the composer's **single** claim table — 13 letters (`b d e i j k o p u U v w x`), each with exactly one owner, an explicit condition (`j`/`k` only, and only on a multi-line composer buffer), an explicit encoding set (a claimed capital answers to both the `SHIFT` and the `NONE` encoding) and an explicit status — and the claim step runs in `App::on_key` **ahead of** the binding map, gated on the *resolved* mode plus `App::composer_holds_keyboard`, so a reserved verb is consumed and does nothing and a claimed letter can never leak to an unrelated app action. That is the fallthrough rule the ADR adopted from the terminal panel, and it is what makes the table safe to grow before the verbs exist. The three re-spellings are `Ctrl+Alt+X` (stop/resume), `Ctrl+Alt+D` (open-diff) and `Ctrl+Alt+E` (focus-prompt), in **both** maps — `Ctrl+Alt+I` is not deliverable, because crossterm reports `ESC` + `0x09` as `Tab`+ALT, so the mnemonic spelling would have been unreachable on every terminal — and the default map's bare `/` for focus-prompt stays, because `/` is not a claimed letter and so never conflicted with the claim table, while `space` still opens help in both maps, which is the ADR's amendment. `scripts/check_tui_composer_key_authority.py` (in `verify.sh quick` + the CI `verify` job) fails on a non-closed table, a claimed key that can still reach an app action, a re-spelled action missing from a map, a surviving bare spelling, a capital claim missing an encoding, and four more classes; its `--self-test` injects each class and the delivered tree is clean. **One scoping deviation, recorded in the closure rather than improvised:** the plan put "no insert transition" out of scope, but `i` is the app's **only** route back to Insert mode — it is the `None` arm of the key chain, not a binding — so a reserved `i` would have consumed the key, done nothing, and stranded the user in Normal mode with no way to type. The `i` verb is therefore **live**, running the app's **existing** transition with the table as its new single owner; M006 still owns the insert transitions beyond `i`. The twelve reserved verbs are **not** shipped capabilities and the closure does not present them as such. Open decision points: D3 blink source (**settled in M001** by invariant I7 — the terminal's own cursor; the row was stale until the corrective addendum closed it), D4 undo scope, D5 paste scope — paste has **no precedent in any Helix surface** (the editor and terminal panel yank to the clipboard; neither has a paste verb). Multi-selection, text objects, match mode, and goto mode are out of scope for the whole workstream and remain tracked by the editor roadmap. **Post-closure correction:** M002's exit condition "`h` and `l` still move the composer caret" was **not met** when it closed — the two letters were bound in the vim map only, and `vim_mode` defaults to false, so the default configuration drew a composer caret that no letter could move. The corrective addendum below owns the fix. |
| TUI composer Helix keybinding parity — post-closure corrective | **closed** (C001 closed; the addendum's own status is "closed — C001 closed") | `plans/subsystems/tui-composer-helix-parity-corrective-addendum.md` | C001 — `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity-corrective/001-normal-mode-caret-motion-premise.md`, closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity-corrective/001-status.md` | Opened because M002's closure matrix had **no acceptance-criterion row** for its `h`/`l` exit condition, and that condition was the one that was false: `h` and `l` moved the composer caret in the vim map and in no other, `vim_mode` defaults to false, and the default map's bare letters were only `space` `/` `?` `g` `j` `k` `q`. The user saw a solid Normal-mode caret with no letter that could move it — `h`/`l` unbound, `w`/`b`/`e` claimed and consumed as pending verbs. The guard polices the claim **table** against the maps and says nothing about a letter the table deliberately does not claim, so a false premise expressed in prose about the maps was invisible to it. C001 binds both letters in the default map, makes the guard's parser carry the non-character key code, and adds rule **G9** (the two maps must bind every caret motion to the same key and the same action) with three self-test cases; the guard run against the pre-correction tree reports the two `h`/`l` findings, which is the evidence the rule is armed. ADR-0042's **decision** is unchanged — it was a statement about the binding maps that was false, and the fix makes it true. C001 adds no composer verb: `w`/`b`/`e` remain claimed and reserved, and motion still lands with M004 behind M003. |

a **new** leaf-authority guard polices the leaf tables); M011 closed — `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/011-status.md`; M012 closed — `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/012-status.md`; M013 closed — `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/013-status.md`; M015 closed — `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/015-status.md`; M016 closed — `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/016-status.md`; M017 closed — `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/017-status.md`; it does not reopen that closed roadmap or its milestones. Baseline `719cfeb5d`. Survey of the actual dispatch tables (not the docs) found: **`Ctrl+r` redo is unreachable** — `editor_buffer_key`'s mode-independent resync guard returns `RequestResync` before `handle_normal_key`'s `editor_redo` arm, and that function has exactly one call site, so Normal mode has undo (`u`) and no reachable redo; the three existing redo tests call `editor_redo` directly and therefore never exercise the key path, which is why the suite is green. **M001 is closed:** redo is now `U` (Helix's own chord), `Ctrl+r` keeps exactly one owner in both modes, a second dead-alternative instance (`\| (m, Char('d'))` in the `D` arm) was also closed, and `scripts/check_tui_editor_key_authority.py` plus dispatch-path tests make the class non-recurring. **Both bespoke prefix mechanisms are now gone** — M002 replaced `pending_command: String` and `yank_prefix_pending: bool` with one `EditorPrefix` enum field, so `[`/`]` are armed against a real grammar and a future `Alt-*` or `f`-capture grammar adds a leaf rather than a flag. **M003 used exactly that leaf path:** `f`/`F`/`t`/`T` are `EditorPrefix` variants with no presentation field of their own — their next key is *data* (the target character) rather than a leaf glyph. The find scan is bounded and **discloses** when it stops short (`no 'z' within the 2 MiB scan bound`) rather than reporting a truncated result as absent. Two real defects were found and fixed: a per-byte scan that could not read a byte inside a multi-byte character (`read_bytes` refuses a non-boundary range, so it walked past every multi-byte target — 79 s → 0.22 s once chunked), and an M001 test asserting `f`/`t`/`F`/`T` were unclaimed. **M003 does not fully distinguish `t`/`T` from `f`/`F`:** per the plan's AC2 they land on the target, which at this milestone makes them cursor-equivalent — the real distinction is a selection property and belongs with M007. Recorded, not papered over. **Planning pass 2026-10-18 added M013 after verifying two LSP delivery defects at baseline `84440fe7f`:** the diagnostics publisher has zero callers (so F2 can never return contents) and a multi-server workspace root resolves to a false `NotReady` (so F3–F9 are dead in any polyglot project). M013 is now a hard prerequisite of M010; binding LSP keys before it lands would ship always-`NotReady` panels. ~~`SelectionState` holds one `Option<Selection<P>>`, never a set, so ~15 Helix selection verbs are ~~~~unimplementable as written rather than merely unbound.~~ **Resolved by M007** (closed 2026-10-18): `SelectionState` now holds `Vec<Selection<P>>` plus a `primary` index, and the single-selection accessors were kept **primary-scoped** rather than replaced, so the terminal panel kept running the same rules with no call site changed. **No tree-sitter exists anywhere in the workspace**, so every `TS`-marked Helix binding is blocked at the dependency level — gated on proposed ADR-0034. `LspReadOperation` stays read-only by construction; M013 extends it with read variants and rename/format **preview** only, while `=` format / `space r` rename apply / `space a` code-action apply remain deferred pending an authorization ADR. No paste binding existed at all until **M004**, which is now closed.** M004 shipped `p`/`P` paste, bare `y`, `r`, `R`, `~`, `` ` `` and `` Alt-` ``: a user can now move text (`y` then `p`), which was impossible before. It corrected the live Helix keymap against the M004 implementation plan, which had `R` as vi's "replace to end of line" — Helix binds it to `replace_with_yanked`, taking no argument and entering no Insert mode, so the plan's whole retained-tail design was dropped (amendment A1). `R` was already bound to reload as a Normal-only **guard-block** chord, and that chord is exactly what `scripts/check_tui_editor_key_authority.py` rejects as shadowing a Normal-mode verb, so it and `BufferKey::RequestReload` are gone; **reload survives as `:e`**, so a keystroke moved but no capability was lost. There is no editor-local register: `y`/`space y` both write the OS clipboard, which is why `p` pastes text from any application. M009 is blocked on **ADR-0034 acceptance alone** — its M008 edge was discharged by M008's closure on 2026-10-19, and a proposed ADR confers no authorization, matching the ADR-0030 precedent. **Planning pass 2026-10-18 (later the same day) wrote plans for M006, M007 and M008 and produced nine corrections against this roadmap's own prose.** The three that matter: M006's boundary was **claiming `]d`/`[d`/`]D`/`[D` twice** — M013's boundary already named "]`/`[` diagnostic navigation" — and also described `]D`/`[D` and `]G`/`[G` **backwards** (`]` is *forward* throughout the layer, so `]D` is last and `[D` is first); those four bindings are now **M013's**. M006's **change** quartet (`]g`/`[g`/`]G`/`[G`) has **no reachable data source** — `egggit`'s `ChangedFile`/`FileDiff` carry no line ranges and the only parsed hunk type, `DiffHunk`, is behind a private widget method — so the roadmap's "reuse the existing diff surface" is false and it is registered as deferred product work needing a milestone of its own. M007's entry wrongly named **`D` as selection-dependent**: `resolve_edit` never calls `selection_range`, so `D` stays cursor-only rather than gaining a new semantic on a local extension. M008 was registered **blocked on M007**, which `plans/003-planning-process.md` §4 makes mandatory; it was written early only to freeze the set API contract M007 must expose. **That write-out paid off** — the contract shipped as pinned and M008 went `ready` with no corrective pass. **M008 is now closed** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/008-status.md`, 2026-10-19): all eighteen verbs are bound, the guard moved from 48 to 66 Normal-mode chords, and the `:` command line was reused for the regex verbs, so `check_tui_editor_prefix_state.py` emits byte-identical output before and after — no new prefix field, no new input mode. Two real defects were found by the milestone's own tests and fixed: `K` with no matches refused instead of emptying the set, and `C` at end-of-buffer reported success while adding a cursor on the member's own byte, because `len_lines()` counts a trailing line no byte offset resolves into. **Terminal-panel parity broke on exactly one chord and is recorded, not fixed:** the panel claims `C`/`c` for `Clear` while the editor uses `C` to add a cursor. ~~M008 is now `ready` with no corrective pass.~~ **M011 is now closed** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/011-status.md`, 2026-10-19): the nine Helix insert-mode chords are bound with exactly one owner each — the insert table became a tuple match on `(modifiers, code)` — the two mode-blind panels (the completion popup and the LSP diagnostics panel) no longer claim a bare character or `Enter` in Insert mode, and a typing burst is now **one** undo step through an insert session that commits when insert mode ends. `Ctrl+s`/`Ctrl+r` keep their mode-independent save/resync meaning; Helix's insert-mode `Ctrl+s` (undo checkpoint) and `Ctrl+r` (register) are declined, and the checkpoint capability is delivered by the coalescing instead. The guard's Insert chord count is **8**, not the 9 this roadmap's §13 originally recorded — the guard counts character claims, and two of the nine are key-code claims while `Ctrl+x` lives in a stage the guard does not scan; the Normal-mode count is unchanged at 66, which is the number M012 pins. Four existing tests changed because they asserted the old per-keystroke granularity. **M015 closed 2026-10-19 (roadmap Amendment A1):** the two LSP previews M013 shipped now have keys — `=` shows the format preview, `space r` collects a new name on the `:` command line and shows the rename preview — both **apply nothing**, and `space a` states that no code-action request exists. The chords cannot reach ADR-0008's accepted LSP write path either — they produce a *read result*, not a registered reviewable artifact — so the gap is the **bridge**, not an ADR. The preview/apply transition is registered now, in the roadmap, the closure record and the `CHANGELOG.md`, so the future milestone does not change a chord's meaning silently. The guard's Normal-mode chord count is **71** — one for the `=` arm — and the leaf table grew by two glyphs (`r`, `a`), so the leaf-authority guard reports 5 tables / 44 glyphs / 12 bindings. **M016 is `ready`** (`plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/016-preview-apply-bridge.md`): the bridge the M015 closure named — the editor's preview becomes a **reviewable artifact**, the chords lead to the change review, and the review is the apply step, through ADR-0008's existing accepted path (no new ADR). The candidate is **carried** in the read result rather than registered: `LspReadGet` registers nothing, no protocol request exports a preview, and the TUI's `app.lsp_tool` registry is `None` in socket/remote mode. The apply path needs no registry either — `apply_preview` recomputes the digest over the request's own patches, so a candidate altered in transit fails the check. **M019 closed 2026-10-20** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/019-status.md`): `o` and `O` were **bound and inert** — the code that opens the line was written, unit-tested, and unreachable from the key path, so both keys dropped the reader into `INS` on the line they were already on, while `docs/tui.md` had been describing the working behaviour the whole time. The two arms now go through the **shared edit funnel**, so the line they open is a real document edit and a read-only attachment refuses them and says so. Two real defects were found by the milestone's own dispatch-path tests and fixed: the open-line cursor was **one byte past** the line it opened, which put the reader's typing on the *next* line; and the open-line edit was being folded into the typing run it cannot continue, which split the word the reader typed across two entries — a typing run now starts only where the cursor ends. The line and the word are **two** undo steps, registered as a residual rather than papered over. The chord and leaf counts did not move: **2 / 71 / 8** and **5 / 44 / 12**. **M020 closed 2026-10-20** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/020-status.md`): the roadmap's §11 binding bullet named a **dead** help overlay (`src/tui/components/help_overlay.rs` is unreferenced), a `:` palette that is a command registry rather than a keymap, and a hand-maintained document that **nothing compared to the code** — so the bullet could not be true, and could not be false either. §11 now carries a dated **Amendment A1** narrowing it to the surfaces that exist, and `scripts/check_tui_editor_doc_drift.py` makes the amended bullet mechanical: every leaf gesture and claimed chord the code owns is documented, and every key the document declares unbound **is** unbound, checked in both directions through the key- and leaf-authority guards' models. It is registered in `scripts/verify.sh quick` and in the CI `verify` job. The chord and leaf counts did not move: **2 / 71 / 8** and **5 / 44 / 12**. |

## Eggplan integration planning reconciliation

The Eggplan integration M001 had not been implemented or closed when the
Eggwork M002/M002a execution surface changed. Per `plans/003-planning-process.md`,
this is not a post-closure corrective pass; the ready M001 handoff was revised
in place against current head `f5f8d96d7b7371c8583196c58d36ef7b3118ed3c`.

Material corrections now registered:

- storage layout remains v66, so M001 owns additive migration v67;
- Eggwork exact-source sealing occurs around construction of the immutable
  in-memory `WorkspaceSnapshot`, before upload;
- canonical Eggwork manifest digest is persisted as remote-input integrity
  provenance;
- `skipped_non_regular` or `skipped_oversize` makes exact-subject provenance
  unavailable even if Git S1 == S2;
- later local edits after the immutable snapshot seal do not invalidate the
  historical remote input;
- no current-worktree recapture may backfill historical execution evidence.

M001 is now closed at CodeGG `418fdc85656e7e1faa57f71e5e7f10f7f4859c60`
with hosted CI run `36106606574` green. Eggplan CodeGG Integration M002 is
dependency-ready; its own verification-digest and differential-adoption work
remains in scope. The older planning-registration run
`36027583460` failed Workspace Clippy and is retained only as historical
non-passing evidence.

| TUI subagent and job visibility convergence (corrective) | C001 `Ctrl+T` consolidation and reachability | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/001-ctrl-t-consolidation-and-reachability.md` | Depends on: nothing. Class **capability**, delivering I3 and new invariant **I6** (every advertised chord resolves to the action it advertises, in the mode it is advertised under). Rebinds the **existing** `Ctrl+T` chord from `ToggleTodoList` to `ToggleTasksList` in all three keymaps and retires `Shift+T` in both encodings; adds no new chord, so `modifier_chords` stays green. Grouping and empty-group omission are **reused** from `build_task_rows`, not rewritten. The coupling that matters: `ToggleTasksList` may leave `is_normal_only` **only** because no capital spelling reaches it — keeping `Shift+T` while relaxing the gate silently breaks the composer for any capital `T`, and exit condition 3 exists to catch exactly that. Ordered guard-first (WP-1): `documented_keys_resolve_in_the_active_map` currently asserts only that a key resolves to *something*, which is how the stale `Ctrl+T` → "Toggle sidebar" Normal row survived; strengthening it to a label↔action comparison before any help text is edited turns four reported-invisible features into a measured drift count. Also resolves **D-C7** (`todo_expanded` retire or keep — recommend retire; `todo_strip_height` already returns 1 whenever todos exist, so the one-line summary is unaffected), documents the Editor-route `Ctrl+T`/`Ctrl+S` overrides per **D-C8**, and purges `help_overlay.rs`, which is undeclared in `components/mod.rs`, compiled nowhere, and whose tests never run. Two user-visible behavior changes are accepted and must be stated in closure: `Shift+T` is gone, and `Ctrl+T` no longer expands the todo list in place. Stop conditions include any attempt to bind `Ctrl+S`/`Ctrl+J`/`Shift+W`, and moving the Editor's `Ctrl+T` file-tree toggle (belongs to the editor roadmap). |
| TUI subagent and job visibility convergence (corrective) | C002 `/sessions` gains Subagents + Jobs sections | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/002-sessions-dialog-pending-sections.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/002-status.md` | Depends on: C001 (closed). Class **capability** K2, reversing D-C4. `Dialog::Session` is a flat session list today (`components/dialogs/session.rs:79-97`, `selected: usize` at `:83`, `selected_session()` at `:205`); `/sessions` is the surface the roadmap called "the sessions panel", so its closure claim read to a user as "`/sessions` has subagents" when the sections had in fact landed in the *side panel* titled `SESSIONS` (`SidePanelId::Session`). Sections read the **same** derived row sources the overlay and sidebar read — one derivation rendered in more places, not a fourth projection — with jobs filtered by `state::tasks::is_live_state` and keyed by `job_id`, never by index. The cursor becomes row-type aware: sessions lead so `selected_session()` keeps working, headers are not selectable, and `Enter` on a subagent opens the same tab as the sidebar. A job row consumes `Enter` rather than inventing a destination. The highest-risk detail is the **two instances**: `open_dialog` pushes a *clone* onto the focus manager and `render_dialog` paints that clone, so a fill touching only `dialog_state.session_dialog` passes every App-owned test and shows nothing — the exact bug recorded at `commands/sessions.rs:2670-2677`, whose test is the pattern to copy. |

Measured at this baseline: 46 pool entries, longest `profile` 223 B, longest combined 291 B, `MAX_PROJECTION_SUBAGENTS` 16 → **≈4.5 KB** worst-case snapshot growth, so the milestone must name its own per-descriptor cap rather than reuse `MAX_PROJECTION_STRING_BYTES` (4,096). **Two traps recorded in the plan:** (a) the addendum's "keep desktop bridge field parity" is *satisfiable by doing nothing* — `SubagentSummaryView` already omits `display_name`/`run_id` and `check-bindings.mjs` only asserts fields it lists — so leaving it alone leaves the guard vacuous for this milestone and the decision must be written down; (b) the `names.toml:13-16` "Not rendered by the TUI today" comments are emitted by `generate_subagent_names.py:132,134`, so `--check` passes on two identical lies and correcting them requires editing the generator.
