| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M001 key dispatch authority and binding reachability | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/001-key-dispatch-authority-and-reachability.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/001-status.md` | Resolved as **shape (b)**: redo is `U` (Helix's own chord; Helix binds `Ctrl+r` in *Insert* mode only), and `Ctrl+r` keeps exactly one owner — resync — in both modes, so no chord was invented and `RequestSave`/`RequestResync` never lost a path. Shape (a) was rejected because it would require a new Normal-mode resync chord and would change a working binding. Also closed a second instance of the same class: the `\| (m, Char('d'))` alternative in the `D` arm, which could never match because bare `d` is claimed earlier. New `scripts/check_tui_editor_key_authority.py` (wired into `verify.sh quick` and the CI `verify` job) attributes every chord to its dispatch stage and fails when a mode table re-declares a chord an earlier stage owns or when one table declares a chord twice; an unresolvable arm shape is exit 2, never a pass. Eight new dispatch-path tests drive every Normal-mode arm through `editor_buffer_key` and assert both the reported `BufferKey` and the observable change. **Non-vacuity was demonstrated, and it caught two blind spots in the guard's first draft** (an `ALT_RE` that could not parse `KeyModifiers::CONTROL`, so every control-chord arm was invisible; and an unparseable `Char` arm skipped instead of erroring). Work package D **added** code: `builtin_shift_letters_are_composed_text_in_insert_mode` in `src/tui/input.rs` covers the global keymap only, so the editor route needed its own Insert-mode sweep (91 assertions, letters in three encodings plus punctuation). **Correction:** the earlier claim that the help overlay advertised both `Ctrl+R` and `Ctrl+R (redo)` is true of `src/tui/components/help_overlay.rs`, which is **unreferenced dead code** — absent from `components/mod.rs`, never compiled, never rendered. Edits there were reverted rather than cited; the shipped `/help` (`dialogs::help::HelpDialog`) lists no editor chord at all. Verified locally: `verify.sh quick`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `tui::commands::editor` 53 / `tui::input` 49 / `tui_main_buffer` 44 / `tui_render` 111, all passing. No hosted CI run claimed. **M002 is unblocked.** |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M002 unified prefix state machine | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/002-unified-prefix-state-machine.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/002-status.md` | Class **infrastructure**. Both legacy mechanisms **deleted**, not bypassed: `pending_command: String`, `yank_prefix_pending: bool`, plus the two helpers that served them (`push_pending`, `bounded_pending`) and the renderer's byte-truncation of the pending label. Replaced by one `TuiDocumentPresentation::pending_prefix: EditorPrefix` with `arm_prefix`/`disarm_prefix` and one resolver, `resolve_pending_prefix`. **One deviation from the plan's §6, recorded rather than followed:** §6 said an unmatched key disarms and is then resolved normally, which describes `space y` but not `gg` — the baseline's `gg` *consumed* it. Applying §6 uniformly would have made `g j` move the cursor, so the policy is carried per-prefix by `EditorPrefix::swallows_unmatched` and acceptance criterion 2 still holds. **`gg` and `space y` tests passed unmodified** (the `pending()` helper reports `display()` so the four `"g"`/`""` assertions needed no rewrite) — the strongest available evidence the gestures did not change. `[`/`]` armed with no leaves (M006); a second key disarms and resolves normally, proven by a test that asserts the cursor *moved*, not just that the key was returned. New `scripts/check_tui_editor_prefix_state.py` (in `verify.sh quick` + CI `verify` job) fails on any deleted name under `src/`, on a missing replacement declaration, and — what a fixed name list cannot express — on `TuiDocumentPresentation` declaring any second prefix field. **Fix found while migrating:** reconciliation disarmed `pending_command` via `clear_history` but never the `space` flag, so a `space` typed before a reload stayed armed and yanked in the reloaded buffer; all four disarm points now cover both. **Visible change:** `space` previously had no indicator at all (a `bool` with nowhere to render to), so every pending prefix now renders a distinct label — `g` is byte-for-byte unchanged. Terminal panel keeps its own `prefix_pending`; convergence recorded as deferred, terminal non-regression proven. **Unblock audit:** M003, M006, M011 and M012 have their M002 edge discharged; M004, M005 and M010 remain gated on M003/M005. **M003 is next and alone.** |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M003 character-finding motions | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/003-character-finding-motions.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/003-status.md` | Class **capability**. `f`/`F`/`t`/`T` search the whole document rather than the cursor's line (Helix's documented difference from vi) and `Alt`-`.` repeats the last find. **Built as M002 grammar extensions, not a new mechanism:** the four keys are `EditorPrefix` variants whose next key is *data* — a target character — rather than a leaf glyph, so `EditorPrefix::leaf` is `None` for all of them and a separate `find_target_char` does the capture. No presentation field was added for the target. **Three capture rules, each with a test:** a non-character key is *retried* not swallowed (`f` then `Left` moves left, rather than eating an arrow that would otherwise reach the composer); `:` and `/` outrank a pending target and open their modal lines; Insert mode never captures, leaving the armed prefix for Normal mode. **The scan is bounded and discloses.** `MAX_EDITOR_FIND_BYTES` is `MAX_EDITOR_SEARCH_BYTES` measured *from the cursor*, and `FindOutcome` keeps `Absent` (whole direction scanned) distinct from `Bounded` (window ran out) — the status row renders `no 'z' in that direction` versus `no 'z' within the 2 MiB scan bound`, because a motion that stops and a character that is absent otherwise look identical. **Real defect found and fixed:** the first `find_char` walked the document one byte at a time via `byte_at`, which is `read_bytes(offset, offset+1)` — and `read_bytes` refuses a range that is not boundary-aligned on both ends, so the leading byte of every multi-byte character was *unreadable* and the scan stepped past every multi-byte target. It passed on ASCII and failed silently on any document containing non-ASCII. Now chunked with a `len_utf8()-1` overlap: 79 s → 0.22 s on the bound test, measured. **Honest gap:** per the plan's AC2, `t`/`T` land *on* the target, which at this milestone makes them cursor-equivalent to `f`/`F` — the real Helix distinction is a selection property (`find_till` places the head past the match so the selection covers it) and belongs with M007. `docs/tui.md` says `as f` rather than implying otherwise. **One existing test changed:** M001's `modal_lines_open_and_unclaimed_keys_reach_the_composer` asserted `f`/`t`/`F`/`T` were unclaimed; M003 claims all four, so they left that list with a comment saying so. No `gg`/`space y` assertion was touched. **Non-vacuity:** six injections (line-confined scan, before-target `t` landing, `:`/`/` as target, swallowed non-target key, dropped disclosure, undisarmed repeat slot), each reverted hash-identically. The harness itself was corrected first: it scored an injection as non-vacuous on a non-zero exit that was actually a *compile* error, proving nothing — it now requires a named test to report `FAILED`. No new guard; `check_tui_editor_key_authority.py` needed no change and now reports 37 Normal-mode chords. **Unblock audit:** M004's hard edge is discharged and M005 is dependency-clean; M010 still waits on M005, so the plan's §15 prediction that they would all become ready together did not match the roadmap graph. **Tree note:** the unrelated terminal-panel work restored into this tree at the end of M002 has 2 failing tests and 1 clippy error, which blocks `verify.sh full` for reasons unrelated to M003; `verify.sh quick` passes. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M006 unimpaired navigation, non-LSP/non-TS subset | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/006-unimpaired-navigation-non-ts-subset.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/006-status.md` | Depends on: M002 closed (hard, for the armed `[`/`]` prefixes and M005's leaf table), M003 closed (`Alt-.`). Class: capability. **Scope is smaller than the roadmap claimed, for two recorded reasons.** (1) The `]d`/`[d`/`]D`/`[D` quartet is **M013's** — the roadmap already claimed those bindings twice (M013's boundary names "]`/`[` diagnostic navigation"), and the read path they need is broken until M013 repairs it. (2) The `]g`/`[g`/`]G`/`[G` change quartet has **no reachable data source**: `crates/egggit/src/diff.rs` exposes only `ChangedFile { path, kind }` and `FileDiff { path, patch }`, neither carrying line ranges; the only parsed hunk type, `DiffHunk` (`src/tui/components/diff.rs:27`), is produced by the **private** `DiffViewer::compute_diff`; and `handle_normal_key` receives an `EditorState` with no git handle. The roadmap's "reuse the existing diff surface" is **false**; that slice is registered as deferred product work and needs its own milestone. **Third correction:** the roadmap read `]D`/`[D` and `]G`/`[G` as "first/last"; the live keymap has `]D` = `goto_last_diag`, `[D` = `goto_first_diag`, `]G` = `goto_last_change`, `[G` = `goto_first_change`. `]` is *forward* throughout this layer, so the roadmap's pairs were backwards. Delivers `]p`/`[p` paragraph, `]Space`/`[Space` add-newline, the `[`/`]` grammar rows, and `Alt-.` widened to repeat unimpaired **motions** (not edits). Requires `PrefixAction::Edit`, because the current two-variant enum can carry a motion and a yank but not a mutation, and folding a mutation into `Motion` would route an edit past the `editable` gate. Also closes a gap: **no test asserts `[`/`]` stay literal in Insert** today. **M006 is now closed** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/006-status.md`, 2026-10-19): all four chords are bound and the `[`/`]` prefixes finally have leaf rows, so M002's "armed but inert" odd case is gone. The planned `PrefixAction::Edit` arm is real and necessary — `]Space` inserts a newline, and `editor_motion` is the one function in the dispatch that promises never to touch text, so a leaf now says whether it moves or edits and `]Space` lands in the same read-only gate, undo entry and document transaction as `x`/`d`/`y`. Helix's `add_newline_below` was read from source rather than assumed: it moves **no** selection, so `]Space` leaves the cursor where it was, and it is not `open_below` (`o`) — `o` also takes the keyboard. A paragraph scan turned out to need **three** answers, not two: `Landed`, `NoParagraph`, and `Bounded` for a walk that ran out of budget, with `MAX_PARAGRAPH_SCAN_BYTES` (the frame's own 256 KiB) as the whole-walk cap — the per-line 32 KiB cap alone still leaves a 200 MB scan on a 200 MB file with no blank lines. A line the scan cannot read all of is content, never a separator. **Deliberate divergence from Helix, recorded not matched:** with no blank line anywhere Helix's `move_next_paragraph` lands on end-of-buffer (`test_behaviour_when_moving_to_next_paragraph_single`, `helix-core/src/movement.rs`), and CodeGG reports "no next paragraph" instead — an EOF jump looks like the key worked. **A pre-existing `Alt-.` defect was found and fixed:** a find returned early from `note_motion` without clearing the other repeat slot, so `]p`, `f x`, `Alt-.` repeated the *older* `]p` backwards; every recorder now closes the other two doors. **Found, recorded, not fixed:** `o`/`O` never insert a line — `enter_insert` maps `OpenLineBelow` to the cursor, so `resolve_edit`'s open-line arms are reachable only from their own unit test. The terminal panel is **explicitly exempt** and `src/tui/interactive_terminal.rs` is unmodified. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M007 multi-selection core | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/007-multi-selection-core.md` | Depends on: M001 closed (hard, for the key-authority guard). Independent of M002, so it may run in parallel. Class: capability (enabling). **Longest pole, but smaller than it looks.** `TextTransaction.edits` is already a `Vec<TextEdit>`, `invert()` (`src/tui/editor.rs:1854-1871`) already walks every edit with a shift offset, and `apply_local` is transaction-level — so "one transaction, not a partial multi-cursor edit" needs **no controller work**. The load-bearing design choice is to keep `selection()` / `is_selected()` / `span()` / `line_range()` **as primary-scoped rather than replaced**: the `SelectionState` forms have **no production callers at all**, and the panel's two production uses of the *inherent* `Selection::line_range()` (`src/tui/interactive_terminal.rs:766`, `:1932`) both reach it through `selection()`, so the panel's ~25 call sites and the one `restore` caller keep compiling and **terminal-panel parity is preserved by construction** instead of by exemption. **Correction:** the roadmap named `D` as a selection-dependent effect. It is not — `resolve_edit` (`src/tui/editor.rs:2525-2614`) never calls `selection_range`, and `D` resolves against `cursor_byte` and `line` only. Making it selection-aware would be a *new semantic on a local extension*, which M004 required to stay unchanged; `D` stays cursor-only and the set-scoped effects are `d`, `y`/`space y`, and M004's six verbs. **Also restated:** "N cursors deletable in one gesture" is not available here, because no verb *creates* N cursors until M008 (`C`/`Alt-C`); M007 proves the model. **Real gap it creates work for:** `apply_helix` (`src/tui/commands/editor.rs:3275-3283`) and `helix_motion` have **no `editable` gate**, so `v`/`x`/growth arm selections on a read-only buffer today with no notice; the fix is narrow — refuse **growth**, allow plain cursor movement. §5 of the plan froze the set API **M008 was written against**, and it shipped as pinned. **Closed 2026-10-18:** `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/007-status.md`; `cargo test -p codegg --lib tui::` 2060 passed / 0 failed, all 32 `helix_selection` tests green (19 pre-existing **unmodified**), clippy `-D warnings` clean, `verify.sh quick` passed, three editor guards exit 0. `verify.sh full` does **not** go green here — its guards, fmt, clippy and check stages pass, but the workspace test stage fails outside `tui::` in `lsp`, `tool_execution`, `workspace_isolation`, `session_family` and `tool_surface_minimization`. `core::daemon::tests::session_create_through_daemon` is **proven pre-existing** by re-running it at baseline `84440fe7f` with the five changed `.rs` files stashed (`InvalidRootPath("/tmp")`); the rest are environment-dependent by inspection only and were not each re-run at baseline. Every test this milestone touches is green: 2060 `tui::` unit tests, 403 TUI integration tests. **The worktree therefore has no green full-sweep gate today, which M008 inherits.** Also found and fixed: a pre-existing defect where a character selection reaching the last byte of a document resolved to an empty range (`next_char_boundary` is `None` at EOF, and the fallback dropped the head character), so `d` on the final character deleted nothing. **M008 is unblocked.** |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M008 multi-selection command surface | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/008-multi-selection-command-surface.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/008-status.md` | Depends on: M007 **closed 2026-10-18** — `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/007-status.md`; its §5 set API contract shipped exactly as pinned, so this plan needs no corrective pass. M009 also waits on this. Class: capability. Eighteen verbs, all quoted from the live keymap's Normal mode → Selection manipulation table rather than from the roadmap prose (the M004 `R` rule). **Two findings change the sizing estimate.** (1) **No chord collides** — the guard block (`src/tui/commands/editor.rs:2795`) claims exactly two chords and `handle_normal_key` 48, and none of the eighteen is claimed; bare `c`, `s`, `C`, `S`, `K`, `;`, `,`, `%`, `&`, `(`, `)` and every `ALT` variant are free, so no existing binding moves. (2) **`S`/`K`/`Alt-K` need no new dependency** — `regex = "1"` is already in the root package (`Cargo.toml:213`) and already used under `src/tui/`; the real cost is the **argument prompt**, which the plan answers by reusing the `:` command line rather than building a new input mode (which would also risk the one-prefix-field invariant in `check_tui_editor_prefix_state.py`). `&` must build its own alignment logic — there is **no padding primitive in the repo**, only width measurement (`bounded_display_width`), and `EditorPoint` orders by `byte`, so alignment is by **display width**, a decision to make explicitly. `%` is O(1): `line_start(0)..buffer_content_end` are offset lookups, not scans. **Unverified item, recorded as a stop condition:** whether a terminal delivers `Alt-(` as `Char` + `ALT` at all — terminal-dependent, with a related legacy-encoding footgun already recorded at `tests/modifier_chords.rs:42-52`. **Seven real Helix verbs sit outside the boundary** (`s`, `Alt-s`, `_`, `X`/`Alt-x`, `J`/`Alt-J`, `Ctrl-c`) and are enumerated with reasons in the plan §7; `Alt-s` is cheaper than `S` and is the natural next slice. **Closed 2026-10-19.** All eighteen verbs bound; key authority 48 → **66** Normal-mode chords, the count the plan pinned. The `:`-reuse decision held: `check_tui_editor_prefix_state.py` emits byte-identical output before and after, so §6's claim is verified rather than argued. **Two real defects found by this milestone's own tests and fixed** — `K` with no matches refused instead of emptying the set (plan §13.11 required empty), and `C` at end-of-buffer returned `Handled` while adding a cursor on the member's own byte, because `len_lines()` counts a trailing line no byte offset resolves into; the walk is now bounded by the last line a cursor can reach, with an explicit self-byte guard. **The plan's §12 prediction was right and its recorded exemption is `C`:** the terminal panel's own Normal-mode table claims `C`/`c` for `TerminalNavAction::Clear` while the editor uses `C` to add a cursor — different subjects, so neither surface could choose otherwise. The other seventeen are unclaimed by the panel and swallowed by it. **The guard needed teaching, not skipping:** `ALT_RE` matched a `Char(...)` payload with `[^()]*`, which cannot spell `Char('(')`; it now matches the glyph as a quoted literal, and non-vacuity was proven twice — a shadow on a paren glyph and a shadow on an ordinary glyph both exit 1, each reverted byte-identically. **Unblock audit:** M009's M008 edge is discharged; M009 remains blocked on ADR-0034 acceptance alone, and the closure records that a milestone must not discharge a blocker it did not touch. `verify.sh quick` is green; `verify.sh full` did not go green for reasons outside `tui::`, inherited from M007. Nothing committed. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | merge reconciliation with `main` (supplemental record, no new milestone) | **closed** | no implementation plan of its own; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/021-merge-reconciliation-status.md` | `main` was reconciled twice — 113 commits at `fe2c1306`, then seven more at `6c348f03` (PR #134, the permission temp-root redirect, and PR #136) — and touched **zero** editor-owned files, so the editor's key authority, prefix grammar, undo model and guard set are unchanged by the merge. The first merge's four conflicts were resolved by union with the side that owns the milestone; the second had none. The reconciliation fixed six defects: three documentation ones (sixteen of the twenty-four rows of `architecture/overview.md`'s verified-counts table were stale in the merged tree, with four of the methods pinned against the commits that originally recorded each value and the drift attributed between the two sides; the registry's editor rows had to agree with the roadmap's own status line after `main` still listed M004–M012 as eligible; and the TUI skill's guard table had outgrown its own "Both guards" sentence and was missing two guards that were on disk), and three in this branch's own code — the M013 LSP read-delivery test target was missing the `required-features` gate its three sibling fake-LSP tests carry, so a feature-less CI sweep built it and ran it against a binary `required-features` had left unbuilt; M011's change to the completion popup's `Enter` contract left five of `main`'s integration tests asserting the old contract, which survived because the branch's sweeps ran the library unit tests and never the `tests/` integration suite; and the branch's own `egglsp` code-action preview had no gate at all — neither the manifest declaration nor the in-file `#![cfg(feature = …)]` its directory-mate uses — which survived even a feature-less local sweep because a stale feature-enabled test-server binary was still on disk. The Desktop E2E's `permission-list` timeout is recorded in the closure and is attributed to `main`'s PR #134 — the temp-root redirect, whose commit post-dates every green Desktop E2E run and which the E2E workflow's path gate did not watch; this branch changes nothing under `apps/`. M014 remains blocked on ADR-0034 acceptance; this record opens no milestone and discharges none. |
| TUI composer Helix keybinding parity | M001 composer keyboard ownership and cursor presence | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/001-composer-keyboard-ownership-and-cursor-presence.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/001-status.md` | Depends on: nothing. Class **capability** (roadmap C6) with **infrastructure** (N4, the keyboard-ownership derivation). **No ADR required** — this milestone introduces no key claim, so it is independent of ADR-0042 and landed first on purpose. It closed the two facts the rest of the workstream stands on: (1) a **derived** "does the composer hold the keyboard" fact, placed beside the existing `App::composer_input_mode()`, and (2) a caret-cell computation shared by the paint and the terminal-cursor positioning, so the two cannot disagree about where the caret is. **The dead `focused` flag is gone, not rewired:** `PromptWidget::blur()`'s only caller in the tree was a test, so the flag was dead in production and "no cursor when another surface holds the keyboard" was unimplementable as written; `PromptWidget::focused`/`focus()`/`blur()`/`is_focused()` and the equally vestigial `TuiMsg::FocusPrompt` (whose only effect was setting that flag to the value it already had) are removed, and `ComposerCursor` (`None`/`Block`) replaces them. The ownership fact is `App::composer_holds_keyboard`, whose arms mirror `App::on_key`'s priority chain — a mounted modal, a focused side panel (`App::panel_owns_keyboard`, **extracted** from the inline `panel_active` condition so the key chain and the caret rules read one derivation), the timeline overlay, and the editor's buffer when it is explicitly focused. The blink comes from the terminal, not the app: ratatui 0.30.2 hides the terminal cursor whenever the frame did not position it, and the app positions it **nowhere** today, so Insert mode gets a natively blinking cursor with **no new timer, poll interval, or animation cadence** — the single-cadence rule in `AGENTS.md` is the constraint that makes this the only acceptable implementation, and the milestone's stop conditions forbid an app-driven blink. Normal mode paints a solid block instead; the fake block and the real terminal cursor are **mutually exclusive** and are asserted so. The cursor is gated on the **resolved** mode, never the raw `UiState::input_mode` field, which is forced to `Normal` whenever a side panel takes focus — gating on the raw field would show a composer cursor while the user types into a panel. The blink rate is the terminal's, so a non-blinking terminal shows a steady Insert cursor; the Insert/Normal distinction is enforced by **shape**, not by blink. The editor's and the terminal panel's cursors are untouched. New `tests/tui_composer_cursor.rs` (24 tests) drives the app's real render path through a recording backend that captures the terminal cursor calls ratatui's `TestBackend` keeps private, and covers the caret cell, the ownership derivation, the paint rules, the terminal-cursor positioning, the block/cursor exclusivity, a resize, and the badge/caret agreement. **M002 is unblocked**: ADR-0042 was accepted 2026-10-19 (help keeps `space`; the copy verb is `y` in the help box that chord opens) and M001 is closed. |
| TUI composer Helix keybinding parity | M002 normal-mode key ownership contract | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/002-normal-mode-key-ownership-contract.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/002-status.md` | Depends on: nothing outstanding. ADR-0042 was **accepted 2026-10-19** with the `space`-chord amendment — help keeps `space` in both maps, and the Helix copy gesture is preserved as the **second step** of that chord (`space` opens the help menu; `y` in the menu it opened copies the composer's selection; the `/help` command's box does not carry the verb, and the composer's claim set contains neither `space` nor `y`). M001 is **closed** (`plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/001-status.md`), which discharged the second dependency: the claim table needs M001's `App::composer_holds_keyboard` derivation to tell "the composer is not holding the keyboard" apart from "the composer leaked the key". Class **invariant** (one owner per chord, no fallthrough from a claimed key) with **infrastructure** (the composer key-ownership guard). The rule adopted is the terminal panel's: **a claimed key is consumed even when the verb is a no-op**, so it can never leak to the app action — the editor's `check_tui_editor_key_authority.py` targets only the editor's command module, so M002 adds a **composer-scoped** guard with a `--self-test` that injects each violation class (the three editor TUI guards predate that convention; `check_tui_subagent_name_pool_isolation.py` is the precedent). The re-spellings are a **deliberate user-visible break** — stop/resume, open-diff, and focus-prompt move to non-letter chords in the main buffer, and the capabilities are **not** removed; help is **not** re-spelled, which removes the sharpest instance of that break. A claimed **capital** letter must accept **both** key encodings (`SHIFT` and `NONE`), because some terminals report a shifted key without the `SHIFT` modifier — the app's existing convention, and the reason `U` for redo is reachable on both. That matters because a bare capital letter bound under the unshifted encoding once **swallowed typed text** in Insert mode (typing it opened a panel); the fix was made at the key in the Insert-mode text-key guard, and `builtin_shift_letters_are_composed_text_in_insert_mode` sweeps both keymaps and asserts non-vacuity — the model for the composer's claim-set sweep. The milestone's stop conditions forbid satisfying the help audit by duplicating the claim table into a binding map, which would create a second source of truth for key ownership. **Delivered:** `src/tui/composer_claims.rs` is the single table — 13 letters (`b d e i j k o p u U v w x`), each with one owner, an explicit condition, an explicit encoding set and an explicit status; the claim step runs in `App::on_key` **ahead of** the binding map, gated on the *resolved* mode and `App::composer_holds_keyboard`, so a reserved verb is consumed and does nothing and a claimed letter can never become an unrelated app action. The three re-spellings are `Ctrl+Alt+X` (stop/resume), `Ctrl+Alt+D` (open-diff) and `Ctrl+Alt+E` (focus-prompt), in **both** maps; `Ctrl+Alt+I` is not deliverable because crossterm reports `ESC` + `0x09` as `Tab`+ALT, and the default map's bare `/` for focus-prompt **stays** — `/` is not a claimed letter, so it never conflicted with the claim table, and the plan's invariant ("a letter the composer does not claim must resolve to the same app action it resolves to today") keeps it resolving to `FocusPrompt`; it is also the gesture that enters command mode, which is what makes the rest of a typed slash command land, so removing it broke the only route back into the panel manager from a focused panel. `scripts/check_tui_composer_key_authority.py` (in `verify.sh quick` + CI `verify` job) fails on a non-closed table, a claimed key that can still reach an app action, a re-spelled action missing from a map or without a chord to reach it by, a re-spelled action that still answers to a claimed key, a capital claim missing an encoding, and three more classes; its `--self-test` injects each class. **One scoping deviation, recorded not improvised:** the plan put "no insert transition" out of scope, but `i` is the app's **only** route back to Insert mode — it is the `None` arm of the key chain, not a binding — so a reserved `i` would have consumed the key, done nothing, and left the user in Normal mode with no way to type. The `i` verb is therefore **live**, and it runs the app's **existing** transition, moved into the table so the table is the one place that says who owns the key; M006 still owns the insert transitions beyond `i`. The closure does **not** present the twelve reserved verbs as shipped capabilities. |
| TUI composer Helix keybinding parity | M003 composer text model (selection, undo/redo, shared geometry) | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/003-composer-text-model.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/003-status.md` | Depends on: nothing outstanding (soft on M001). Class **infrastructure** — the composer's model, the shared word geometry, and the shared selection rules run against the composer's point type. Gave the composer a text model (`src/tui/composer_text.rs`: a point type satisfying the shared selection contract, the shared machine's selection state, and a history bounded in **both** depth and bytes), moved the word geometry into one module (`src/tui/text_geometry.rs`) with the editor migrated onto it, and made `helix_selection.rs`'s rules generic so they now run against the composer's point as well as the terminal's. No key handling and no rendering change: the claim table is untouched and the widget's public API is intact. M004 (composer motions) is now dependency-ready. |
| TUI composer Helix keybinding parity | M004 composer motions (`w`/`b`/`e` word, `j`/`k` vertical) | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/004-composer-motions.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/004-status.md` | Depends on: **M002 — closed** (the claim table the motions are reached through) and **M003 — closed** (`plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/003-status.md`, the geometry and the caret model). Class **capability** (roadmap C1). **The key set is the five letters ADR-0042 already gave the composer** — `w`, `b`, `e`, `j`, `k` — and the claim set is **fixed** (roadmap §3), so this milestone added no row and claimed no key: it flipped five `Reserved` statuses to `Live` and implemented five verbs. `h`/`l` are **not** in it: ADR-0042 part 4 records them as no conflict, they are bound in both maps since C001, and they reach the caret through the app-level `Left`/`Right` actions, which the composer keeps. The targets were built in M003 and are **already computed** — `ComposerText::word_forward`/`word_backward`/`word_end` (`src/tui/composer_text.rs:444-456`) and `text_geometry::move_vertical` (`src/tui/text_geometry.rs:262`) — so what was missing was only the step that moves the caret, and it moves it through `move_caret` (the shared machine's `move_to`), **not** `set_cursor`, because "moving the cursor extends a held character selection" is the rule M005 depends on. **The `j`/`k` conditional is load-bearing and preserved:** `ClaimCondition::MultiLineBuffer` (`src/tui/composer_claims.rs:463`, `:323`) means both keys still fall through to transcript scroll on a single-line buffer, which ADR-0042 part 4 requires. **The roadmap's exit condition demands a dispatch-reachability test, not a helper call:** `tests/tui_composer_claims.rs` already has the machinery (`test_app`, `press` → `App::on_key`, `normal_mode`, `prompt_text`, at `:41-64`), so each motion is proved by pressing the key and asserting the caret's new position. The caret's visibility is the render layer's job (`src/tui/app/render.rs:2067-2069` calls `ensure_cursor_visible_with_width`), so this milestone touches no scroll code and no rendering. **Line start/end is deliberately out of scope** and is recorded as decision **D7**: the claim set is fixed, and the two available routes — a new `0`/`$` claim, or re-pointing the app-level `Home`/`End` from the buffer's start/end to the line's — each change an ownership boundary or a key's existing behaviour, which ADR-0042 and M004's own exit condition forbid. |
| TUI composer Helix keybinding parity | M005 composer selection and clipboard verbs | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/005-composer-selection-and-clipboard.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/005-status.md` | Depends on: **M004 — closed** (`plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/004-status.md`, the motions the selection grows with) and **M003 — closed** (`plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/003-status.md`, the selection machine and the model). Class **capability** (roadmap C2, C3). The verbs are `v` (select chars), `x` (select line), `d` (delete selection) and `p` (paste) — four of the thirteen letters ADR-0042 already gave the composer, flipped `Reserved` → `Live`; **`y` is deliberately not among them**, because ADR-0042 part 7 gives the copy gesture to the help box the `space` chord opens, and "one gesture, one owner" is the reason. **The decisive finding behind that decision:** the help box's *mode* cannot decide whether `y` is a verb. A `/help` box starts in Insert but `Esc` moves it to Normal, where a mode-only rule would make `y` copy in a box that must not copy; and a `space`-opened box moves to Insert with `Enter`, where `y` must stay a filter character. So the **opening route is carried in the open request** — `Dialog::Help(HelpRoute)` — and a call site cannot open the box without saying which help box it is; the default route is the one that cannot copy. **The copy cannot be done inside the dialog:** `open_dialog` pushes a *clone* onto the focus manager (`src/tui/app/modal.rs:323-325`) and the clone receives the keys, so the verb **returns a `TuiMsg`** the app handles — the pattern `q` already uses. **The selection has one byte range:** delete, the copy and the renderer all read `ComposerText::selection_range`, which is the editor's range term for term (char mode inclusive of the head character, line mode whole lines including terminators). The rendering is **new work**: `PromptWidget` drew each line as one span before, and the theme already had `selection` / `selection_dim` / `selection_text` (`src/tui/theme.rs:57-62`). The clipboard has both directions (`src/util/clipboard.rs:4`, `:19`), behind the default-on `arboard` feature, so a build without it makes the paste inert — which is why both clipboard verbs report their outcome rather than failing silently. Open decision **D5** (paste scope) is **discharged** by this milestone: the composer gained a paste verb alone, the editor has its own paste milestone in its own roadmap, and the terminal panel was left alone. |
| TUI composer Helix keybinding parity | M006 composer undo, redo, and insert transitions | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-composer-helix-parity/006-composer-undo-redo-and-insert-transitions.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/006-status.md` | Depends on: **M005 — closed** (`plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/005-status.md`, the selection model the selection rule extends) and **M003 — closed** (`plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/003-status.md`, the bounded history the undo verbs traverse). Class **capability** (roadmap C4, C5). The deliverable is undo (`u`), redo (`U`), insert-after (`a`) and open-line-below (`o`), plus the mode transition each causes; `i` is already live from M002 and does not move the caret, and this milestone must not change it. **Open decisions D4 and D6 are owned here** and are settled in the plan: **D4** (the undo scope) resolves to *the composer's own text*, because the composer has exactly one buffer — a slash command and a staged shell projection are typed into that same buffer, and the two wholesale-replacement paths (`prompt.clear()` on submit at `src/tui/app/prompt_turn.rs:320`, `prompt.set_text(include_text)` for a staged projection at `src/tui/commands/shell.rs:655`) both drop the history, so the wider state is outside the undo scope by construction; **D6** (the `a` conflict) resolves to *delete one binding*, not invent a chord: the vim map bound bare `a` to `FocusSidebar` (measured at the baseline `3cad45929`, `src/tui/input.rs:1199-1202`; the binding is **deleted** by this milestone), but `FocusSidebar` was **already** bound to `Ctrl+Alt+S` in **both** maps (`src/tui/input.rs:940-946` default, `:1219-1225` vim, at that baseline; now `:971-977` default and `:1249-1255` vim), so the bare letter was an extra spelling of an action that already had one and no capability was lost by removing it. ADR-0042 part 4's conflict table does not list `a`; that is a **gap in an accepted ADR**, and since an accepted ADR is immutable the resolution is recorded in the roadmap's D6 row and in the closure record, **not** by editing the ADR. The claim table grows from **13 rows to 14** — `a` is one of the letters roadmap §3 names, so this completes the fixed set rather than extending it. **Two defects in the existing model are in scope, because this is the milestone that makes undo reachable at all:** `UndoHistory::evict` (`src/tui/composer_text.rs:219-249`) removes the **oldest** entry while either bound is exceeded, so a single edit larger than `MAX_UNDO_BYTES` (a paste) is evicted immediately and becomes **not undoable** — the roadmap's exit condition requires the bound to be enforced "without losing the ability to undo the most recent edit", and the fix is that eviction must never remove the newest entry (the byte bound may then be exceeded by that one edit, which is the stated trade-off); and `ComposerText::replace_range` (`:615-627`) leaves a held selection in place across a length-changing edit, whose byte coordinates then describe the pre-edit text, so the milestone introduces one rule — **an edit that changes the buffer's length drops the held selection** — enforced in `replace_range` and in `undo`/`redo`, the same discipline `delete_selection` already follows (`:574`). The caret rules live in the **model** (`ComposerText::insert_after`, `ComposerText::open_line_below`), not in the app, and `a` moves the caret through `move_caret` (the shared machine's `move_to`), **not** `set_caret`, because the deliberate-movement path is what extends a held selection. `o` is an ordinary undoable edit; `a` is a movement and is not recorded. The help row that advertises bare `a` as "Focus sidebar" (`src/tui/input.rs:1630-1635` at the baseline `3cad45929`; the row is **deleted** by this milestone) becomes **false** and is removed; the `Ctrl+Alt` chord comment above the vim map's binding (`:1216-1218` at that baseline, unchanged since) is **left alone** — it reads oddly next to the house label `Ctrl+Alt+S`, but it is accurate (the chord family is deliberately `Ctrl+Alt+<lower>`, because the terminal delivers the unshifted letter with `CONTROL\|ALT`), and rewording it would touch the chord contract this milestone does not own. The guard gains `"FocusSidebar": "a"` in `RESPELLED_ACTIONS` plus three `--self-test` cases (a claim on `a` with the vim map still binding bare `a`; a `FocusSidebar` spelled as a bare letter; a `FocusSidebar` missing from one map). **D7** (the line start/end motion) stays **open** and is not this milestone's. **Closed.** All four verbs are live and the claim table is **closed**: every letter the composer owns has a behaviour, and the runner's match on the verbs is **exhaustive**, so a verb added to `ComposerVerb` without an arm does not compile — the table/runner disagreement that used to be a runtime warning is now a build error. The eviction never drops the last remaining entry, so an **oversized paste is undoable** (the byte bound used to evict the paste the moment it was made, leaving the history empty). The selection rule — *an edit that changes the buffer's length drops the held selection* — lives in `replace_range`, the one place every edit passes through. `u` and `U` report in **both** branches. The vim map's bare `a` and the help's false `a` row are gone; `FocusSidebar` keeps `Ctrl+Alt+S` in both maps and the guard's `RESPELLED_ACTIONS` now carries it. **D4 and D6 are closed here.** One deviation recorded: the help's composer section is **derived** rather than hand-written, because M007 lands with it — M006's criterion is met as a superset. Closure record: `plans/closure/tui-composer-helix-parity/006-status.md`. |

The deliverable is the composer's hint row (**focus gates the row's presence, the mode changes its text** — the terminal panel's shape at `src/tui/panels/terminal.rs:400-427`, so the transcript does not resize on every `Esc`), the help's composer section **derived** from the claim table rather than hand-written, the three-surface consistency sweep, and two new guard rules: **G10** (the help audit) and **G11** (the modifier-chord audit), each with `--self-test` cases. **Closed.** `composer_verb_entries` (`src/tui/composer_claims.rs`) is a **read-only view** over the claim table, and **three** surfaces read it with no copy of their own: the hint row, the help's composer section, and the guard. The hint row names the composer's **keys** — measured, not preferred: at eighty columns the grouped form runs to ninety-four characters and loses the line motions to the truncation, while the keys fit in fifty-three. The guard's self-test grew from fourteen cases to **twenty-six**. **The sweep's divergence was recorded, not fixed**: the terminal panel's `e`/`b` are a row-scoped reader's motion over a PTY's screen, not a buffer motion, and roadmap §3 protects that surface's keymap. The full table — three agreements, two deliberate divergences, two known differences — is in `architecture/tui.md` and the closure record. **D7 stays open**, with its recommendation unchanged. | TUI panel surface convergence corrective | M002a `InfoDialog` chrome and keymap adoption | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective/002-info-dialog-chrome-and-keymap-adoption.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective/002-status.md` | Approved into planning and split out of the former M002 (§7.1 of the roadmap): one widget with a 16-`DialogType` fan-out is a differently-sized unit from the remaining twelve. Both hard dependencies were closed **and committed** — `file-picker-rework` M001 `b4f4b916f`, panel M001 `18bcfa3d9`. **Implemented.** `InfoDialog` now overrides `render_in_panel` and `keymap_hint`, both delegating to one private `render_body` parameterised by `Chrome::{Owned,Hosted}`, so the wrap width, `visible_lines` and the `scroll` clamp have exactly one implementation. The seam counts move `render_in_panel` 2→3 and `keymap_hint` 1→2. Baseline proof: **8 of the 13 new integration tests fail at `f8286a284`**, verbatim `"hosted ShellShow: host side border broken at row 2"` and `"Context does not advertise the scroll keys it dispatches: \"\""`; the other 5 are recorded as guards, not signals — `modal_rendering_is_unchanged` must be green before *and* after, and the two scroll-parity tests could not fail at baseline because `render_in_panel` *was* `render`. **Three findings changed the design.** (1) `Esc` never reaches a hosted widget — `SidePanelHost::handle_key` claims `Esc`/`q` as the universal exit and blurs before the panel is consulted (`HostPanel` does not override `owns_exit_key`) — and `Enter` reaches it but closes nothing, because docking *pops* the box off the modal stack so `TuiMsg::CloseDialog` runs `close_dialog()` against an empty one. A docked `InfoDialog` therefore has **no in-panel dismiss key**; `Shift+←` is the way back to a modal. Recorded as a host-level design question and **not fixed here**; M002b inherits it for all twelve of its boxes. (2) `set_custom_footer` strings are deliberately **not** replayed into the hint: both reachable footers are modal-path text whose `Esc: cancel` / `Esc close` is false hosted, and `/shell-show`'s additionally advertises `k kill` where `handle_key` scrolls — so all four call sites needed **no edit**. (3) The `scroll` sentinel is **not** written back by the render pass, and must not be: `set_styled_content_preserving_scroll` restores it every refresh. The scroll indicator was **kept** on one reserved row rather than dropped, because `Down`/`j` is unbounded by design and the indicator is the only end-of-content signal. `verify.sh quick` exit 0, `fmt` clean, clippy `-D warnings` clean, 13 + 8 + 52 + 5 + 31 tests pass. `verify.sh full` exits 100 on **pre-existing** failures only, stopping at the same test and message M001 recorded (`session_create_through_daemon`, `InvalidRootPath("/tmp")`). No production file outside `info.rs` changed. **M002b newly eligible.** Class: capability/polish. |
| TUI panel surface convergence corrective | M002b dockable box chrome and keymap adoption | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective/003-dockable-box-chrome-and-keymap-adoption.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective/003-status.md` | Approved into planning with M002a (§7.1). Twelve widgets, each verified `docks_as_viewer() == true` unconditionally with a `DialogType` mapping to a non-bespoke panel: `tree`, `keybind`, `theme`, `task_view`, `review`, `security_review`, `plugin`, `research`, `diff`, `plan`, `mcp`, `panel_picker`. Excluded: `files` (adopted in `file-picker-rework` M001), `chat`/`help`/`agent` (bespoke targets, never rendered through `render_hosted`), and `InfoDialog` (M002a). **Implemented.** All twelve now override `render_in_panel` and `keymap_hint`, each delegating to one private body routine parameterised by `Chrome::{Owned,Hosted}`, so wrapping, row counts, selection windows and scroll clamps have exactly one implementation per widget. Seam counts move to `render_in_panel` 15 / `keymap_hint` 14. Baseline proof: **24 of the 26 new integration tests fail at `f8286a284`**, verbatim `"TreeDialog: the panel title \"Tree ●\" is the host's, not the widget's — row 0 is \"┌ Tree …┐\""` and `"McpDialog/browse: answers no keymap_hint, so the panel host advertises \"j/k scroll · i act\" for a box that dispatches none of it"`; the 2 that passed are recorded as guards, not signals. **The plan's work packages missed one widget:** §3.1 lists twelve but W2–W5 name only eleven, and `mcp` — two bordered panes and two copies of the same false footer — was in no package. Found at implementation and closed rather than shipped half-done. **Four findings changed the design.** (1) **The host answers more keys than M002a found:** besides `Esc`/`q` and the dead `Enter`, `SidePanelHost::handle_key` resolves a panel `InputAction` and routes it to `MoveDirection` *before* the widget is consulted, and `insert_panel_direction_bindings` binds the four arrows plus bare **and** shifted uppercase `H`/`J`/`K`/`L`. Every `↑/↓ navigate` in the twelve footers was therefore dead, and so were `/tasks`' advertised `J/K reorder` and `/security-review`'s `H copy hunk` — the latter two were false even modally. Lowercase `j`/`k` are not panel directions and do arrive, which is why every hint spells them that way; `Home`/`End`/`PageUp`/`PageDown` likewise arrive. (2) **§14 fired for `panel_picker`.** It declares `docks_as_viewer() == true` but `DialogType::PanelPicker` has no `dock_target` arm, so it never docks — meaning its footer is its *only* affordance and retiring it into an unreachable `keymap_hint` would leave the modal with nothing. Recorded answer to §3.5: the gesture is swallowed and refused, harmless for the `/panels` picker, **no `dock_target` arm added**; `keymap_hint` and `render_in_panel` added anyway so the seam is truthful if a mapping ever appears, and the footer kept with a doc-comment saying why. (3) **The §3.3 hazard was real.** `research`'s report view stored a row offset no render pass clamped, so `Home`'s `usize::MAX` sentinel reached ratatui as `u16::MAX` and rendered the report **blank**, unreachable because the offset only grew. The clamp now lives in the shared body routine against the rect actually being filled, in both paths — the one deliberate modal-output change in this milestone, in one state, mandated by the milestone's own structural rule. (4) **Three pre-existing scroll defects were found and deliberately not fixed** as behaviour changes: `review`'s diff view renders from line 0 and ignores its own `diff_scroll` (its scroll is inert), `review`'s and `research`'s `k`/`j` move the wrong way relative to the arrows, and `tree`/`theme` carry a `visible_height` **no production code ever sets** (`set_visible_height` is called only from `tests/tui.rs`), so it stays at 10 however tall the box is and a 12-row modal clips its tail. That last one is pre-existing and left alone deliberately: re-deriving the field from the rect would change modal rendering, and a single field shared by the render pass and the selection clamp is still one source of truth. **No M003 item landed:** `Color::White` counts are unchanged (`plan` 6, `mcp` 2; the only diff lines are indentation moves), `set_theme` signatures are unchanged, no `DialogType`/`SidePanelId`/`config_key`/placement change, no new command, key, gesture, panel, read or daemon call. Ten legacy `impl Widget for &XDialog` bodies (`connect`, `goto`, `theme`, `import`, `plan`, `tree`, `keybind`, `share`, `diff`, `template`) and the public `render(&self, frame, area)` on `diff`/`plan` have **no production caller** — all ten dispatch exclusively through `Component` — and were left for the later de-duplication milestone. (Corrected during M003 planning: this originally read "Five legacy … bodies" and "no caller in `src/` or `tests/`". The count is ten, and `connect` and `share` do have **test-only** callers, in tests whose purpose is comparing the Widget path against the Component path; the other eight have no caller at all.) Verification: `cargo test --test tui_panel_host_chrome` 26/26; `cargo test -p codegg --lib dialogs::` 292/292 (incl. 16 new in-module tests: every-render-path sweeps for `diff` and `plan`, plus modal-still-chrome and zero-interior guards); `verify.sh quick` exit 0; `cargo fmt --check` clean; clippy `-D warnings` clean. Full sweep `cargo nextest run --workspace --locked --profile ci --no-fail-fast` gives **14240 run / 14228 passed / 11 failed / 1 timed out / 6 skipped**, and every non-passing name is on M001's recorded pre-existing list (including the load-sensitive `same_scope_requests_coalesce_to_one_publication` timeout, which recurred here and not in M002a's run — variance, not a fix). No panel, chrome, keymap, chat, placement, diff, review, research, mcp, plan, theme, keybind or task_view test fails. M001's stash-and-run re-derivation of that baseline was not repeated; the attribution rests on the names matching. **M003 was newly eligible but not approved** at this closure, and its theme-cache and colour-literal counts were to be re-derived rather than copied from the roadmap. That has since happened: it is approved into planning at `plans/implementation/tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective/004-theme-sourcing-and-shared-chrome-geometry.md`, where the `Color::White` counts held (`plan` 6, `mcp` 2) but roadmap §4.9's "five boxes" is **ten** and its claim that `mcp` demonstrates the correct pattern is **false** (`mcp` is the worst instance: 44 cache reads, zero live). Class: capability/polish. |
| TUI panel surface convergence corrective | M003 theme sourcing and shared chrome geometry | **closed** | `plans/closure/tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective/004-status.md`; plan `plans/implementation/tui-panel-surface-convergence-corrective/004-theme-sourcing-and-shared-chrome-geometry.md` | Approved into planning after M002b closed; **counts re-derived, and one of this roadmap's own claims was false**. §4.9's "five boxes" is **ten**, and §4.9's "`mcp` already demonstrates the correct pattern" is the inverse of the truth — `mcp`'s shared body reads its `set_theme` cache 44 times and the live theme zero times, making it the worst instance; `ui_node` has no `render_in_panel` and was never a docked box. Nine boxes read the cache exclusively, one (`diff`) is stale via `DiffViewer`, and four of the ten mix live and cached reads. `Color::White` counts (`plan` 6, `mcp` 2) were correct; two further literals in `confirm.rs`/`run_detail.rs` were unlisted. §4.10's "byte-identical" `centered_rect` pair is semantically but not textually identical, and its layout count drifted 63/24 → 56 `Layout::` sites across 31 files. **The selection-foreground token cannot be ingested from Halloy**: the format declares `buttons.*.foreground_selected` and **0 of the 50 bundled themes define it** (50/50 define `background_selected` and `buffer.selection`), so it must be **derived** — picking whichever of `base.foreground`/`base.background` has the higher `contrast_ratio` against the target, following the existing `derive_selection_dim` and `accent_primary` precedent. This is what keeps the constraint that **any Halloy theme ingests unchanged**; no new key may be required. No plumbing needed: `render_hosted` and both modal call sites already pass the live theme, so the fix is what ten bodies *read*. Class: polish. **Two scope items deliberately declined at planning**, both recorded in the plan rather than silently dropped: the shared chrome/geometry helper (no seam, no failing test, and it would run against the `Chrome::{Owned,Hosted}` contract M002b just established) and the legacy `Widget` cleanup (M002b's closure said five impls with no callers in `src/` or `tests/`; the real count is **ten**, none with a production caller, but `connect` and `share` have test-only callers in comparison tests that must be rewritten first — the other eight delete cleanly). Recommended as standalone milestones. **CLOSED.** Implementation overturned this row's headline number again: the `self.theme.` count measures how much code *reads a cache*, not whether the cache is *stale*. `tests/tui_theme_source_of_truth.rs` renders each box with theme A then theme B and inspects the cells, and the stale set is **four**, not ten — `review`, `plugin`, `security_review`, and `diff` via `DiffViewer`. `mcp` (44 reads) and `research` (46) reassign `self.theme = Arc::clone(theme)` *inside their own render body*, so each is overwritten on the frame it reads: they led this row's source count and are the two boxes that were already correct, and `mcp` was named "the worst instance" twice on the strength of a grep. `keybind`, `tree`, `plan` and `theme` were recorded as mixing live and cached reads, but their cached reads are all in the **dead legacy** paths, so their live bodies never repainted incoherently. At the pre-change baseline the new suite fails **4 and passes 5**. W2 converted `mcp` and `research` anyway (a cache a render method overwrites is a hazard; the acceptance criterion covers every render path) and deleted the now-dead field, `set_theme`, and constructor theme parameter from all six — which forced `Default` impls on the three constructors that lost their only argument, under clippy's `new_without_default`. **The token is derived and needs no new Halloy key:** `foreground_selected` is 0-of-50 across bundled themes while `background_selected` and `selection` are 50/50, so sourcing from it would return `None` for every real theme. `derive_text_on` picks whichever of the theme's own `foreground`/`background` contrasts more with the target fill, falling back to whichever of black/white contrasts **more** — taking the maximum is a guarantee (≥ 4.5:1), where picking by `is_dark()` leaves a surface at luminance 0.3 with white at 2.1:1. Sweep over all 50: worst `selection_text` 3.36:1 (`macaw-dark`), worst `accent_text` 3.07:1 (`broc`). Ten `Color::White` literals replaced (`plan` 6, `mcp` 2, `confirm` 1, `run_detail` 1) — **but `run_detail`'s was not a selection row**: it is body text with no fill behind it, so it takes `theme.foreground`, and the plan's `32 ThemeData literals` is **31** (`grep -c '^    ThemeData {'` → 31). `Widget` cannot carry a theme, so `DiffViewer`, `PlanDialog` and `DiffDialog`'s legacy paths became inherent methods rendering byte-identically; the dead legacy paths were **not** deleted. `centered_rect` de-duplicated onto `component.rs`. New guard `scripts/check_no_hardcoded_selection_colours.py` in `verify.sh quick`, **verified to exit 1** on a probe file with two live literals while ignoring its `#[cfg(test)]` fixture. `verify.sh quick` green; clippy `-D warnings` green; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean. Class: polish. |

| Desktop frontend and IDE foundation | M006-B native LSP read surface | **implemented; closed** | `plans/implementation/desktop-frontend-ide-foundation/008-native-lsp-read-surface.md` | Closure: `plans/closure/desktop-frontend-ide-foundation/009-status.md` (implementation `4ce0343b`, plan `49ed8b4d`, PR `#99`). Unblocked by accepted ADR-0012 (`plans/adrs/ADR-0012-native-lsp-read-surface-delivery-and-authorization.md`): two new `CoreRequest` variants — `LspReadGet` (`via_session` + `file.read`, covering hover/definition/references/document symbols/workspace symbols/semantic tokens via one operation enum) and `LspDiagnosticsGet` (`direct_project` + `file.read`, the resync authority) — plus `ProjectionEvent::LspDiagnosticsUpdated` on the existing `ProjectionStreamKind::Project` stream. A digest-keyed publisher emits only on content change, so a dropped envelope costs freshness, never correctness. Reads are warm-server-only via `find_existing_client_for_root_hint`. Two traps found while planning and recorded in the plan: the debouncing `get_diagnostics_for_file` would have wiped client diagnostics if used as the authority (the correct accessor is `get_diagnostic_snapshot_for_file`), and `MAX_CONNECTION_DIAGNOSTICS` is lifecycle diagnostic strings, not LSP infrastructure. **No TUI rendering** — that is M006-C. No ADR required beyond ADR-0012; `src/lsp/mutation.rs` and the `LspPreviewApply` path stay untouched. |

A review surface in front of the *existing* `LspPreviewApply` write: `build_apply_request` is the single constructor of a `CoreRequest::LspPreviewApply`, and the legacy `/lsp-preview-apply` command was refactored to route through it, so "one apply path" is structural rather than conventional. Adds no protocol, storage, migration, or authorization change. The daemon keeps the apply authority and its refusal is surfaced verbatim. `src/lsp/mutation.rs` is byte-identical; the dirty-buffer rejection is correct as written and is not weakened. Explicitly excludes the LSP read surface (M006-B/C). Closure: `plans/closure/desktop-frontend-ide-foundation/007-status.md`; implementation `cde7dbfe` (PR `#96`). The whole `scripts/` tree is absent from the diff, so the M006-A text-authority guard keeps a byte-identical `SCANNED_FILES` list. Must preserve the removed-`ShortCycle` regressions and the true positive that an identical repeated call still escalates and still terminates the turn. | Interactive terminal liveness post-closure corrective | C001 live output pull and terminal liveness closure | **closed** | `plans/implementation/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/001-live-output-pull-and-terminal-liveness-closure.md` | No open dependency. Baseline `719cfeb5`. Owns: a per-attachment bounded pull on the existing event-loop cadence; per-attachment read state that does **not** share `dialog_state.terminal_request`; a resize drain independent of typing; repaint for both surfaces; honest surface status text. No daemon change, no protocol variant, no storage, no bus event.
