| Interactive terminal liveness post-closure corrective | C002 honest transport refusal and capability boundary | **closed** | `plans/closure/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/002-status.md` | Baseline `8b6191140`. The nine terminal refusal sites all emitted "Daemon connection unavailable; …", which is false on the `/tui` WS-attach frontend: the daemon is healthy and serving every other surface, and `core_client` is `None` only because `src/client/attach.rs` opens `/tui`, speaks `TuiMessage`, and never builds a `CoreClient`. One shared classifier now owns the wording (`TerminalRefusal` — `TransportUnsupported` vs `NoDaemonConnection`) and all nine sites route through `terminal_core_client`, so the two causes can no longer share one sentence; the connection wording is preserved byte-for-byte where it is true. Falsified on baseline first: the refusal test failed with `toasts: ["Daemon connection unavailable; cannot create a terminal"]`. Implementation also found an unanticipated defect in the same silent class — `flush_terminal_input` classified *after* `take_pending_input` and `begin_input`, so a refused flush dropped the queued keystrokes and left `input_in_flight` permanently set (one toast, then silence for the life of the attachment); the drained resize is likewise restored to its queue on refusal. 169 tests green across the five terminal/panel suites (6 new, 157 C001 tests unmodified); `verify.sh quick` passes locally (CI has not run it). **Delivered no transport** — `src/server/**`, `crates/codegg-client/**`, `crates/codegg-protocol/**` and `crates/codegg-core/**` are untouched, and J5 forbids closing this as "remote principals cannot get a PTY": `/core` still routes `InteractiveProcess*`, owned by R001. |
| Interactive terminal liveness post-closure corrective | C003 live-surface cadence arming and honest mode state | **closed** | `plans/closure/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/003-status.md` (plan `plans/implementation/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/003-live-surface-cadence-and-honest-mode-state.md`) | No open dependency; no ADR required (the closed event-loop tick and panel-key authority are reused, and no transport is introduced). Class: **capability**, reclassified from polish on 2026-10-17 because the residue is a falsified capability claim plus false mode text, not ergonomics. Corrective against C001's strict closure claim, whose record is **not** edited. Baseline `18c3dca92`. Owns: arming the event loop whenever a mounted terminal surface is live, reusing the existing tick and `mounted_terminal_surface`'s visibility rule; one derived effective-input-mode accessor consumed by both the composer badge and the contextual hint line; and acceptance evidence driven through the **real** `select!`. Preserves I1–I5, in particular no second timer (I4) and no terminal bytes on the bus (I2). Start at Work package A — the acceptance signal is a real-event-loop test that types a command, then goes idle with no toast/streaming/resize and asserts the output still appears; it must fail on baseline for the stated reason, and that reason must be the dark wake arm. Stop rather than improvise if arming appears to need a second timer, if the mode fix would require changing what a key *means* (I3), or if it appears to need bytes on the bus or a push transport (new ADR). **Closed 2026-10-17 on baseline `18c3dca92`.** Both findings confirmed and fixed. The premise was falsified on baseline first: with the arming contributor removed, `a_live_terminal_surface_repaints_with_no_further_user_input` and `a_docked_terminal_keeps_output_after_the_keyboard_leaves_the_panel` both fail with "the event loop is not armed for a mounted live terminal surface" — `animation_interval` returns `None`, so the timed arm selects `pending()` and `service_terminal_output_pull` is never reached. The fix keys the contributor on **mount, never focus or read state** (focus is what changes when nobody is looking; gating the arm on `begin_output_read` would latch the loop off, since a dark arm means nothing wakes it to issue the read that would clear the condition), and `animation_interval` was extracted as a pure `&App` function so the arming decision is testable at all. The mode fix is one accessor, `App::composer_input_mode`, resolving over two seams key routing already reads; it is a **pure read** (`&mut self` only because `PanelCtx` grants are `&mut`), because a badge that forced `input_mode` to match what it displays would turn the state it reports into the state it causes. **C001's conditional status is discharged.** Unblock audit: C004 newly eligible — its blink was blocked because a parked loop cannot animate, and its hint-row work shared `composer_hint_text` with this milestone's mode work. |
| Interactive terminal liveness post-closure corrective | C004 terminal surface ergonomics | **closed** | **Unblocked 2026-10-17 by C003's closure** (`plans/closure/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/003-status.md`), which discharged both technical reasons: the blink was impossible before because the loop only repaints while something arms it (`src/tui/runtime/event_loop.rs:288-292`), and the hint-row change shared `composer_hint_text` with C003's derived-mode work. Plan `plans/implementation/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/004-terminal-surface-ergonomics.md`; class polish; baseline `18c3dca92`. **Closed 2026-10-17 on baseline `18c3dca92`, all four owned items delivered.** (1) **Unfocused hint row** — `TerminalPanel::composer_hints` answers `None` when `!hints.focused`. Falsified on baseline first at two levels: the unit seam returned `Some("TERM · focus to start a shell")`, and a rendered frame showed `TERM · focus to scroll, select and type` sitting directly above the composer while `[NOR]` held the keyboard. The seam doc claimed a bespoke panel was entitled to advertise without the keyboard; that rationale is now split **by kind** — a *hosted* panel (an adopted box like `/files`) still advertises and leads with the gesture that makes its verbs live, a *bespoke* panel declines, and the terminal is bespoke. The three hosted `/files` tests pass unmodified. Losing the row does **not** move the input box: `session_layout` gives the prompt chunk a fixed, bottom-anchored height and the working strip, hint line and todo strip are carved off its top, so the row goes to the transcript (`Min(10)`) while the composer stays put — pinned, because the arithmetic reads as though the opposite should happen. (2) **Insert blink** — `spinner::cursor_blink_visible(elapsed)` is a pure sibling of `spinner_frame` on the one frame table, half a second visible and half a second hidden, threaded in as a phase (`PanelCtx::caret_blink_on`, `render_view(.., caret_blink_on, ..)`) rather than counted on a view, so two renders at one instant agree and a coalesced tick cannot desync. **The hidden phase drops `REVERSED` and nothing else** — the cell stays painted, or the line would trim back and the whole prompt line would blink away. **NOR never blinks**, because there the cursor is the anchor `x`/`v`/`d` act on. No second ticker (I4): the armed 100ms pull C003 registered is what repaints it. `CURSOR_BLINK_PHASE` is a literal because `Duration`'s `Mul` is not `const`, and a test pins the derivation from `SPINNER_FRAME_INTERVAL`. (3) **Prompt line after new/clear** — `clear_terminal` queues `CLEAR_THROUGH_PROCESS` (`ESC [ 2 J ESC [ H CR`) through `queue_input`, the same bounded, permissioned, live-link path as any keystroke, **before** the local wipe; a typed refusal reports itself and leaves the screen intact. Both halves are load-bearing: the erase+home is what a terminal means by clear, but a shell draws `PS1` after a *command* rather than after an escape, so the newline is what makes it repaint. The literal `clear` command was rejected — it depends on a `clear` binary on the child's `PATH` and would surface `clear: command not found` for a keystroke the user never typed. Nothing on the cleared screen is fabricated, and a new terminal shows its own prompt with no keystroke, which is C003's armed cadence rather than work here. (4) **`Esc` reader-cursor placement** — `escape` places the reader cursor on **the last row that has content, or row 0 when nothing has**, via `set_cursor` so placement is bookkeeping and does not extend a `v` selection. This deliberately supersedes the plan's default wording ("the line above the last one"), which trades one wrong place for another: a shell whose prompt sits on row 0 of a 24-row screen would have the cursor parked in blank space *below* the only line on screen. The `Hidden → Viewing` sweep stays a separate pass and never touches `nav`. **Fixture races found and fixed, not worked around:** `drain_tui_commands` stops at the first 25ms gap, but these tests depend on two asynchronous steps and the second is spawned by the first — the create is what materialises the view, opens the box and gives the panel something to adopt, and the attach is spawned by the create completion. Three C003 tests (`box_rendered_buffer_shows_live_pty_output`, `a_docked_terminal_keeps_output_after_the_keyboard_leaves_the_panel`, `a_docked_terminal_reads_normal_once_it_lets_go`) were passing by luck, failing on their *premises* (`the box must be mounted…`, `the panel adopted the new terminal`) rather than on any behaviour. Latent until this milestone, for a precise reason: the hint row used to answer `Some` in both focus states, so "never got the keyboard" and "just handed it back" were indistinguishable. Replaced by two bounded waits, `await_terminal_create` and `await_live_attachment`; no sleeps, and no product defect — `focus` refusing an unattached view and `owns_exit_key` declining a keyboard nobody held were both correct. 21 new tests across `tui::interactive_terminal`, `tui::panels::terminal`, `tui::components::spinner` and `tui::commands::interactive_terminal`, plus an integration test in `tests/tui_panel_docking.rs`. Outstanding by design: the event loop's `select!`/sleep itself is still not exercised by a test — only the arming decision is — and CI evidence is outstanding.| No open dependency. No ADR required: the blink rides the existing frame clock and the armed pull (I4), the clear goes through the existing typed input path, and the placement rule is a pure function of what is on screen. Push transport and bus-carried bytes remain ADR-gated and untouched — `src/server/**`, `crates/codegg-client/**`, `crates/codegg-protocol/**` and `crates/codegg-core/**` are all unmodified. **Corrected 2026-10-17:** this cell previously carried a *blocked* paragraph about M008 and proposed ADR-0034 (syntax-node text objects) belonging to the selection-advisor workstream; it contradicted the row's own `ready` status and was a copy error, not a dependency of this plan.|
| User prompt queue | M001 turn contention refusal correctness | **closed** | `plans/implementation/user-prompt-queue/001-turn-contention-refusal-correctness.md` | `plans/subsystems/user-prompt-queue-roadmap.md` §7 Milestone 1. Class **capability**. Dependency-clean: no queue, no storage, no protocol, no daemon change, and **not** gated on ADR-0035 acceptance — it takes only ADR-0036's refusal-taxonomy boundary (an authorization refusal must stay distinguishable from a channel/resource condition), and if that ADR is rejected the work package stands with different naming. Written first because the queue would otherwise be built on a submit path that reports "no active turn" while one is running. Objective: a submit refused because a turn is active leaves turn state truthful and the composed text intact. **The substantive defect is state corruption, not message wording** — the arm clears turn identity and working age for a turn that is still in flight, so treating this as "make the error nicer" would leave the queue reading an invalidated state. **The correct restore mechanism already exists and is unused on this path:** Session-create failure already stashes the newer draft within its bound, restores the refused prompt, and marks it so retry does not duplicate the transcript entry (`apply_prompt_session_failure`); the submit completion arm instead receives a formatted string with no access to the prompt. Reuse that shape rather than writing a second restore with its own no-duplicate proof. Also replaces the submit path's bare `tokio::spawn` and hand-rolled identity comparison with the shared task-spawn helper and the existing `AsyncUiRequestState` request-state machine, preserving the daemon's `request_id` derivation because that is its idempotency key. Hard stop recorded in §14: **no retry-on-behalf-of-the-user** — resubmitting when a turn ends would be an unauthorized queue. M002's hard dependency on M001 is discharged by this closure, so M002 becomes plannable once ADR-0035 is accepted; the closure record must perform and record that unblock audit. **Closed** — closure record `plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/001-status.md`; the unblock audit was performed there: M002's hard dependency on M001 is discharged, but M002 stays unplanned and blocked solely on ADR-0035 acceptance. 23 new unit tests plus the `tui_render`, `tui_completion_trajectory`, and `tui_usability_fixes` targets pass. Two corrections recorded rather than re-cited: the plan's §11 filters `--lib tui::async_request` and `--lib tui::runtime::command_dispatch` are module paths, not test names, and select zero tests; and §6.1's "unknown code → unexpected" is implemented as a recoverable rejection, because `Rejected` is the only class that keeps the user's text. One pre-existing environmental failure is unrelated and verified against the clean baseline: `core::daemon::tests::turn_submit_rejects_when_active` (`InvalidRootPath("/tmp")`). Superseded as the next step: ADR-0035 was accepted 2026-10-18 and M002 is split into M002a/M002b. |
| User prompt queue | M002a durable queue storage and read projection | **closed** | `plans/implementation/user-prompt-queue/002a-durable-queue-storage-and-read-projection.md` | All hard deps satisfied: M001 **closed**, ADR-0035 **accepted** 2026-10-18. Class **infrastructure** — one additive **v80** migration plus the `STORAGE_LAYOUT_VERSION` bump (both asserted by `scripts/check_project_catalog_invariants.py` and `tests/storage_migrations.rs`), the entry model with length/count/expiry bounds enforced as typed refusals *and* schema `CHECK` backstops, a daemon-maintained ordinal under a `UNIQUE (session_id, ordinal)` so total order is schema-enforced, lazy deterministic expiry, and `UserPromptQueueList` under `Capability::SessionRead` classified in `UNINSTRUMENTED_OPERATIONS`. `human_shell_promotion` (v70) is the in-tree analogue and `crates/codegg-core/tests/human_shell_promotion.rs` its test precedent. Two deliberate narrowings were recorded rather than hidden: the v80 `status` `CHECK` enumerates all five roadmap values even though three are unreachable until M003, because SQLite cannot `ALTER` a `CHECK` and narrowing would force a rebuild of a durable user table; and `run_at_ms` is **omitted** because nothing here writes it and M007 adds it with an in-place `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN` (**v82**, since ADR-0037 acceptance spent v81 on the queue-owner binding). **Closed** — closure record `plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/002a-status.md`. It **delivered no user-visible capability**, stated in those words in the closure record: no mutation exists, so the read has no producer and the queue always reads empty. A guard test asserts that no `CoreRequest` variant can mutate the queue, so M002b's boundary is enforced rather than implied. |
| User prompt queue | M002b queue mutation operations and mutation authority | **closed** | `plans/implementation/user-prompt-queue/002b-queue-mutation-operations-and-authority.md` | All hard deps satisfied: M002a **closed**, ADR-0037 **accepted** 2026-10-18 (Option B). Class **infrastructure**. Scope: the four typed mutations (add/edit/delete/reorder), the **v81** additive migration carrying the durable queue-owner binding, adoption and revision-fenced transfer (both audited to `prompt_submit` per ADR-0037, deliberately **not** `membership_change` as `session_control_transfer` uses), and one generalised controller predicate whose call shape is a **named** `ControllerScope` enum — `ControllerScope::Turn` vs `ControllerScope::QueueMutation` — rather than the `Option<&str>` binding an earlier blocked draft suggested, because `None` cannot distinguish "an unowned queue" from "a turn operation with no turn" and those need different refusals. Three design points are settled in the plan: **first add adopts atomically** on an `Absent` queue (otherwise nothing could ever create the first entry) while an **`Unowned`** queue is refused for every mutation and repaired only by explicit adoption, so an invalidated queue cannot silently become owned by whoever touches it next; **reorder renumbers collision-free** under M002a's `UNIQUE (session_id, ordinal)` using a band offset above `USER_PROMPT_QUEUE_MAX_PENDING` (negative sentinels are impossible — the `CHECK (ordinal >= 0)` forbids them); and **add is idempotent on a client-supplied `entry_id`**, applying turn-admission's at-most-once discipline to identity without inventing a receipt namespace ADR-0035 forbids. **Closed** — closure record `plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/002b-status.md`. It **delivered no user-visible capability**, stated in those words in the closure record: no command, dialog, key, row-0 change, or projection field was added, so nothing a person can press reaches the six new operations. The adoption policy ADR-0037 deferred to this milestone is settled and audited — **the calling principal becomes the owner** on first add, not the session creator and not the local interactive owner. `ControllerScope` shipped with **three** variants, not the plan's two: routing `adopt` through `QueueMutation` would refuse it with `QueueUnowned` and make the ADR's mandated repair path unreachable, so `QueueAdoption` asks the question adoption actually asks. That deviation was found by a failing test, and the code was wrong rather than the test. Two real bugs the new tests caught are recorded in the closure record rather than shipped: `delete` never renumbered its survivors, leaving a permanent ordinal gap, and `is_permutation` had an inverted `HashSet` duplicate check. `cargo test --test session_family` reports 100 passed / 2 failed, both failing on `workspace_register_failed` for `/tmp` because `/tmp` is a symlink on this host — verified environmental and outside this diff, not a regression, and not presented as green. |
| User prompt queue | M003 queue delivery through ordinary turn admission | **closed** | `plans/implementation/user-prompt-queue/003-queue-delivery-through-turn-admission.md` | Class **capability**. Its stated hard dependency (M002b closure) is discharged; planning it exposed the decision ADR-0035 and ADR-0037 both left open — **as whom** a queued entry is submitted. The daemon may originate an admission, but the only such path (`submit_idle_continuation_turn`, ADR-0031) reconstructs authority from durable attribution via `AuthenticatedPrincipal::reconstructed`, which needs five fields, while the v81 owner row stores one (`owner_principal`). Delivering under a synthetic daemon identity takes the controller lease from the real owner and locks it out of its own queue at its first mid-turn mutation. **`plans/adrs/ADR-0038-queue-delivery-execution-authority.md` raised 2026-10-18, `proposed`** — a proposed ADR confers no authority, so the plan is registered `blocked` rather than `ready`. It recommends Option B (resolve attribution from team state, no migration) over Option C (a self-contained owner row, which **contests migration v82 with M007's `run_at_ms`**). The inherited `status` `CHECK` question is answered in the plan: make all five values reachable rather than rebuild a durable user table. Research additionally found **three projection behaviours that break the instant `delivering` has a producer** — `list_live` sweeps *and* selects `status = 'pending'`, `check_capacity` counts only `pending`, and the expiry sweep covers only `pending`, so an unresolved `delivering` row is invisible, uncounted, never expired, and never retried. The daemon has **no periodic session-observation loop**, and the queue has **no `CoreEvent`** (it reloads only on the session-change edge), so delivery needs its own trigger and a push path. |

The prior registry row therefore **overstated** M004's blocker: the roadmap's own fallback (duplicate rather than fork) applies and M004 is `ready`. Two roadmap descriptions of the TUI were **corrected against source**: §4's "right-hand side reserved for a named escape gesture" is actually a **left-aligned `" · "`-joined suffix** (`stop_hint`, `src/tui/app/render.rs:1690-1703`), and M004's implied `Dialog` key arms would target the legacy router that is `#[cfg(any())]`-disabled (`src/tui/app/mod.rs:4359-4368`) — FocusManager is the sole key authority, so the box must be a FocusManager-mounted `Component`. Planning surfaced **two store-level facts that were not previously recorded anywhere**, both from M003's `user_prompt_queue.rs`, and both are registered as roadmap risks: (1) `reorder` validates its permutation against the **pending** set only (`list_live_in` → `OPERABLE_STATUS_PREDICATE`, `:839-865`, `:1157`) while the client projection carries pending **and** delivering (`list_projected_in`, `:1184`), so a client sending every id it rendered is refused with `OrderMismatch` the instant any entry is delivering — M005 must filter before permuting; (2) `renumber_dense` displaces **every row session-wide and status-blind** before densifying, which prevents an `UNIQUE (session_id, ordinal)` collision but jumps a `delivering` row's ordinal above every pending row, so it renders last and a **released** delivery returns to `pending` at the back of the queue — closed M002b/M003 behaviour, so M005 must test and record it and a store fix is a maintainer decision, not a TUI workaround. `entry_id` is **client-supplied**, so the TUI mints it, which is what makes add idempotent under retry. There is **no shared `captures_text` predicate on `App`**; the precedent is `ConnectDialog::is_text_input_step` (`src/tui/components/dialogs/connect.rs:495-501`, `:1189-1195`), so M005 introduces the box's own `is_text_step()` and guards every action letter with it. **Correction recorded at closure:** the plan's last sentence was a wrong premise — this box **overrides** `docks_as_viewer()` to `true`, so the shifted-key reasoning does not hold for it. The keys survive anyway because `App::dock_target` has no `DialogType::Queue` arm, which makes the docking-steal branch dead regardless of the viewer flag; that is now asserted rather than assumed. `[`/`]` were chosen over `Shift+K`/`Shift+J` regardless, because a shifted variant of the movement key that does something *other* than move is a pairing people misfire. Closure: `plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/005-status.md`. **Superseded in part 2026-10-18 by C001** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/C001-status.md`), which fixes the measured §3(b) behaviour rather than documenting it. M005's own record is accurate and is **not** rewritten: it measured what the store did and disposed of it honestly inside its scope. What is superseded is the disposition — "accepted and documented" — and the `docs/tui.md` workaround it produced. |
| User prompt queue | M007 `/queue` delayed delivery: scheduling a prompt for later | closed | `plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/007-status.md` | Class **capability**; **takes migration v82** — `STORAGE_LAYOUT_VERSION` 81 -> 82; v80 and v81 untouched. The milestone was blocked on one named decision, taken as **Option A** (a bounded queue-scoped advance tick) and recorded in `plans/adrs/ADR-0039-queue-advancement-wakeup-authority.md`, **accepted 2026-10-18**. Its roadmap premise — that advancement rides an existing periodic observation point — was falsified during planning, so the tick is new: it computes the earliest due instant, sleeps exactly until it, and **parks with no timer armed** when nothing is scheduled. `run_at_ms` is nullable with **no `CHECK` and no sentinel**, because v80's `status` `CHECK` cannot be altered additively. Ordering stays `ORDER BY ordinal` and eligibility is one shared predicate, so reorder permutes only already-eligible entries and cannot invert timer precedence. Authoring is a **second step** (text, then duration) rather than an inline `| 1h` suffix, because a delimiter is a character the user cannot type. A due time is a **floor, not a promise** — delivered late across a restart, never early. The delivery entry gained a **third** caller as a recorded decision, with `--self-test` re-proven by injecting a **fourth** and rejecting it. The milestone's own wakeup test found and fixed a real defect: a due-but-undeliverable entry left the tick spinning at zero delay |
| User prompt queue | M006 `/queue` send-now: steering the running turn | **closed** | `plans/implementation/user-prompt-queue/006-send-now-steering-from-the-queue.md` → `plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/006-status.md` | Class **capability**. **Delivered 2026-10-18**; the design below is as planned, with three corrections recorded in the closure record. **The only milestone in this roadmap whose authorization is a precondition**: ADR-0036 names `plans/implementation/user-prompt-queue/006-*` in its own text, and its acceptance (2026-10-18, Option A) is discharged. M004 and M005 closures both discharged. Objective: make the existing, implemented, controller-gated `CoreRequest::TurnSteer` reachable from the box, with honest behavior on every backend error branch. Measured: `TurnSteer` is dispatched at `src/core/daemon_turns.rs:1258-1310` and **no TUI path issues it**; the five outcomes already exist as distinct wire codes (`Ack`, `steer_queue_full`, `steer_unavailable`, `steer_not_supported`, `turn_id_mismatch`/`no_active_turn`, plus the controller-predicate denial); and `app.session_state.active_turn_id` already carries the turn identity, so **no projection field is added**. **No migration**: on `Ack` the entry is marked `delivered` and bound to the accepting turn via the `delivered_turn_id`/`delivered_at_ms` columns v80 already created, so the only new store work is one `pending → delivered` transition guarded on `pending`. **This is the decision ADR-0036 explicitly deferred to this milestone** ("a UX decision for the milestone that implements the box", forbidden to M004/M005) — leaving the entry `pending` would run the same instruction **twice**, once steered and once claimed by M003 as a fresh turn, which ADR-0036's own drivers forbid. The rejected alternative, a new `steered` status, cannot be added cheaply: v80's `status` `CHECK` is not `ALTER`-able, so SQLite would force a rebuild of a durable user table. **Steering must not consult the queue owner** — ADR-0037's exclusion is quoted verbatim in the plan; steering addresses a running turn with a live lease, while ADR-0037 governs mutation between turns. **Correction:** the plan's transport guidance is superseded by measurement — send-now issues `CoreClient::request`, the same call every other queue operation already makes, so it needs **no** `is_ws_attach_transport()` gate; that guard governs `send_remote_message` answer channels, and adding one here would have been cargo-cult. True at-most-once across a *lost* `Ack` needs a durable steer-attempt record and is explicitly out of scope: the plan reports the unknown outcome rather than retrying. Adds no third caller of `submit_queued_prompt_turn`. **What shipped:** `UserPromptQueueSendNow` request/response, `handle_user_prompt_queue_send_now`, `mark_steer_delivered` (guarded on `pending`, ownership re-checked inside its transaction, one typed vocabulary shared with `delete`), `DialogState::queue_send_request` on its own generation, and the `s` key. The request carries `entry_id` and nothing else. One outcome exceeded the plan: `AcceptedNotSettled`, for a turn that took the prompt while the queue could not record it. `steer_channel_code` is now shared with `TurnSteer`. 17 new box tests plus 10 new store tests; both mandatory injections proven non-vacuous. |
| User prompt queue | C001 released-delivery order displacement corrective | **closed** | `plans/implementation/user-prompt-queue/C001-released-delivery-order-displacement-corrective.md`; closure `plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/C001-status.md` | Class **invariant** — ADR-0035 fixes queue ordering as a daemon-owned total order. This is the maintainer decision M005's closure record asked for, and planning it measured a **worse** defect than M005 recorded. M005 observed that a released `delivering` entry keeps the displacement ordinal (1 000 000) and *renders* last; the delivery read is `ORDER BY ordinal ASC` (`user_prompt_queue.rs:1401-1405`), so it is **delivered** last too — contradicting ADR-0035's own "a trigger that fires out of order must not reorder the user's work". **Three** paths are affected, not one: `release_delivery` (`:1530`), `retry_failed` (`:1554`), and `resolve_stranded_delivery(Retry)` all restore `status = 'pending'` with **no ordinal write**. Any of them followed by any reorder silently demotes a queued prompt; daemon shutdown or a transient storage error is enough to trigger it. **No new ADR is required** — the fix implements ADR-0035 rather than changing it, and M005's closure record is accurate and is **not** rewritten. **The fix takes no migration**: `renumber_dense` writes dense ordinals for the whole *live* set (pending **and** delivering) with non-pending rows preserved at their existing rank, while the uniform session-wide displacement step stays **exactly** as it is — which is what keeps `UNIQUE (session_id, ordinal)` safe against terminal rows, so M002b's uniqueness tests pass unmodified. This also removes M005's cosmetic symptom as a side effect. The client's permutation contract is unchanged (still pending ids only). **Two M005 tests asserted the defect** and are to be replaced by position-preservation tests; they were measurements of an accepted-and-documented outcome, not contract requirements, which is what makes correcting them safe. The `docs/tui.md` callout at `357-362` ("A reordered prompt that was mid-delivery may come back at the end … add it last rather than reordering an older one") is removed **in full**, because after the fix its premise is false. Independent of M006/M007; takes no migration number, so **v82 stays M007's**. **Delivered 2026-10-18.** `merge_operable_into_live` folds the client's desired pending order with each non-pending live row held at **the rank it already had** — *preserve existing rank*, never pinned to an end, because pinning a released entry to the tail is the defect itself — and `renumber_dense` writes that merged live set. The displacement step is unchanged and terminal rows are still displaced: they are not live but still hold ordinals under a session-wide `UNIQUE`, so excluding them would reintroduce the collision the offset prevents. Because a `delivering` row now *receives* a dense ordinal, `release_delivery` and `resolve_stranded_delivery(Retry)` needed **no change at all** and are now **order-neutral** — the released queue is byte-identical to the queue before the release. The client's permutation contract is unchanged, verified by still refusing a permutation that names a `delivering` row. The two M005 tests that asserted the defect are **replaced** by position-preservation tests, and the `docs/tui.md` callout is removed in full. Verification is an **exhaustive** enumeration of all **1 554** sequences of length 1–4 over **six** operations — deterministic, not sampled — and a sixth operation `ClaimHold` was **added after** injection (i) showed the enumeration passed against the unfixed store: with no step leaving a row `delivering`, no two operations ever straddled a reorder with one, which is the whole defect scenario. **Two defects were found and reported, not fixed**, per C001 §14. (1) **`retry_failed` is not reached by the merge** — the plan's claim that all three return paths need no change is falsified for this one, because it revives a row that was *terminal* during the reorder and therefore absent from the live set; no production caller, absent from the client projection, and where such a row belongs is an open maintainer decision. (2) **The uniform displacement collides with itself on a second renumber** — pre-existing and independent of the merge, since the displacement `UPDATE` is byte-identical before and after; reachable from the queue box alone (queue three prompts, reorder, let one fail, reorder, **reorder again** — refused with a storage error). Both are pinned by tests so a fix is met by a red test, not a silent behaviour change. |
| User prompt queue | C002 queue write-transaction shape corrective | closed | `plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/C002-status.md` | Class **invariant**; **no migration** — `STORAGE_LAYOUT_VERSION` stays 82 and v80/v81/v82 are untouched. Class: `crates/codegg-core/src/session/user_prompt_queue.rs`. A deferred `pool.begin()` takes no lock, so a read-then-write path holds a shared read lock and must **upgrade** it to write; SQLite refuses that upgrade with `SQLITE_BUSY` (code 6) **without waiting**, so `busy_timeout` never applies and two such callers can **both** fail. Measured at **204 of 256** concurrent claim rounds before the change (M003's test failed ~4 runs in 12); after, **0 of 192**, with every round still admitting exactly one entry. Fixed by `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` on all eight read-then-write paths, through sqlx's `TransactionManager` so its transaction-depth counter stays correct. `ownership` (writes nothing) and `list_live` (its first statement is already the expiry sweep) keep a deferred transaction **on purpose**. `scripts/check_queue_write_transaction_shape.py` pins the shape and the allowlist, wired into `scripts/verify.sh quick` and CI, with a `--self-test` that injects both a deferred read-then-write path and a closing rollback. M003's and M006's records are **not** rewritten |

the workstream now has **no ready item and no open decision**. **C001** closed the
release-order displacement without a migration. **M006** closed the send-now
handover (`s`), also with no migration. **M007** closed delayed delivery and **took
v82**, so `STORAGE_LAYOUT_VERSION` is now **82**. Its §2 decision was taken as
**Option A** (a bounded queue-scoped advance tick) and recorded in ADR-0039, drafted
**proposed** — acceptance is a maintainer action and is the only thing outstanding
across this workstream. **M008** (desktop parity) is now eligible and remains
explicitly deferrable. Nothing here reopens a closed milestone.



**C002 (decided 2026-10-17, baseline `8b6191140`) — Option B. Closed 2026-10-17.** The transport research that preceded C002 falsified its original premise in a way that changed its shape: `/core` already routes `InteractiveProcess*` (no variant allowlist in `handle_core_frame`), so there is no missing route to add — only the WS-attach TUI frontend (`/tui`, `TuiMessage`, no `CoreClient`) genuinely lacks terminals. C002 therefore does **not** route bytes. It fixes the diagnostic instead: all nine refusal sites blame a daemon that is healthy, which sends the operator to restart the wrong component. Deliver the typed refusal that names the transport, and record the boundary honestly. **Do not** let this close as "remote principals cannot create interactive processes" — `/core` still routes the family, and `InteractiveAuthority::remote()` can create a PTY and run arbitrary commands while being denied only `can_terminate()`. That question is registered as `remote-interactive-process-authorization-corrective` R001 (`proposed`, new ADR required) and deliberately does not block C002. C003 (polish) remains unopened.

**C002 outcome.** Closed at `plans/closure/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/002-status.md`. One shared classifier (`TerminalRefusal` — `TransportUnsupported` vs `NoDaemonConnection`) owns the wording and all nine entry points route through `terminal_core_client`, so the two causes can no longer share one sentence; the connection wording is preserved byte-for-byte where it is true. The premise was falsified on baseline before any production change (`toasts: ["Daemon connection unavailable; cannot create a terminal"]`). Implementation also found an unanticipated defect in the same silent class: `flush_terminal_input` classified *after* `take_pending_input` and `begin_input`, so a refused flush dropped the queued keystrokes and left `input_in_flight` permanently set — one visible toast, then silence for the life of the attachment; the drained resize is likewise restored to its queue on refusal. 169 tests green across the five terminal/panel suites (6 new; the 157 C001 tests unmodified); `verify.sh quick` passes locally, CI has not run it. The change delivered **no transport** and claims none: `src/server/**`, `crates/codegg-client/**`, `crates/codegg-protocol/**` and `crates/codegg-core/**` are untouched, and the closure states plainly that `/core` still routes `InteractiveProcess*`. R001 remains `proposed` and blocked behind its own new ADR. C003 was subsequently opened as capability class and **closed 2026-10-17** at `plans/closure/interactive-terminal-liveness-post-closure-corrective/003-status.md`, discharging C001's conditional status; C004 (polish) moved from blocked to **ready** and then closed on 2026-10-17. **Unblock audit: nothing newly unblocked** — neither was waiting on C002, and C002 had no open dependency.

**TUI editor Helix keybinding parity (planning 2026-10-17; ADR-0034 proposed, not accepted):** This workstream is the explicitly deferred successor named in the closed `tui-editor-correctness-usability-roadmap.md` §3; it adds no authority, no protocol, and no dependency, and does not reopen any closed milestone. **M001 is closed** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/001-status.md`) — redo is now `U` (Helix's own chord), `Ctrl+r` keeps exactly one owner (resync) in both modes, a second dead-alternative instance (`| (m, Char('d'))` in the `D` arm) was closed, `scripts/check_tui_editor_key_authority.py` is wired into `verify.sh quick` and the CI `verify` job, and eight dispatch-path tests plus a 91-assertion Insert-mode sweep now cover the route through `editor_buffer_key`. **M002 is closed** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/002-status.md`) — `pending_command` and `yank_prefix_pending` are both deleted and replaced by one `EditorPrefix` state machine; `gg` and `space y` are unchanged and their existing tests passed **unmodified**; `[`/`]` are armed with their leaves deferred to M006; and `scripts/check_tui_editor_prefix_state.py` fails the build if a second prefix field ever reappears. A latent fix rode along: reconciliation disarmed only the `g` prefix, so a `space` typed before a reload stayed armed and yanked in the reloaded buffer. **Execute M003 next and alone** (character-finding motions) — M004, M005 and M010 are all waiting on it. M006, M011 and M012 also had their M002 dependency edge discharged and are eligible for fresh planning; M007 remains independently available in parallel. M007 (multi-selection) is the longest pole and depends only on M001, so it may be developed in parallel with M003 on a separate branch. M009 is blocked on ADR-0034 acceptance. The terminal panel shares the selection and navigation dialect and must be re-verified after M002, M007, and M008. Two residual findings are recorded in the M001 closure and remain owned: `src/tui/components/help_overlay.rs` is unreferenced dead code, and the shipped `/help` lists no editor chords at all. Full Helix parity is explicitly **not** the roadmap completion definition — the deferred list in §3 and the ADR-0034-gated slices stay owned elsewhere. Source: `plans/subsystems/tui-editor-helix-parity-roadmap.md`. **M003 is closed** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/003-status.md`) — `f`/`F`/`t`/`T` search across line boundaries and `Alt`-`.` repeats the last find, armed through M002's `EditorPrefix` with no new field; the bounded scan discloses `Absent` versus `Bounded` in the status row; and a per-byte scan that silently skipped every multi-byte target (because `read_bytes` refuses a non-boundary range) was replaced with a chunked one, 79 s to 0.22 s measured. It does not yet distinguish `t`/`T` from `f`/`F`, because the distinction is a selection property owed to M007. **Planning pass 2026-10-18:** M004, M005, and **M013** now have implementation plans and are ready; M010 is blocked on M005 and M013. **M013 is new and is a corrective insertion** — verified at baseline `84440fe7f` that the LSP surface F2–F9 already calls cannot deliver: `publish_changed_diagnostics` and `DIAGNOSTICS_POLL_INTERVAL` in `src/core/daemon_lsp.rs` have zero callers, and the only production write to `daemon.lsp_diagnostics` sits inside that uncalled function; separately, `find_existing_client_for_root_hint(Some(&root), None)` returns `Err` when multiple clients share a root and that `Err` is mapped flatly to `NotReady`, so any polyglot workspace has F3–F9 silently dead. **Execute M013 before M010** — M013 repairs delivery and extends the read-only surface, and M010 binds the keys. Suggested order: M004 and M013 in parallel, then M010. **M005 is closed** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/005-status.md`) — `gs`, `W`/`B`/`E` and `gj`/`gk` shipped, and `EditorPrefix::leaf()` became a per-prefix `leaves()` table that M010 and M013 build on, so **M010's M005 edge is discharged** and M010 now waits on M013 alone. Two keymap corrections were made against the live Helix docs during implementation: `gl` is `goto_line_end` (not "last goto"), and `gt`/`gc`/`gb` are **screen**-relative and are deliberately unbound because motions resolve against a fixed `NOMINAL_VISIBLE_LINES` guess. M005 also fixed a pre-existing defect in which `w`/`b`/`e` skipped every non-ASCII character (`read_bytes` refuses a non-boundary range, so the **first** byte of a multi-byte character is unreadable) — on `日本語 text`, `w` from byte 0 landed at the end of the document — and an unbounded loop in `long_word_end` on any document ending in a multi-byte character. **Planning pass 2026-10-19 (M009–M012, M014, M015):** the four milestones that had no implementation plan now have one, written against the live Helix keymap and the tree at `a8778e87c`, and the roadmap's status table was reconciled to match. **M009** was registered `ready` rather than blocked: ADR-0034's own decision text says the word and delimiter-pair text objects are *"explicitly **not** gated"* on the ADR, and Helix's text-object table agrees — the syntax-node slice moved to a new milestone, **M014**, which is blocked on ADR-0034 acceptance and deliberately has **no plan** until the ADR selects Option A or Option B, because the two produce different contracts. **M010** is `ready` with a plan and now owns the **pickers and the clipboard family**; it reclaims `space f`, which M013 had bound to a *duplicate* of `space s`, and makes it the file picker. **M011** decided the `Ctrl+s`/`Ctrl+r` conflict the roadmap had deferred — both keep their app meanings, and the undo checkpoint capability is delivered by insert-session coalescing instead — and found that the roadmap's conflict list had omitted three further global chords. **M012** replaces the two undo stacks with one history and a time cursor, and corrects the roadmap's pointer to a `TextTransaction::invert()` method that does not exist. **M015** is new and exists because the planning exposed a real gap: M013 delivered the format and rename **previews** with daemon arms and panel renderings, and **no key reaches either**. Nine corrections against this roadmap's own prose were produced by the four plans and are recorded as dated amendments on the milestone entries rather than absorbed. **Implementation started 2026-10-19 (wave 1).** M009, M010 and M011 moved from `ready` to `active`; M012 and M015 remain `ready for handoff`. Three work packages landed in parallel on disjoint files: M009's byte foundation (`src/tui/editor_match.rs` — a dependency-free pair table and depth-counting scanner, 33 unit tests, verified standalone with `rustc --test` so it needs neither the cargo build lock nor the editor's snapshot type); M010's pending-effect generalization (`src/tui/editor_effect.rs` — `EditorLspLeafEffect` became `EditorPendingEffect`, `PrefixAction::Lsp` became `PrefixAction::Effect`, and the pending slot moved from `EditorLspState::pending_leaf` to `EditorState::pending_effect`, binding nothing); and M011's guard teaching (`scripts/check_tui_editor_key_authority.py` — the Insert table is now found in either dispatch shape, and a `--self-test` with 9 cases proves the guard **fails** on planted violations). Two deviations were recorded rather than absorbed: the plan's `pub(crate)` on `ClipboardVerb` is **compiler-forced** to `pub` (a `pub` enum may not hold a `pub(crate)` type), and the guard's `arm_blocks` had a **parser defect** that mis-split the real Insert table's catch-all and had to be fixed before the guard was reading the table it claimed to police. The M013 closure record is historical evidence and was **not** edited, but it now names symbols that no longer exist; the M010 closure must state that it changed a closed milestone's surface. The five plans **cannot** be implemented as five independent efforts — they share three core files, one guard script and one set of counters — so the total order is recorded as roadmap **§13**, with the mandatory constraints, the per-stage chord-count ledger, the shared-state assumptions, the open questions and the pre-existing failures that are **not** this work's. Source: `plans/subsystems/tui-editor-helix-parity-roadmap.md`.

OpenCode Go is no longer a one-surface wrapper: `crates/codegg-providers/src/opencode_go.rs` resolves each requested model to exactly one wire surface through the shared profile, and `provider_profile.rs` is the narrow adapter (explicit `opencode_go` → `opencode-go` id map; durable CodeGG ids never renamed). Chat and Responses send Bearer, Messages sends `x-api-key`, and the stable `x-opencode-session` header survives all three. Unknown wire mappings fail closed locally instead of defaulting to Chat, and an unresolved surface maps to `ProviderError::ModelNotFound` (class `model_not_found`) so a local metadata failure cannot corrupt M010's credential axis. No speculative cross-surface retry exists: `stream()` resolves exactly once before any I/O.

One constraint was discovered while verifying the pin and is recorded because it is not stated in the plan: `eggpool-provider-profile` depends on `eggpool-wire` **by path**, and Cargo resolves a git dependency's path dependencies against the *parent's* revision. CodeGG pinned `eggpool-wire` at `f05b18b`, so adding the profile at `9ac6a131` without touching that pin resolves **two copies** of `eggpool-wire` 0.1.0 and would give the workspace two distinct `WireSurface` types — breaking M011 `4's "no second wire-surface vocabulary" invariant while satisfying the plan's literal pinning instruction. WP-A therefore realigned CodeGG's `eggpool-wire` pin to `9ac6a131` so exactly one copy links; the diff between the two revs is additive and `codegg-providers` compiles and passes unchanged against it. `eggpool-model-routing` (`d70b5963`) depends only on `sha2` and needs no change. This also forced a deliberate change to the pre-existing `scripts/check_provider_wire_boundary.py`: its allowlist became an explicit `PERMITTED` set of neutral contract crates (each carrying its admitting evidence) that now *requires* both `eggpool-wire` and `eggpool-provider-profile`, while still rejecting every other `eggpool*` package. With both milestones closed, the predecessor implementation roadmap is technically complete. A separate post-closure corrective C001 was registered at `plans/subsystems/provider-connect-qualification-wire-dispatch-post-closure-corrective-addendum.md` as the strict merge/closure authority. **C001 has now closed** at `plans/closure/provider-connect-qualification-wire-dispatch-post-closure-corrective/001-status.md` (implementation `ce23b4e4`, closure `4a7567d4`; both hosted workflows green — `CI / verify` 37736136266 at 12386/12386 and Desktop E2E 37736136595 on the closure revision, and at the later plans-only tips `verify` 37737818615 plus Desktop E2E 37737818626 attempt 2): it reconciled this workstream's status text, added the one bounded durable connection→selection→OpenCode inference trajectory (`tests/opencode_go_connection_trajectory.rs`, negative-tested against both a single-surface-compatible OpenCode Go and a dropped durable selection), disposed both M011 low findings, and discharged the hosted root-CI gap this paragraph previously recorded. It did not reopen M010/M011 behavior. No registered downstream plan was unblocked by its closure, so the provider connection qualification / multi-surface workstream is now terminal with no active successor. Desktop E2E run 37737818626 attempt 1 was red on a **pre-existing projection defect outside this workstream** — a mid-turn resync installs a durable-only snapshot and advances the cursor past never-durable `TurnTextDelta` envelopes, permanently losing an already-rendered assistant message (`should_persist` excludes `TurnTextDelta` at `src/core/event_log.rs:44-47`). It is diagnosed with a full file:line chain in the C001 closure record and belongs to the desktop/projection subsystem, not here; attempt 2 of the same SHA is green.

**Project picker rework gate (closing; ADR-0033 accepted 2026-10-16):** M001–M005 are implemented and have closure records under `plans/closure/project-picker-rework/`. M005 enforces new directory↔project 1:1 bindings, preserves historical multi-root reads, shares startup/picker ensure-bound registration, and keeps working directories in client tab context.
