**User prompt queue and turn contention (opened 2026-10-17, baseline `18c3dca9`; ADR-0035, ADR-0036, ADR-0037, and ADR-0038 **all accepted** 2026-10-18; M004 closed, M005 written and closed):** At baseline a prompt composed while a turn is active was destroyed, and the same refusal path also cleared the active turn's identity and working age for a turn that was still running on the daemon. **M001 is closed** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/001-status.md`) — contention preserves the running turn, every recoverable refusal restores the composed text through one shared bounded shape, and the submit path now uses the shared request-state guard. **Outcome of the ADR-0035 acceptance (2026-10-18):** the durable decision is settled in favour of a daemon-owned, session-scoped, durable queue admitted only through ordinary `TurnSubmit`, which is correct — a frontend-local queue would lose every entry on reconnect and any reconnect-side replay would bypass the `request_id` receipt and the interrupted-no-replay rule that make at-most-once submission correct. But accepting it **did not simply clear M002**. Its clause "queue mutation is authorized by the same controller predicate that governs turn admission" is **unsatisfiable while a session is idle**: the durable lease is keyed `(session_id, turn_id)`, `acquire` rejects an empty `turn_id`, and `release` `DELETE`s the row, so `controller_lease_for` returns `None` between turns and `check_turn_controller` refuses every principal including the owner. The only idle-passing path is `is_controller_bypass` → `is_local_owner_broad`, an identity comparison against `LOCAL_OWNER_PRINCIPAL_ID` that is **not session-bound** and therefore cannot express ADR-0035's own refusal clause. Mutating a queue between turns is the primary use, so the authority model is a canonical ownership-boundary decision requiring its own ADR. **M002 is split into M002a (storage + read projection) and M002b (mutation operations + mutation authority).** ADR-0037 was raised as `proposed` and has since been **accepted**, selecting **Option B** — a durable, session-scoped queue-owner binding that exists while idle, survives restart, and changes only by explicit audited transfer. `is_controller_bypass` is explicitly not extended. Mid-turn the queue owner and the turn controller must both match the caller and all four mismatch combinations are typed refusals, never a precedence rule. **M002a is closed** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/002a-status.md`) and **M002b is now closed too** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/002b-status.md`). M002b landed **v81** carrying only `user_prompt_queue_owner`, gave the four mutations real producers and a durable authority, and kept `is_controller_bypass` out of queue authority entirely — its early return is guarded by `matches!(scope, ControllerScope::Turn { .. })`, proven by a test rather than by inspection. It **still delivered no user-visible capability**: no command, dialog, key, or projection field, so nothing a person can press reaches the six new operations. The adoption policy ADR-0037 deferred to M002b is settled and audited — **the calling principal becomes the owner** on first add. One correction is recorded inside ADR-0037 itself: its draft folded the owner binding into **v80**, but M002a had already shipped v80 with the queue table alone and a shipped migration is never edited, so the claim was corrected to **v81** while the ADR was still `proposed`, with the draft-versus-accepted difference stated in the accepted record's header. `run_at_ms` therefore moves to **v82**. M003's next action was taken: plan **M004**, for which M003's closure was the only stated blocker. **ADR-0036** has since been **accepted 2026-10-18 (Option A)**, which discharged the last ADR gate in this workstream; steering is *acceptance into the turn's channel, not delivery*, a failed steer keeps the entry in its original position, and "force" is forbidden in copy. `TurnSteer` already exists daemon-side and is already controller-gated, so M006 is frontend wiring rather than a new backend — but it is **best-effort** (`try_send` on a bounded channel), so `f` = "force" would overstate it, and ADR-0036 fixes the guarantee as acceptance and forbids "force" in copy. ADR-0036 also fixes terminology because a second, unrelated mechanism (`AgentLoop::drain_follow_up`, turn-boundary, agent-internal) already drains prompts; the two must never be conflated. **M002a and M002b are infrastructure and must not be reported as a user-visible queue** — M002a's read had no producer until M002b, and M002b adds no surface a user can reach. **M003 is planned and now `active`**; its blocker, `plans/adrs/ADR-0038-queue-delivery-execution-authority.md`, was raised by the M002b closure audit and **accepted 2026-10-18 selecting Option B**. Its own hard dependency — the M002b closure — is discharged, and planning it exposed the decision ADR-0035 and ADR-0037 both left open: **as whom** a queued entry is submitted. The daemon may originate an admission, but the repository's only such path (`submit_idle_continuation_turn`, ADR-0031) reconstructs authority from durable attribution via `AuthenticatedPrincipal::reconstructed`, which needs five fields, while the v81 owner row stores one (`owner_principal`). Delivering under a synthetic daemon identity would take the controller lease from the real owner and lock the owner out of its own queue at its first mid-turn mutation — a failure invisible until someone uses the feature that now works. ADR-0038's accepted **Option B** resolves attribution from team state with **no migration**, which **removes** the v82 contest rather than deciding it: `run_at_ms` keeps **v82** for M007 and M003 takes no migration number. Its stated trade is that `AuthMethod`/`TransportClass` become a **delivery classification** rather than a record of what originally authenticated the owner — a queue delivery has no originating connection, so a per-connection classification would be a fabrication. Six clauses are settled regardless of attribution source: submission **only** through `TurnSubmit`; a **deterministic** submission id derived from the entry id, so at-most-once is inherited from the existing receipt rather than reimplemented; a **named** entry restricted to exactly two guarded callsites; authority **re-authorized against current team state** at delivery time; delivery runs **as the queue owner**, never synthetic and never `is_controller_bypass`; and ADR-0036 untouched. The inherited v80 `status` `CHECK` question is **answered in the plan**: make all five values reachable rather than rebuild a durable user table. Research also found three projection behaviours that break the instant `delivering` has a producer — `list_live` sweeps *and* selects `status = 'pending'`, `check_capacity` counts only `pending`, and the expiry sweep covers only `pending` — so an unresolved `delivering` row is invisible, uncounted, never expired, and never retried; M003 treats startup reconciliation of `delivering` rows as correctness, not cleanup. Two further facts are recorded rather than assumed: the daemon has **no periodic session-observation loop**, and the queue has **no `CoreEvent`** (it reloads only on the session-change edge), so delivery needs its own trigger and a push path. **M003 is closed** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/003-status.md`) and **M004 is now closed** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/004-status.md`), the workstream's first user-operable milestone: `/queue` mounts a FocusManager-owned box, `j`/`k`/`r`/`d` are the only keys it advertises, a `delivering` row is listed and its refusal is reported as *being delivered* rather than *expired*, a delete applies the projection that delete produced with no second read, row 0 names the waiting count as a suffix that adds no row, and `/queue` is blocked for observers by omission. **M005 is now closed** (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/005-status.md`): the queue is authorable and correctable rather than append-only. `a` composes and commits on `Enter` with a client-minted `entry_id` (the protocol's at-most-once key, minted at commit so an abandoned step burns none), `e` revises the selected row seeded from its current text and never changes identity or ordinal, and `[`/`]` swap it with a neighbour. Two findings from its closure audit are recorded rather than assumed. First, the text-step guard is **structural**: `handle_key` matches `is_text_step()` first as a separate match and returns from it, so the browsing arm is unreachable while a composer is open — the per-letter `!is_text_step()` guard the plan asked for is the shape that eventually ships a sixth action with a forgotten guard, and the failure is `d` deleting a prompt the user was typing. Second, §3(b) is settled by **measurement**, and the answer is sharper than predicted: a released `delivering` entry resumes at the back of the queue *and keeps* the displacement ordinal (`USER_PROMPT_QUEUE_RENUMBER_BAND_OFFSET` = 1 000 000), sitting outside the dense band entirely, because a release restores the status and not the ordinal. It is recorded as accepted-and-documented; fixing `renumber_dense` reopens closed M002b/M003 and is a maintainer decision. A live bug was found by the close-during-mutation test — a shared refusal helper returned `None` for both success and stale, so a completion arriving after `close_dialog` cancelled it wrote its projection anyway; fixed with an explicit three-state outcome. M006 and M007 are the next unwritten milestones and have no remaining gate, and M008 remains deferrable. M008 remains registered but explicitly deferrable, and its deferral must be stated rather than implied. Source: `plans/subsystems/user-prompt-queue-roadmap.md`; ADRs `plans/adrs/ADR-0035-durable-user-prompt-queue-ownership.md`, `plans/adrs/ADR-0036-mid-turn-prompt-steering-delivery-contract.md`, `plans/adrs/ADR-0037-idle-session-queue-mutation-authority.md`, and `plans/adrs/ADR-0038-queue-delivery-execution-authority.md` (**all four accepted** 2026-10-18).

## Milestones eligible for fresh planning

| Subsystem | Milestone | Status | Implementation plan | Authority / handoff note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M004 core change verbs (`p`/`P` paste, bare `y`, `r`/`R` replace, `~` and case verbs) | **ready** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/004-core-change-verbs.md` | Depends on: M003 closed. Class: capability. Closes the missing basic edit loop — **yank exists today, paste does not exist at all**. Source evidence at baseline `84440fe7f`: `yank_editor_range` writes **only** to the system clipboard and there is no editor-local register, so `p` sources from `util::clipboard::read_from_clipboard` (already present, `arboard`-gated with a typed failure stub). Every verb routes through the single `submit_edit` → `apply_local` funnel. `r` must refuse on multi-byte characters rather than corrupting them — the M003 `read_bytes` boundary defect is the live hazard. `R` retention must not survive a buffer switch or reload. `c`/`Alt-c`, noyank, `>`/`<`, and `=` remain deferred by scope. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M005 textual motions and goto mode (`gs`, `W`/`B`/`E`, `gj`/`gk`, goto leaves) | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/005-textual-motions-and-goto-mode.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/005-status.md` | Depends on: M002 closed (goto prefix), M003 closed. Class: capability. `gs` removes the largest single omission: **there is currently no way to reach first non-whitespace** (`0` is the only line-start key). **Carried the second shared shape change:** `EditorPrefix::leaf()` returned a single `Option<char>`, which could not express `gg` plus more; it is now `leaves() -> &'static [PrefixLeaf]` with a `PrefixAction::{Motion, YankSelection}` action, because `space y` is not a motion and must hand a byte range back out. **Two corrections against the live Helix keymap, found at implementation time and recorded in the plan rather than silently absorbed:** `gl` is `goto_line_end`, **not** "last goto" (there is no jumplist here to repeat, so the planned one-slot `gl` would have been a dead key), and `gt` is `goto_window_top` — with `gc`/`gb` all three are **screen**-relative and are **deferred**, because motions resolve against `NOMINAL_VISIBLE_LINES`, a fixed guess rather than the real viewport. `gj`/`gk` are Helix's *textual* motions against `j`/`k`'s *visual* ones; with no soft-wrap model they coincide, so the leaves resolve to the same commands rather than to near-duplicates that could drift. `W`/`B`/`E` are whitespace-delimited, **not** `is_word_byte`-classified, and step on char boundaries so they cannot land mid-codepoint. `first_non_whitespace` inherits C002's bound: it scans at most `MAX_RENDER_LINE_BYTES` and such a line is already drawn truncated, so the answer is disclosed rather than silently wrong. **The desired column is kept, not re-derived** — horizontal moves re-seed it, vertical moves keep the column they aimed for (re-seeding from the short line's clamped landing column is the bug the field exists to prevent), and everything else clears it; it crosses `apply_helix_verb`'s throwaway probe, and `offset_on_line` clamps it so a stale value cannot land outside a line. **One existing test changed:** the M002 table test asserted `EditorPrefix::leaf()` directly, i.e. the old *shape* rather than `gg`/`space y` behaviour; its leaf column became the leaf **set** and every other asserted fact is unchanged. `gg` and `space y` behaviour tests passed unmodified. Every goto leaf resolves through the **raw** motion path, preserving `gg`'s existing collapse-selection behaviour. LSP goto leaves are M013/M010, not here. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M013 LSP read delivery and editor reachability | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/013-lsp-read-delivery-and-reachability.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/013-status.md` | Depends on: M005 closed (hard, for the leaf table), M002 closed (hard). Class: **invariant (delivery) + enabling capability (reach)**. **Corrective insertion, not a successor** — two defects verified at baseline `84440fe7f` make the existing F2–F9 path return `NotReady` in ordinary configurations. (1) `publish_changed_diagnostics` and `DIAGNOSTICS_POLL_INTERVAL` in `src/core/daemon_lsp.rs` have **zero callers**, and the only production write to `daemon.lsp_diagnostics` sits inside that uncalled function, so diagnostics are permanently `NotReady`. (2) The read path calls `find_existing_client_for_root_hint(Some(&root), None)`, which returns `Err` when **multiple** clients share a root, and that `Err` is mapped flatly to `NotReady` — so any polyglot workspace has F3–F9 silently dead. Fix is a daemon-owned publisher cadence plus optional `server_id` with explicit → extension-matched → unique → **typed ambiguity** resolution. Warm-only reads (ADR-0012 §3) and no app-side startup (ADR-0011) are invariants, not negotiable. Extends `LspReadOperation` with **read** variants plus rename/format **preview** only; write apply stays deferred pending an authorization ADR. **Must precede M010** — binding LSP leaves first ships keys that open always-`NotReady` panels. **M013 is closed** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/013-status.md`): the daemon now owns the diagnostics cadence (`start_lsp_diagnostics_driver`, ADR-0011), reads resolve explicit `server_id` → unique extension claimant → unique warm client → typed `Ambiguous`, and warm-only is **structural** rather than a pre-gate check — the resolved key is threaded into every query through `*_for_warm_client`, which required refactoring fourteen `egglsp` operations and the service's document-sync helpers, not just the daemon. `LspReadOperation` grew from six to twelve (declaration, implementation, document highlight, signature help, format **preview**, rename **preview**) plus a `new_name` field and an `Unsupported` status for a live server that lacks a capability — distinct from `NotReady`, because retrying cannot conjure an `advertised` provider. **Six further "correct code never reached" defects were found and fixed while implementing this, the same class as the two the plan recorded:** `CoreRequest::LspReadGet` had **no session-locator arm**, so every M006-B read failed `authorization_scope_required` before the resolver ran and the whole read surface was dead in production while its 14 tests passed (they called the daemon as `LocalOwner`, which bypasses scope resolution); no guard script covers that match, so a guard here is the top follow-up. Also: warm-only was a claim about two servers; diagnostics were keyed by `file_name()` so `src/lib.rs` and `tests/lib.rs` collided and two open projects recorded each other's files; point-read server errors were all mapped to `OutsideWorkspace`; signature-help, format-preview and rename-preview reached `get_or_create_client` three layers down through `ensure_file_open_from_disk` → `open_file`/`update_file`/`set_managed_document`; and capital leaves (`space S`) were dead on every terminal that reports a capital as a shifted lower-case glyph, despite `PrefixLeaf::glyph`'s own doc claiming otherwise. Helix leaves are bound in the M005 leaf tables with visible degradation; `space r`, `space a` and `=` stay **unbound** because applying an LSP-driven mutation has no accepted ADR. *(M015, 2026-10-19: that reason was wrong — ADR-0008 is accepted — and the two previews are now bound; what the editor lacked was the bridge to a reviewable artifact. See the M015 row and roadmap Amendment A2.)* **Residual registered, not discharged by M013.** `scripts/verify.sh full` runs but is **not green**: its nextest phase reports 69 failing tests in 11 `tests/`-integration binaries, and the script exits 100. These are **pre-existing and not M013's** — the change was stashed entirely and the same 11 binaries re-run at baseline `a8778e87c`, giving the **same 69 failing test names, identical name for name**, so M013 introduced zero new failures. Two causes: a tool constructed without a workspace root now refuses (`ToolError::MissingWorkspaceRoot` — the `workspace-authority-and-session-navigation` contract), and `/tmp` is a symlink on macOS so `workspace root '/tmp' must be an absolute, non-symlink path`. The owning workstream already recorded the same break and repaired it for the **lib** tests — *"47 lib tests broke … 44 were fixed by naming a root"* — but never repaired the **`tests/` integration** binaries. The repair is one line per helper (`.with_workspace_root(...)`, or the existing `make_tool_with_root` in `tests/lsp.rs`). It is left to that workstream rather than folded into an LSP-delivery milestone. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M011 insert-mode control chords | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/011-insert-mode-control-chords.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/011-status.md` | Closed 2026-10-19. The nine chords are bound with one owner each, the two mode-blind panels are fixed, and a typing burst is one undo step. The **`Ctrl+w`/`Ctrl+k`/`Ctrl+u` route-scoped shadow over the global session actions remains a product decision for the maintainer** — it is Helix's arrangement, scoped to Insert mode, and `Alt+Backspace`/`Alt+d` are offered as the alternative spelling. The `Ctrl+s`/`Ctrl+r` decision stands: both keep their mode-independent save/resync meaning, Helix's insert-mode meanings are declined, and the undo-checkpoint capability is delivered by insert-session coalescing instead. **The guard's Insert chord count is 8, not 9** — two of the nine are key-code claims and `Ctrl+x` lives in `handle_completion_key`, a stage the guard does not scan; the Normal count is unchanged at 66, which M012 pins. The plan's §6.3 prose contradicted its own table about `Tab`; the table won, and Helix's completion menu agrees with it. Three divergences from Helix are recorded, all the same decision — in Insert mode a printable is text, so `Enter` is a newline, `↑`/`↓` move the cursor, and a printable types rather than accepting the completion. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M012 undo history traversal (`Alt-u`/`Alt-U`) | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/012-undo-history-traversal.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/012-status.md` | Closed 2026-10-19. The history is **one ordered list with a cursor in it** — `history`, `history_bytes` and `history_cursor` on `TuiDocumentPresentation` — replacing the two stacks, so an entry never moves between containers and the *both directions* invariant is structural rather than a discipline. `Alt+u`/`Alt-U` walk that cursor and **report** their boundary ("no earlier change" / "no later change") in the editor's notice surface, while `u`/`U` stay silent at theirs as they always have — a **CodeGG convention, not a Helix behaviour**, recorded as one. The cursor's position is **observable**: the banner row reads `history <at>/<of>` while the cursor is off the tip and says nothing at the tip, so the ordinary case draws exactly what it drew before. The memory bound is now **one cap** over the history (256 entries, 4 MiB) rather than one per direction, so the worst case is **half** what the two-stack model could reach and the code finally matches the bound `architecture/tui.md` documents. **The guard's Normal-mode chord count is 69, not the 68 the plan recorded** — the plan counted the two *gestures* and the guard counts *chords*, and the two terminal spellings of `Alt-U` differ in the **key code**, not only in the modifier, so they need two arms and `Alt-u` is the third. **The plan's §6.9 claim that the key-authority guard "needs no change" was wrong**, and its own §6.4 snippet is what made it wrong: the old conflict rule keyed a claim by its *modifier names*, so exactly `ALT` and exactly `ALT \| SHIFT` looked like a duplicate although Rust's `==` on the bitflags says they are disjoint; the rule now compares **exact modifier bitmasks**, reads multi-modifier `let` bindings, and a tenth self-test case pins the model. A redo now restores the same recorded position an undo restores, because one entry carries one position — visible after `u` then `U`, and more symmetric than the old rebased redo stack. The dead `EditorCommand::Undo`/`Redo` variants and `EditorUndoEntry::rebased` were **deleted**, not wired: they had no producer, and an undo is not derivable from a snapshot the way `resolve_edit` derives an edit. Evidence: 2249 `tui::` lib tests pass; the guard reports 2/69/8 and its self-test is 10 cases; the closure records three guard injections and two source injections, each restored byte-identical. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M009 match mode and text objects | **closed** (byte-derived boundary) | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/009-match-mode-and-text-objects.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/009-status.md` | The M008 (multi-selection command surface) edge was discharged by the M008 closure. **The ADR-0034 gate is narrower than this cell previously said.** ADR-0034's own decision text states that *"Word and delimiter-pair text objects are implemented frontend-side from bytes and are explicitly **not** gated on this decision"*, its Option C is written as *"Land M001–M008, M010–M012 and their non-TS slices"*, and Helix's text-object table agrees independently (`w`, `W`, `p`, the specified surround pairs and `m` are parser-free; `f`, `t`, `a`, `c`, `T`, `x` need a grammar). This cell previously said *"Word and delimiter-pair text objects do **not** need the ADR and must not be gated behind it"* — that sentence was right and the status beside it was wrong, so the whole milestone was blocked by a dependency it does not have. The syntax-node slice moves to **M014**, which is blocked on ADR-0034 acceptance and has **no plan yet, deliberately**: ADR-0034 records that *"no option is selected by this record"*, and Option A (a frontend tree-sitter dependency) and Option B (daemon-owned structural reads) produce different contracts and would need different plans. Two further corrections recorded by the plan: Helix marks **`m m` as `TS`**, so CodeGG's `m m` is a deliberate **delimiter-pair table** divergence rather than a syntax-tree query; and Helix's `m` is a **two-level** grammar (`m` + command, then command + argument) while this editor's machine is one-prefix-one-terminal-key, so the sub-state rides in the `EditorPrefix` variant to keep the presentation's field count at one. **Closed 2026-10-19.** The `m` grammar landed whole — `m m` (bracket match), `m s`/`m d`/`m r` (surround family), and `m a`/`m i` (word, WORD, paragraph, delimiter-pair and closest-pair text objects) — and the status row shows which half of the grammar is pending. The bracket match is a **delimiter-pair table** match, the recorded local divergence from Helix's `TS`. The two-level grammar keeps the presentation at **one** prefix field: the sub-state rides in the `EditorPrefix` variant, so the prefix-state guard stays green. The key-authority guard's Normal-mode chord count is **70** — the `m` arming arm is one chord. The syntax-node slice (`f`, `t`, `a`, `c`, `T`, `x`) and a syntax-aware `m m` remain M014's, blocked on ADR-0034. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M010 space mode and extended goto mode | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/010-space-mode-pickers-and-clipboard-family.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/010-status.md` | Both blockers discharged: the M005 edge by the M005 closure on 2026-10-18, and the M013 edge by the M013 closure on 2026-10-19 (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/013-status.md`). Capability class. Planned 2026-10-19 against the live Helix keymap and the tree at `a8778e87c`. **M010 now owns the pickers and the clipboard family, not the LSP leaves** — M013 bound `space k`/`s`/`S`/`d`/`h`/`f`, `gd`/`gi`/`gr`, `gy` and `space F` as visible unavailability notices, and `]d`/`[d` diagnostic stepping. **One M013 binding is reclaimed:** `space f` is bound to `DocumentSymbols`, a **duplicate of `space s`**, because the workspace file browser was not reachable from the editor route at the time; the browser (`Dialog::Files`) exists and the route is what M010 fixes, so `space f` becomes the file picker — a **breaking change to a live binding**, recorded in the `CHANGELOG.md` under `### Changed`. **`space s` and `space S` are as the code has them** (document and workspace symbol pickers); this cell's earlier prose left them ambiguous. The plan binds `space p`/`P`/`Y`/`R` and `space ?`, and binds the seven leaves with no substrate to a **visible statement** rather than leaving them silently dead. The M013 pending-effect mechanism is **generalized** — it was LSP-typed, and a file picker is not an LSP fact. **Closed 2026-10-19 (Amendment A5).** The space menu landed whole: `space f` is the workspace file browser (reclaimed from the duplicate of `space s` — a **breaking change to a live M013 binding**, recorded in the `CHANGELOG.md` under `### Changed`), `space ?` is the editor's own `:` command palette, `space p`/`space P`/`space R`/`space Y` are the clipboard family (`space Y` takes **only** the main selection where `space y` takes the whole set), and seven leaves (`space F`, `space b`, `space j`, `space D`, `space c`, `space C`, `space '`) are bound to a **visible statement** of why they cannot run — no space key is silently dead. **The picker browses the *tab's* workspace, not the document's:** the attachment carries a `workspace_id` and a relative path and **no root**, and the daemon resolves the listing from the tab's canonical project/workspace binding (ADR-0022, C005), so `space f` checks that binding **before** opening and refuses with a reason rather than opening an empty browser. The seeding decision moved into a new `App::open_file_picker`, which `Dialog::Files` now delegates to, so `/files` and `space f` cannot disagree — and `/files` was **fixed** on the way, since it used to show a tree belonging to another workspace under this one's name. **A new guard polices the leaf tables**: `scripts/check_tui_editor_leaf_authority.py` (in `verify.sh quick` + CI `verify` job) fails on a duplicated glyph in one table, a glyph that is not printable ASCII, one glyph claimed by two tables of the same prefix, a leaf behind a guard-block chord, and a table no prefix binds; it reads the guard-block chords **through** the key-authority guard rather than re-parsing the dispatch chain. **The plan's own rule 4 was wrong as written** — it forbade a leaf glyph equal to a guard-block chord's *glyph*, and the guard block's glyphs are `s` and `r`, with `space s` live — so it is implemented as the **chord** a leaf can resolve to; and the plan's rule 3 is implemented as the prefix's **union** across its tables. **The M013 leaf test lost exactly one row, by design:** the plan required those tests to pass unmodified *and* required the reclaim, and the `f` row asserted the old binding, so the row was **removed** — `space f` is no longer an LSP read at all — and the other thirteen M013 leaf tests are unmodified. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M015 LSP preview leaves | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/015-lsp-preview-leaves.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/015-status.md` | Depends on: M013 closed (hard) — **discharged**. Capability class. **A milestone added by the M010 planning, from a gap the plans exposed rather than from preference.** M013 shipped `LspReadOperation::FormatPreview` and `LspReadOperation::RenamePreview` as read-only operations, with daemon dispatch, warm-client plumbing, protocol bounds and panel renderings — and **no key reaches either of them**. They were capabilities a reader could not invoke. A preview is a **read**, so it needs no LSP-write ADR; only the **apply** does, which is why M010's A2 amendment reserves M010 for "the **pickers** and the clipboard family, not the LSP leaves". **Closed 2026-10-19 (roadmap Amendment A1).** The milestone binds `space r` (the rename **preview**, with the new name collected through the `:` command line, the M008 precedent) and `=` (the format preview), and binds `space a` to a visible statement because no code-action **read** exists to preview. **Nothing is applied** — the M013 acceptance criterion 7 changed in its *unbinding* half only, and there is still no LSP write path. The **preview/apply transition is registered now**, in the roadmap, in the closure record and in the `CHANGELOG.md`: when the editor's preview becomes a reviewable artifact, `=` and `space r` become the apply step, and that meaning change must not land silently. **The gap is the bridge, not an ADR:** ADR-0008 is accepted and owns the LSP write path (`CoreRequest::LspPreviewApply`, `via_session` + `file.modify`, applying a registered still-current preview by id); what the editor's chords produce is a *read result* — no preview id, no patches, no `register_preview_artifact` in the editor path — so they cannot reach it. **Nothing is applied.** The key-authority guard's Normal-mode chord count is **71** — one for the `=` arm; `=` is a Normal chord with no prefix, so it is reported through the new `BufferKey::LspRead(LspReadOperation)` arm rather than through `pending_effect`, whose slot is documented as *a prefix leaf's* effect. The leaf table grew by two glyphs (`r`, `a`), so the leaf-authority guard now reports 5 tables / 44 glyphs / 12 bindings. The `:` palette gained a command (`rename-preview`), so it now has 13 rows. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M016 the bridge: a reviewable preview | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/016-preview-apply-bridge.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/016-status.md` | Depends on: M015 closed (hard) — **discharged**; ADR-0008 accepted (2026-09-20), so **no new ADR** is written. Capability class. **This is the transition M015 registered and deliberately did not land:** *"when the editor's preview becomes a reviewable artifact ADR-0008's apply path can consume, `=` and `space r` stop showing what would change and start doing it"* (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/015-status.md` §3.5). **The candidate is carried, not registered.** "Register the editor's preview so the existing apply path can find it" is not available: `LspReadGet` registers nothing, no protocol request lists or exports a preview, and the TUI's own `app.lsp_tool` registry is `None` in socket/remote mode (`src/main.rs:3454`) and empty in production. The read result therefore carries the candidate and the TUI relays it into the apply request — which works in **both** transports and adds no daemon state. **The apply path needs no registry either:** `apply_preview` verifies a digest recomputed over the request's *own* patches (`src/lsp/mutation.rs:380-401`) and revalidates every `original_hash` against the file on disk, so a candidate altered in transit fails the check. **The material already exists and is discarded:** the rename preview's per-file `patch` and `original_hash` are computed by `egglsp` and dropped at `src/core/daemon_lsp.rs:664-667`. The milestone adds the protocol's candidate DTO, the daemon's minting of it, the editor carrying it, the change-review surface opening on it, and the buffer reload after a successful apply — through the **one** existing apply path, which is widened, not duplicated. **The dirty-buffer refusal stands** and is not this milestone's to change. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M017 the code-action read: `space a` previews instead of stating | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/017-code-action-read.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/017-status.md` | Closed 2026-10-19. Depends on: M016 closed (hard) — the candidate and the bridge are its machinery; ADR-0012's read family, whose "read-only by construction" note is **corrected in place, not deleted** (a *preview*'s payload is a diff; the raw action's payload is an edit). Capability class. `space a` is the last leaf that names a thing it cannot do, and its reason was true: the protocol had no operation that asks a server what code actions it would offer. It has one now. **One request, many previews**, and the shape is **measured** rather than asserted: the egglsp integration test reads the fake server's own transcript and asserts it saw exactly one `textDocument/codeAction`, so there is no index to go stale between two requests — the index hazard the model-facing `codeActionPreview` already carries. **The panel is the picker**: `j`/`k` select, `Enter` opens the change review on the selected action's candidate — and the review does **not** open by itself, because a code action is a choice and the reader has not made it yet. The candidates in one result are bounded **together** (2 MiB against a 1 MiB per-candidate bound), and the reason an action carries no candidate is **per action** (`LspCodeActionUnavailable`), because a result-wide flag cannot say which action the bound cut. A command-bearing action is refused **whole** — edit or not — since the LSP specification applies an action's edit *and then* its command, and applying half of an action is not applying it; a disabled action is refused with the server's own reason. The chord and leaf counts did not move: **2 / 71 / 8** and **5 / 44 / 12**. |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M018 the completion-trigger guard: the `Ctrl-x` chord becomes visible | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/018-completion-trigger-guard.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/018-status.md` | Closed 2026-10-19. Depends on: nothing — this milestone changed **no behaviour**. Infrastructure class. It answers roadmap §13.4 question 5 (*"Does M011's `Ctrl-x` need a guard?"*): **yes**, and the answer is a **separate** guard, for the same reason M010's leaf guard is a separate one — the completion popup is a surface *above* the dispatch chain, with a modality the key guard's model does not have (a claim that is conditional on a surface being open). `scripts/check_tui_editor_completion_authority.py` reads the guard-block chords **through** `scripts/check_tui_editor_key_authority.py` rather than re-parsing the chain, so the two guards cannot disagree about what the guard block claims. The triggers and the popup's own keys are told apart **structurally** — the triggers are the arms **before** the popup-open check — because a guard that counted both would report `j` and `k` twice and call it a defect. Four invariants: the `F8` trigger exactly once, the `Ctrl-x` trigger exactly once, no trigger shadowing the guard block's `Ctrl+s`/`Ctrl+r`, and `Ctrl-x` in **Insert mode only** — checked from the arm's predicate, because a comment is not a mechanism. A shape the guard cannot resolve is an **error** (exit 2), not a skip. The key guard's counts are untouched: **2 / 71 / 8** and **5 / 44 / 12** |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M019 the open-line verbs: `o` and `O` open a line | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/019-open-line-verbs.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/019-status.md` | Closed 2026-10-20. Depends on: nothing hard — M001 and M004 are closed and the milestone moves no chord. Capability class. `o` and `O` were **bound and inert**: the code that opens the line was written, unit-tested, and unreachable from the key path, so both keys dropped the reader into `INS` on the line they were already on — and `docs/tui.md` had been describing the working behaviour the whole time, which is how the defect was found. The two arms now go through the **shared edit funnel** (`editor_open_line`), so the line they open is a real document edit with the read-only gate `d`/`D`/`x` use, and a read-only attachment refuses them and says so. `submit_edit` now returns whether the controller **accepted** the transaction, which is the only honest answer to "did the document take this edit?" — a mode transition decided on a rejected transaction would leave the editor in `INS` over an unchanged document. Two further defects were found by the milestone's own dispatch-path tests and fixed: the open-line cursor was **one byte past** the line it opened, so the reader's typing went to the line *after* it; and the open-line edit was being folded into the typing run it cannot continue, which split the word across two entries. A typing run now starts only where the **cursor** ends — the question the run model already asked of every later edit — so the line and the word are **two** undo steps and the word is one step however many keystrokes it took. The chord and leaf counts did not move: **2 / 71 / 8** and **5 / 44 / 12** |
| TUI editor Helix keybinding parity | M020 the binding-set coherence guard, and §11's amended bullet | **closed** | `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/020-binding-set-coherence-guard.md`; closure `plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/020-status.md` | Closed 2026-10-20. Depends on: nothing hard — the key guard and the leaf guard are closed, and this guard reads their **models** rather than re-parsing the dispatch. Infrastructure class; **no production code changed**. The roadmap's §11 binding bullet named three surfaces and got all three wrong: the help overlay it names (`src/tui/components/help_overlay.rs`) is **unreferenced dead code**, the `:` palette is a *command registry* rather than a keymap, and `docs/tui.md` is hand-maintained prose that **nothing compared to the code** — so the bullet could not be true, and could not be false either. §11 now carries a dated **Amendment A1** narrowing it to the surfaces that exist and naming the two exceptions, and `scripts/check_tui_editor_doc_drift.py` makes the amended bullet mechanical: every leaf gesture and every claimed chord the code owns is documented, every key the document declares unbound **is** unbound, and a document with no unbound declaration at all is a defect rather than a clean pass. The document's two "not bound" lists gained an HTML marker so the claim is checkable in both directions. Registered in `scripts/verify.sh quick` and in the CI `verify` job, with an eight-case self-test and a six-injection proof on the real tree. The key guard's counts are untouched: **2 / 71 / 8** and **5 / 44 / 12** |

| User prompt queue | M007 delayed delivery — the wakeup owner | **Closed (was: blocked on one named decision, not on a closure).** The blocker was that the roadmap scoped M007 against an **existing periodic observation point that does not exist**: the daemon has no periodic session-observation loop (its three `tokio::time::interval` loops are projection retention, opt-in provider refresh, and opt-in TTS), and `submit_queued_prompt_turn` had exactly two permitted callers — the post-terminal wake and the bounded restart twin — neither of which fires for an idle session holding a scheduled entry. Enforcing due-ness in the store query alone makes an entry deliver **late, or not at all**. **Discharged 2026-10-18**: the decision of who owns the wakeup was taken as **Option A**, a bounded queue-scoped tick that parks when nothing is scheduled, recorded in `plans/adrs/ADR-0039-queue-advancement-wakeup-authority.md` and **accepted 2026-10-18**; M007 then closed (`plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/007-status.md`). `PERMITTED_CALLERS` in `scripts/check_queue_delivery_callsites.py` went from two to three as a recorded decision, and `--self-test` injects a **fourth** and proves it is still rejected. The blocking `render_in_panel` default flip is **withdrawn, not deferred** — the host passes `frame.area()` (border cells included) to `render_in_panel`, so an inset-by-one default would not stop a widget painting its own `Block`, it would only move it one character inward; it does not reduce the override count, while `Component::render` remains the modal path where a border is correct, giving it whole-tree blast radius for marginal gain. The per-widget override is the fix. | User prompt queue | M006 `/queue` send-now: steering the running turn | closed | `plans/closure/user-prompt-queue/006-status.md` | Class **capability**; **no migration** — `STORAGE_LAYOUT_VERSION` stays 81 and v82 stays M007's. `s` hands a queued prompt to the turn that is already running. The request carries `entry_id` alone; the handover is gated by the turn controller and **not** by `ControllerScope::QueueMutation`, with queue ownership re-asked inside the settlement transaction. `mark_steer_delivered` is the second `delivered` path, guarded on `pending` so a row M003 already claimed is never settled twice. Four outcomes, not two: `Accepted`, `AcceptedNotSettled`, `Refused { code }`, `EntryNotFound` — the third state exists because reporting it as an error would invite the user to resend a prompt that already ran. Six outcomes get six distinct sentences and none claims more than acceptance into the steering channel. Both §10 guards proven non-vacuous by injection |
| TUI subagent and job visibility convergence (corrective) | C007 send-to-subagent | closed | `plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/005-status.md` | Plan `plans/implementation/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/005-send-to-subagent.md`. ADR-0041 **Option A** as planned. `/subagent-send` arms the composer to one running subagent (bare = focused tab, `<name>`/`<task id>` explicit, `off` clears); the badge reads `[TO Sally]` and the placeholder says what the prompt is for. An addressed reply issues **one** `TurnSteer { session_id, turn_id }` where `turn_id` is `session_state.active_turn_id` read at **send** time — the subagent is named inside the payload, never used as the turn id. `InputTarget` did **not** grow a variant (ADR-0041, *Neutral or deferred*) and **no key** is bound, so the I3/I6 guards are untouched. Invalidated three ways, all from evidence: arming refuses a finished or detached run; `App::apply_subagent_rows` re-checks on **every** snapshot and clears when the run left the tree *or* is present but no longer running (presence is not liveness); and `TurnStarted` drops it. Both `set_rows` sites route through the one funnel. **34 tests** added (20 unit, 8 render/lifecycle, 6 request-shape) and all five proved non-vacuous by an injected defect. Three things the plan did not have: the parent model has **no name pool**, so the payload carries name + agent + task description or `Sally` is unresolvable; a steer is **never stored** (it reaches the loop's `pending_steer` and nothing else), so a refused reply's text is carried back on `TuiCommand::SubagentSteerFinished` and restored into an empty composer, and the TUI writes **no** transcript message for an accepted one; and `send_now_outcome_message` could not be reused verbatim because its accepted sentence claims a queue write a bare steer never makes — it now takes a `SteerConsequence`, so one vocabulary serves M006 and C007. M006's own sentences changed and its three tests were updated, not weakened. No CI receipt and no runtime observation. |

| TUI subagent and job visibility convergence (corrective) | C003 `/agents` opens agents | closed | `plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/006-status.md` | Class **capability**; implementation committed at `e8a6b5ca2`. Bare `/agents` opens `Dialog::Agent` and the panel picker it used to open was **deleted outright** — `open_panel_picker` has zero occurrences in `src/`. That is the correct outcome: the addendum forbade "routing around it by relabelling the picker". The milestone's stop condition was **never hit**, because `Dialog::Agent` already existed as `/agent`'s surface, so no new dialog was built. Closing it found two things. The test file's module doc still described the retired picker in detail — "the picker opens on the active panel, `j` walks the registry" — while the tests directly below it drove `/panels`. And `agents_and_agent_open_the_same_surface` was **vacuous**: both `/agents` and `/agent` resolve through the completion popup's highlighted row, so the test passed unchanged when bare `/agents` was repointed at `Dialog::Help`. That test was **deleted rather than rewritten**: a replacement was attempted and withdrawn. The assertion that matters (`/agent <name>` changes the selected agent, reachable only via the leading-token fallback) cannot be driven from an integration test — tracing shows the composer receives `/agent <name>` correctly, but the popup's rows still win at `Enter`, their label resolves to no command, and the resolver falls through to “send it as a prompt”. Whether the popup should match a query carrying its own argument is a **product question for the command-palette routing milestone**, not a missing assertion. **C003 has no implementation plan** (D-8 governance gap); no retrospective plan was written. No CI receipt and no runtime observation.
| TUI subagent and job visibility convergence (corrective) | C006 node lifetime when there is no active turn | closed (**answered and escalated — residual not fixed**) | `plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/007-status.md` | Class **capability**; reducer behaviour committed at `e8a6b5ca2`. **The answer is yes: a subagent can start with no active parent turn**, so the reducer's drop is a real defect and C006 routes to the addendum's *fix* branch. `AppEvent::SubagentStarted` is published unconditionally at the top of `run_subagent_task_with_cancel` (`src/agent/worker.rs`), *before* the `tokio::select!` that observes `scheduler_cancel`/`cancel_token`/`lineage_token` — so already-cancelled tokens cannot suppress it. Two reachable paths: a turn stopped while its subagent request is still queued on `request_tx` (the worker later publishes `SubagentStarted` then `SubagentFailed` against a terminal turn), and `MAX_CHILD_TIMEOUT` expiry in `specialized_runtime.rs:193`, which drops the awaiting future while the worker keeps running. The reducer test's stated rationale — "not a turn a subagent can start under" — was **false** and is corrected; its assertions are unchanged, because that behaviour is still what the code does and still what the fix must change. Retaining such a node needs a home outside `active_turn.agent_tree`, which is the addendum's **stop condition**, escalated to parent **D5** and an ADR. Today's drop is bounded (diagnostics cap at `MAX_PROJECTION_DIAGNOSTICS` = 32); the only cost is that the subagent is invisible in the TUI. **No CI receipt and no runtime observation** — the two paths are established by reading the code, not by triggering a live cancellation.

| Provider connect qualification / multi-surface post-closure corrective | C001 terminal reconciliation and hosted qualification | closed | `plans/closure/provider-connect-qualification-wire-dispatch-post-closure-corrective/001-status.md` | Baseline `ce23b4e4`. M011's hosted-CI low finding was not merely paperwork: no test proved a **persisted** OpenCode Go connection plus a **persisted** model selection reaches the correct multi-surface runtime, because M011's tests started at the provider and M010's at durable health. `tests/opencode_go_connection_trajectory.rs` closes that join — durable connection with a real encrypted `CredentialStore` binding → bounded catalog → `update_selection` → `durable_selected_runtime_model` → `ConnectionManager::resolve_with_runtime_reference` over the real `ProviderConnectionFactory` at the pinned revision → captured inference → M010 revision-scoped verdict → secret-absence in DB/protocol/request/credential file. Three tests; negative-tested against both required regressions (single-surface-compatible OpenCode Go: caught 2/3; dropped durable selection: caught 3/3). The only new API is a test-only capture seam behind `codegg-providers` `capture-test-support`, enabled via `[dev-dependencies]` only: `nm` finds **0** seam symbols in the built `codegg` binary and **4** in the `cfg(test)` rlib, so it cannot reach a shipped artifact. An earlier process-global version of the seam was **rejected** during implementation because it raced across concurrently running tests. WP-B: both EggPool crates still resolve to `9ac6a1318e8db3c034b5ab54987317752d5ffea6` with exactly one `eggpool-wire` identity and nothing repinned. WP-D: `eggpool-wire`'s stale OpenCode hint rows are upstream data debt with no CodeGG reader (CodeGG uses only `ir`/`codec`/`profile::WireSurface`/stream APIs), and `ProviderProfile::runtime_capabilities` has **zero** references in CodeGG — both non-blocking, no action. Evidence: `cargo fmt --check`; workspace Clippy `-D warnings`; `verify.sh quick` exit 0; 242 `codegg-providers` lib tests (20 `opencode_go`, 12 `provider_profile`, 21 `wire::`); M010 regressions 28 + 3; `check_provider_multi_surface_dispatch.py` 6/6; `check_provider_qualification.py` 4/4. Hosted CI **observed** for the first time on this workstream: both workflows green on the closure revision `4a7567d4` on the first attempt (`CI / verify` run `37736136266`, 12386/12386, 5 skipped, 0 failed; Desktop E2E run `37736136595`), with earlier green runs `37734112906` and `37730586250`/`37730586236` on the two immediately preceding SHAs, which differ only in `plans/` documents, via PR #105 (opened only to obtain hosted evidence; **not merged**). Three pre-existing runner-sensitive flakes surfaced hosted outside this corrective's blast radius (`goal::checkpoint` tail ordering, `codegg-client` `session_projection_driver`, and one `apps/desktop` `App.test.tsx` case); the 12-commit branch never touched those paths, the desktop flake failed a *different* test in the same suite on re-run and then passed cleanly on the next push — timing, not logic. All are recorded in the closure record for separate triage rather than claimed as fixed. No registered downstream plan was unblocked — a full `plans/` sweep found no plan declaring C001, this roadmap, M010, or M011 as a dependency, and the Blocked-work table has no row naming this workstream. The provider connection qualification / multi-surface workstream is now terminal. |

Containment reuses `validate_target_path` and the root comes from the daemon session row, never the request. `src/lsp/mutation.rs` and `scripts/` are absent from the diff. | TUI subagent and job visibility convergence (corrective) | C001 `Ctrl+T` consolidation and reachability | closed | `plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/001-status.md` | Class **capability** delivering I3 and new invariant **I6**; no storage or protocol change. `Ctrl+T` is now the single key for todos + subagents + jobs and reaches the overlay from `InputMode::Insert`, the mode the app boots in — the mode gate *disappeared* because a CONTROL chord is unambiguous, not because it was relaxed. Retiring `Shift+T` is load-bearing: keeping it would have forced `ToggleTasksList` to stay `is_normal_only` and reintroduced the Insert-mode bug that entry exists to prevent. Recorded deviation **D-C9**: opening the overlay borrows Normal mode (`App::tasks_restore_mode`) because `j`/`k`/`Enter`/`Esc` are Normal-mode gestures by contract — the rebind alone shipped an overlay that opened and then ignored every navigation key, a second instance of the same "the feature is dead" defect. **Verification is local, no CI receipt and no runtime observation** (the TUI was never launched). |
| TUI subagent and job visibility convergence (corrective) | C002 `/sessions` gains Subagents + Jobs sections | closed | `plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/002-status.md` | Class **capability** K2, reversing D-C4; no storage or protocol change. Reverses the recorded defect where M004 landed, passed its tests, and was true — and unreachable from `/sessions`, because the sections had landed in the *side panel* titled `SESSIONS` while `/sessions` opens the flat `Dialog::Session` switcher. The plan's stop condition was **avoided, not hit**: `App::session_dialog_pending_rows` consumes the existing `App::task_rows()`, so "is this live?" still has exactly one implementation (`build_task_rows`) and no second derivation exists. Highest-risk detail was the **two instances** — `open_dialog` pushes a *clone* and `render_dialog` paints it, so a fill touching only `dialog_state.session_dialog` passes every App-owned test and shows nothing. Jobs are read-only: `Enter` is consumed rather than inventing a destination. **Verification is local, no CI receipt and no runtime observation.** Unblock audit: C005 became newly plannable. |
| TUI subagent and job visibility convergence (corrective) | C004 projection-to-row coverage | closed | `plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/004-status.md` | Class **invariant**; **no production change** — this closure added two tests and nothing else, and proved both non-vacuous by injection. **The chain was covered link by link but not at two of its eight links.** The `AppEvent → CoreEvent` mapping (`src/core/mod.rs`) had **no** test — it is the hop the **default** daemon mode depends on, and its test module covered `TestRun*`/`SessionUpdated` but not `SubagentStarted`, so deleting `display_name` from that one match arm would have removed **every name in the default configuration** with the whole suite green. The serde wire round-trip had only the **absent** direction covered (`a_node_without_descriptors_decodes_with_them_absent`); nothing covered the **present** one, though `TuiMessage::ProjectionSnapshot` (`codegg-protocol/src/tui.rs:128`) really does ship the snapshot to the frontend over the WebSocket, so a `#[serde(skip)]` on `display_name` would also have been invisible. Both new tests were run against a real injection of the defect they exist to catch — `#[serde(skip)]` and `display_name: None` respectively — and each failure **named the dropped field**; both suites returned green after restore. **No defect was found in the additive-field chain**, so C005's conditional re-review is discharged rather than pending. `cargo test -p codegg-protocol --lib projection` reports **94** against 93 — the delta is the new wire test, and no other suite's count moved. **Verification is local: no CI receipt and no runtime observation — the TUI was never launched.** **C004 has no implementation plan**: it was built against the addendum's boundary, which is governance gap **D-8** repeating inside the corrective workstream, and **no retrospective plan was written** — a plan authored after the fact is not the specification the work was built against. Unblock audit: **nothing new** — C006 was never gated on a C004 *closure* and C007 depends on C005, not C004. |
| TUI subagent and job visibility convergence (corrective) | C005 agent descriptors | closed | `plans/closure/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/003-status.md` | Class **capability** K3, delivering invariant **I1**; no storage or protocol change. The curated pool's `profile` and `usefulness` are now reachable: drawn once at spawn by `SubagentNameLease::entry()` (bound to the lease's **own** name), carried on `AppEvent::SubagentStarted`, copied verbatim by the projection adapter and the replay publication path, and read off `SubagentRow`. Reusing `MAX_PROJECTION_STRING_BYTES` was **rejected**: a node already carries 4 KB of `description`, so the milestone names its own `MAX_PROJECTION_DESCRIPTOR_BYTES = 256` (measured pool max 223 B) with a derived 8,192 B per-turn budget. The plan's three traps were each handled as recorded: the two "Not rendered by the TUI today" lies were corrected **in the generator** (they are emitted by it, and `--check` passes on two identical lies), the desktop bridge was **deliberately not** widened because `check-bindings.mjs` only asserts fields it lists and cannot see the new ones, and the TUI-side-pool boundary is enforced by `check_tui_subagent_name_pool_isolation.py`, proven non-vacuous both by its `--self-test` and by a real injected violation. **Verification is local, no CI receipt and no runtime observation.** Unblock audit: C007's other hard dependency is now closed, so C007 had no outstanding blocker; its plan was written at `plans/implementation/tui-subagent-and-job-visibility-convergence/005-send-to-subagent.md` and C007 has since been **implemented and closed** (`005-status.md`). |

| Managed scratch native qualification (post-closure corrective) | C001 Linux Required scratch containment | closed | `plans/closure/managed-agent-scratch-native-qualification-corrective/001-status.md` | Implementation `2597ec9e` with the fixture corrections `ea400ff7` and `83fc052f`; plan `plans/implementation/managed-agent-scratch-native-qualification-corrective/001-linux-native-scratch-containment-qualification.md`. Class **invariant**; **no migration** — no schema, protocol or configuration change. Native Linux evidence from the repository's own `CI` workflow, run [`39298041771`](https://github.com/dbowm91/codegg/actions/runs/39298041771) on `ubuntu-latest` at the exact source SHA: 14,813/14,813 workspace tests green, including 10/10 of the new C001 cases. Three earlier dispatched runs did not qualify — `39289232898` was cancelled, `39291133332` failed on a defect in the fixture itself, and `39294747903` failed on an unrelated pre-existing `codegg-core` flake after the fixture had already run 10/10 green — and the two fixture defects were in the test, not the product. The delegated shared-scratch grant path is qualified on a real Landlock enforcement: the run-private root and the explicitly granted shared root are writable, while an ungranted sibling, another session's run-private root, the scratch parent, an unrelated host temporary directory, a pre-existing file outside the granted roots, a cross-boundary hard link or rename, and a symlink planted inside the workspace are all refused by the kernel. The **obtained** status is asserted (Landlock, ABI >= 3, `no_new_privs`) on the same launch whose child behaviour is observed, and a defect-injection control grants the ungranted sibling artificially and observes the difference, so the denials are not vacuous. The fixture is a whole-file `cfg(target_os = "linux")` target and a Linux host that cannot obtain Landlock **fails** it rather than skipping it, so an unavailable qualification can never be counted as a satisfied one. The launch spec handed to the managed process names only the workspace: every scratch write root is composed by the production owner from the durable grant snapshot, so the test cannot have written the answer itself. Filesystem dimension only — no process, IPC or network claim, and no Windows claim. The original M003 stays historically conditional for its **Windows** half; the Linux half is now qualified and is recorded as a factual correction at `plans/closure/managed-agent-scratch/003-status.md`. Unblock audit: C001's only dependency was operational, no registered plan in *Blocked work* listed a native Linux Landlock host as its blocker, and the Windows Required backend stays blocked in its owning roadmap — this closure unblocks nothing, and that is recorded rather than left implicit. |

Historical closure records MUST NOT be rewritten to conceal predecessor defects or failed verification. Corrective/new-evidence passes own new closure evidence rather than editing history.

## Verification policy

Agent-workstream-blackboard plans permit focused deterministic group/SQLite/CAS/Git/scheduler/projection/security tests and optional manually triggered model-backed performance measurements; they MUST NOT add another scheduler, unrestricted sibling chat, shared writes without accepted ADR-0032 and complete execution fencing, a permanent benchmark gate, a required external compiler-cache service, implicit child merge, or an SBC compile/startup benchmark.

Verification remains deliberately light. Newly registered milestones may add focused unit/integration tests or a narrow static guard where it enforces a real ownership invariant, but they MUST NOT add new CI lanes, scanners, coverage/benchmark/binary-size gates, dependency bots, release automation, or fixed release cadence.

The CI/test-throughput roadmap may use temporary Cargo/Nextest timing output, comparable hosted-run timings, and cache statistics as implementation/closure evidence. It MUST keep one routine CI job, MUST NOT introduce a permanent performance gate or benchmark artifact pipeline, and MUST NOT obtain speedups by deleting assertions or silently dropping authoritative Linux/live qualification. Integration-target consolidation must preserve test semantics and resource/feature boundaries; compiler/cache candidates are retained only on measured positive evidence. The post-closure C001 corrective may temporarily add a supported sccache GitHub-Actions-cache probe and ephemeral measurement PR/branch, but the final tree must either retain only a measured-positive cache configuration or fully revert it; no external cache secret/service is permitted.

The Project Work Orders and Task View roadmap may add deterministic SQLite/restart, release-gate, sequence-CAS, managed-worktree, trigger-replay, authorization/privacy, and bounded TUI/tool trajectory fixtures. It MUST NOT add live-provider/network CI, a second scheduler/background loop, a generic workflow/BPM engine, an unbounded recurrence language, a new authorization framework, or a permanent chaos/benchmark framework. External-trigger tests should use loopback/in-process fixtures; M007 should reuse existing deterministic test infrastructure rather than create a broad new verification harness. The UX-fidelity corrective C001 may add focused keybinding/composer, scheduling-queue, one-time-trigger-secret, stale-response/recovery, and trigger-management TUI tests while reusing M003/M005/M007 suites and existing trigger/ownership guards; it MUST NOT add a new UI harness framework, secrets framework, public-network test, or CI lane.

The long-horizon work roadmap may add deterministic WorkPlan/Goal/Todo/context-transition/restart scenarios and force small context limits with scripted providers. It MUST NOT add live-provider CI, a second history/compaction store, a generic workflow engine, or an unbounded plan dump/benchmark gate.

The execution-reliability/approval roadmap may add deterministic provider fault streams, retry/side-effect fixtures, permission/reviewer/sandbox matrices, preference restart tests, and the existing supported-Linux sandbox fixture. It MUST NOT add live-provider CI, a permanent chaos service, a new authorization engine, a second scheduler, a new cross-platform sandbox framework, or network-containment claims without an actual backend.

The dependency security/workspace roadmap may use `cargo audit`, `cargo tree -d`, reverse dependency trees, feature trees, package dry-runs, and `cargo bloat` as temporary local/closure evidence. It MUST NOT turn advisory status, duplicate counts, package counts, or artifact size into new continuous CI gates. M001 may update an existing explicit audit ignore only when reachability/applicability evidence changes. M002 must prove feature equivalence rather than centralizing maximal feature unions. M004 may run `cargo package` dry-runs but MUST NOT publish automatically. M005 remains blocked until its external package interface exists. M006 may perform only targeted Rustls/required-companion updates and focused Eggfetch/provider/TLS-consumer verification; it MUST NOT add an advisory ignore, broad lockfile update, TLS framework, or public-network CI test.

The HTTP client consolidation roadmap may use deterministic loopback HTTP/TLS/SSE fixtures plus temporary `rg`/`cargo tree` dependency censuses for closure evidence. It MUST NOT add network-dependent CI, a permanent dependency scanner, a binary-size threshold, or a generic HTTP abstraction solely for verification.

The upstream tool-surface corrective may use deterministic modern/legacy MCP fixtures and one optional local real-binary smoke for closure evidence. It MUST NOT add a permanent compatibility matrix, scheduled upstream smoke, network-dependent CI check, duplicate search cache, or duplicate progressive-discovery framework.

Repository-surface M001 may repair the existing project-catalog guard and use temporary census commands for documentation review, but it MUST NOT introduce a permanent docs-lint framework or network-dependent CI check.

The context-continuity roadmap may add focused deterministic checkpoint-store, compaction, restart, cancellation, and repeated-trajectory tests. Its closure may force small effective context limits with local fake providers, but MUST NOT add live-provider/network CI, a new benchmark gate, a vector-history service, or another permanent verification framework.

Normal broad local posture remains:

```text
cargo fmt --all -- --check
cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings
scripts/verify.sh quick
```

Hosted `CI / verify` is closure evidence only where the existing closure convention or an exact operational condition requires it.

## Deferred unregistered product work

These remain outside active handoff unless concrete product priority/evidence makes them dependency-ready:

- distribution expansion beyond the closed Linux/macOS binary+installer slice, including Homebrew/deb/rpm/Nix, Windows installer support, signing/notarization, SBOM/provenance and package-manager automation;
- expanding Windows from opportunistic compatibility to a guaranteed support tier;
- full web/mobile frontends and graphical IDE/editor expansion remain deferred; shared M005 document/buffer foundation is strictly closed under `plans/closure/editor-document-foundation-post-closure-corrective/001-status.md`; the M006 TUI IDE presentation audit is complete at `plans/subsystems/desktop-frontend-ide-foundation-m006-tui-presentation-audit.md` and sequences TUI-first work as M006-A…M006-E rather than a Monaco/Tauri milestone, with M006-A implemented at `plans/closure/desktop-frontend-ide-foundation/005-status.md` (deviation approved, hosted guard/lint/E2E green, a pre-existing causal-advisor timing flake filed separately);
- arbitrary LSP `workspace/executeCommand` support;
- TUI editor Helix-parity items deliberately deferred by `plans/subsystems/tui-editor-helix-parity-roadmap.md` §3 and therefore **not** covered by that roadmap's completion definition: editor macros (`Q`/`q`), named registers (`"<reg>`), shell filter bindings (`|`, `!`, `$`, `Alt-|`, `Alt-!` — deferred specifically because they would introduce process execution into the editor surface), `.` repeat-insert (distinct from M003's `Alt-.` repeat-motion), and `Ctrl-a`/`Ctrl-x` increment/decrement;
- TUI editor **unimpaired change navigation** (`]g`/`[g` next/previous change, `]G`/`[G` last/first change), deferred from the Helix-parity roadmap's M006 boundary on 2026-10-18 rather than left unbound. The roadmap claimed it worked by "reusing the existing diff surface"; **that premise is false** — `crates/egggit/src/diff.rs` exposes only `ChangedFile { path, kind }` and `FileDiff { path, patch }`, neither carrying line ranges, the only parsed hunk type (`DiffHunk`, `src/tui/components/diff.rs:27`) is produced by a **private** widget method, and `handle_normal_key` receives an `EditorState` with no git handle and no base copy of the document. Landing it needs a new App-level async request, a daemon response type, and parsed-hunk presentation state — a second ownership boundary inside a capability milestone, which `plans/003-planning-process.md` §5 forbids. It therefore needs **fresh planning as its own milestone**, and the deciding question is the data source: daemon-side git-derived ranges, a frontend-side read on open, or a frontend diff against the file on disk. Only the first keeps the editor free of filesystem authority, so it should be the first acceptance criterion of that milestone. The three options are enumerated in `plans/implementation/tui-editor-helix-parity/006-unimpaired-navigation-non-ts-subset.md` §5;
- TUI editor `Ctrl-w` window mode and editor splits, which are a pane-layout workstream rather than a keybinding one — the editor has no splits today and the roadmap records this as an explicit non-goal;
- TUI editor **the open line and the word typed into it are two undo steps** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/019-status.md` §9). A reader may reasonably expect one, and the roadmap's own wave closed with the two-step behaviour measured and registered rather than papered over. Making it one needs the open-line edit and the typing to merge into a single contiguous insertion, and they are not contiguous — the newline `o` opened the line with sits *after* the text the reader types. That is a change to the history model's notion of a run, not to a verb, and it is a maintainer decision;
- TUI editor **the LSP pickers other than the code-action one show the first rows only** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/017-status.md` §9): diagnostics, locations, symbols, highlights, signature help and rename files keep the panel's standing scroll, so a selection past the visible rows is invisible. M017 overrode the standing scroll for the code-action panel alone, because there `Enter` applies a change and must not offer to apply an unread action;
- TUI editor **the code-action read passes no diagnostics context** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/017-status.md` §9): a server that keys quick fixes to the diagnostics it was told about will offer fewer here than it would with the context attached;
- TUI editor **the document's prose may advertise a key that does not exist** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/020-status.md` §9). The doc-drift guard checks the reverse direction only where `docs/tui.md` makes an explicit, structured claim — the "not bound" lists — because a reverse check over every code span reports fifty defects that are not defects, among them the bracketed identifiers `[INS]`, `[NOR]` and `[human_shell]`, the JSON path `[theme].name`, and the negative example `m z` is not a gesture. Widening it needs a way to mark a prose mention as an example, which is a document-shape decision;
- LSP **write** operations for the editor (`=` format, `space r` rename, `space a` code action). `LspReadOperation` is read-only by construction under accepted ADR-0021/ADR-0028; extending it is a protocol decision beyond the Helix-parity workstream;
- binary topology split or separate daemon/TUI packaging without measured deployment need;
- replacing RustPython with a custom Tool Program parser;
- production hosted Tool Program transport;
- seccomp, namespace, container, or remote-execution sandbox expansion;
- persistent search indexing;
- automatic dependency-update bots or continuous binary-size/audit gates;
- OAuth device/provider expansion beyond the bounded MCP at-rest crypto/key migration now closed;
- generalized OAuth/provider credential-store unification absent a token-set abstraction justified by multiple consumers;
- remote workspace/node/distributed execution phases until identity/audit dependencies make them ready;
- release automation or a fixed release cadence;
- upstreaming the validated-destination/SSRF policy into Eggfetch or creating a generic network-policy crate before a second independent consumer justifies that boundary;
- replacing Axum/Tower with Eggserve or adding Eggress/greggd solely for Eggstack component uniformity.

**M004 is closed** (`plans/closure/tui-editor-helix-parity/004-status.md`) — the
basic edit loop now exists: `y` then `p` moves text, which was impossible before
this milestone. Landed `p`/`P` (paste after/before), bare `y`, `r` (replace with a
character), `R` (replace with the clipboard), `~` (flip case), and `` ` `` /
`` Alt-` `` (set lower/upper case) — the last two because the roadmap's M004
deliverable boundary names them and Helix binds them, even though the
implementation plan's §5 did not spell them out. `r` is the second **capture**
grammar after M003's find family: it arms `EditorPrefix::ReplaceChar`, whose leaf
table is empty because the next key is a character to *write*. The dispatch now
has **one** capture branch classified by `EditorPrefix::is_capture`, rather than a
find branch and an `r` branch — the first draft left `is_capture` as a
doc-comment claim with no production caller, and the guard injection caught it.
**One correction against the live Helix keymap**, the same method M005 used: the
implementation plan had `R` as vi's "replace to end of line", entering Insert mode
with a retained tail. Helix binds `R` to `replace_with_yanked` — no argument, no
Insert mode, no retention — and the roadmap's own deliverable boundary already said
so. Plan §8's entire retained-tail design, including the milestone's one flagged
judgement call and its §14 stop condition, described a verb that does not exist
and was dropped (amendment A1 in `004-core-change-verbs.md` §17).
**A user-visible binding moved:** `R` was already reload, as a Normal-only
**guard-block** chord in `editor_buffer_key` — precisely the arrangement
`scripts/check_tui_editor_key_authority.py` rejects as shadowing a Normal-mode
verb while surviving into Insert. Re-adding it fails the guard (proved by
injection). The chord and `BufferKey::RequestReload` are gone and the guard's
`REQUIRED_SUBSTRINGS` dropped from three guard-block chords to two; **reload
survives as `:e`**, so a keystroke moved and no capability was lost. **There is no
editor-local register**, which is not a gap but a structural answer: `y` and
`space y` are the same path writing the OS clipboard, `p` pastes whatever is
there (including from another application), and that is exactly why Helix's `R` is
`replace_with_yanked`. Three `BufferKey` variants carry a `ClipboardVerb` rather
than bytes, because the clipboard is the App layer's. A failed read, an empty
clipboard, and a read-only buffer are three different answers and each is
reported — without the empty-clipboard toast, "you copied nothing" and "`p` did
not work" look identical. **Two defects found beyond plan scope:**
`next_char_boundary(0)` returns `None` on a document that is a *single character*
(`"日"`), which read as "no character under the cursor" and made `r`/`~`/`p` silent
no-ops in a one-character file — `char_end` now resolves that to the document
length, the same `None`-versus-`Some(len)` distinction M005 hit in `long_word_end`;
and paste was placing the cursor one byte *past* the pasted text, correct for
Insert mode's bar and wrong in Normal mode where the cursor sits on a character,
which made `p p` step over the character between the two pastes. `src/tui/components/help_overlay.rs`
was inspected and deliberately **not** edited: it is unreferenced dead code, so its
drift (`Ctrl+R (redo)`, `dd dw`, eleven implemented verbs missing) is real but
unobservable, and the regression test written for it would never have run — the
same ground on which M001 reverted its edits there. `space p`/`space P` are
deliberately **not** added; they
are one row each in `SPACE_LEAVES` and belong to the milestone that owns that
table. M004 discharges no dependency edge: its own hard dependency (M003) was
already discharged, and no milestone waited on it.
